In particular, for such a section
\phi
, there exists a well-defined (up to a right translation by
H
) isomorphism of
G
-bundles
\tau_{\phi}:\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit},G}|_{U}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}U\times G
via which the
H
-reduction
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit},H}|_{U}
of
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit},G}|_{U}
corresponds to that of
U\times G
determined by the graph
\Gamma_{\phi}
.
Next, let us choose a collection
\{(U_{\alpha},\phi_{\alpha})\}_{\alpha\in I}
, where
I
denotes a finite set,
\{U_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in I}
is an étale covering of
X
, and each
\phi_{\alpha}
(
\alpha\in I
) denotes a morphism
U_{\alpha}\rightarrow P_{S}
belonging to
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}(U_{\alpha})
.
For each
\alpha\in I
, we shall denote by
\phi_{\mathcal{E},\alpha}
the
H
-equivariant morphism
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit},H}|_{U_{\alpha}}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{triv}}_{H}
defined as the following composite:

(102)

\displaystyle\phi_{\mathcal{E},\alpha}:\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit},H}|_{U_{\alpha}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit},G}|_{U_{\alpha}}\xrightarrow{\tau_{\phi_{\alpha}}}U_{\alpha}\times G\left(=U_{\alpha}\times_{S}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{triv}}_{H}\right)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{triv}}_{H}.

By the definition of
\tau_{\phi_{\alpha}}
,
the following square diagram is commutative and hence cartesian:

\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit},H}|_{U_{\alpha}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{\mathcal{E},\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{projection}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{triv}}_{H}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}_{S}\times\pi_{G}}
\textstyle{U_{\alpha}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{P_{S}.}

Hence, the étaleness of
\phi_{\alpha}
implies that of
\phi_{\mathcal{E},\alpha}
.
Under the identification
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit},H}|_{U_{\alpha}}=U_{\alpha}\times_{P_{S}}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{triv}}_{H}
given by
the above diagram,
the pull-back

(107)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit},H}|_{U_{\alpha}},\phi_{\mathcal{E},\alpha}^{*}(\omega_{S\times G/S}))

of
(\mathcal{E}_{H}^{\mathrm{triv}},\omega_{S\times G/S})
forms a flat Cartan geometry with model
(G,H)
on
U_{\alpha}/S

(cf. (
97
), (
98
)).
Note that
\phi_{\mathcal{E},\alpha}
does not depend on the choice of
\tau_{\phi_{\alpha}}
, so the
\mathfrak{g}
-valued
1
-form
\phi_{\mathcal{E},\alpha}^{*}(\omega_{S\times_{k}G/S})
depends only on
(U_{\alpha},\phi_{\alpha})
.

Next, denote by
I_{2}
the subset of
I\times I
consisting of pairs
(\alpha,\beta)
with
U_{\alpha\beta}:=U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta}\neq\emptyset
.
Write
\mathcal{E}_{H,\alpha\beta}:=\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit},H}|_{U_{\alpha\beta}}
and
\mathcal{E}_{G,\alpha\beta}:=\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit},G}|_{U_{\alpha\beta}}
.
For each pair
(\alpha,\beta)\in I_{2}
,
there exists
h\in G_{X/S}^{(1)}(U_{\alpha\beta})\left(\subseteq G(U_{\alpha\beta})\right)
satisfying the equality
h(\phi_{\alpha}|_{U_{\alpha\beta}})=\phi_{\beta}|_{U_{\alpha\beta}}
.
We can find an element
g\in H(U_{\alpha\beta})
with

\mathrm{L}_{h}\circ(\tau_{\phi_{\alpha}}|_{\mathcal{E}_{G,\alpha\beta}})=\mathrm{L}_{g}\circ(\tau_{\phi_{\beta}}|_{\mathcal{E}_{G,\alpha\beta}})
.
Then, we have

(108)

\displaystyle\phi^{*}_{\mathcal{E},\alpha}(\omega_{S\times G/S})|_{\mathcal{E}_{H,\alpha\beta}}

\displaystyle=\tau_{\phi_{\alpha}}^{*}(\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}(\omega_{S\times G/S})))|_{\mathcal{E}_{H,\alpha\beta}}

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{L}_{h}\circ(\tau_{\phi_{\alpha}}|_{\mathcal{E}_{G,\alpha\beta}}))^{*}(\mathrm{L}_{h^{-1}}^{*}(\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}(\omega_{S\times G/S})))|_{\mathcal{E}_{H,\alpha\beta}}

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{L}_{h}\circ(\tau_{\phi_{\alpha}}|_{\mathcal{E}_{G,\alpha\beta}}))^{*}(\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}(\omega_{S\times G/S}))|_{\mathcal{E}_{H,\alpha\beta}}

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{L}_{g}\circ(\tau_{\phi_{\beta}}|_{\mathcal{E}_{G,\alpha\beta}}))^{*}(\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}(\omega_{S\times G/S}))|_{\mathcal{E}_{H,\alpha\beta}}

\displaystyle=\tau_{\phi_{\beta}}^{*}(\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}(\omega_{S\times G/S})))|_{\mathcal{E}_{H,\alpha\beta}}

\displaystyle=\phi^{*}_{\mathcal{E},\beta}(\omega_{S\times G/S})|_{\mathcal{E}_{H,\alpha\beta}},

where the third and fifth equalities follow from Proposition
3.1.1
, (i).
It follows that
\phi^{*}_{\mathcal{E},\alpha}(\omega_{S\times G/S})
’s may be glued together to obtain a
\mathfrak{g}
-valued
1
-form
\omega_{\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}}
on
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit},H}/S
.
The resulting pair

(109)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\clubsuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit},H},\omega_{\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}})

forms a flat Cartan geometry with model
(G,H)
on
X/S
.
Since
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\clubsuit}
does not depend on the choice of
\{(U_{\alpha},\phi_{\alpha})\}_{\alpha}
, the assignment
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}\mapsto\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\clubsuit}
determines a well-defined map of sets

(110)

\displaystyle F\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(G,P),X/S}\rightarrow\mathrm{Car}^{\mathrm{flat}}_{(G,H),X/S}.

In the case of
X=P_{S}
,
this map sends the
F
-
(G,P)
-structure
\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\mathrm{triv},\heartsuit}
(cf. (
38
)) to the flat Cartan geometry
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{triv},\clubsuit}
(cf. (
97
)).

The formation of
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\clubsuit}
commutes with pull-back
to étale
X
-schemes, as well as with base-change to
S
-schemes.
That is to say, with the notation in §
2.4
, the following square diagrams are commutative:

\textstyle{F\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(G,H),X/S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
110
) for
X/S
\scriptstyle{(\ref{L0981})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Car}^{\mathrm{flat}}_{(G,H),X/S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{L023})}
\textstyle{F\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(G,H),Y/S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
110
) for
Y/S
\textstyle{\mathrm{Car}^{\mathrm{flat}}_{(G,H),Y/S},}

\textstyle{F\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(G,H),X/S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
110
) for
X/S
\scriptstyle{(\ref{L009})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Car}^{\mathrm{flat}}_{(G,H),X/S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{L024})}
\textstyle{F\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(G,H),X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(
110
) for
X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Car}^{\mathrm{flat}}_{(G,H),X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}.}

3.7.
The
p
-curvature of a
1
-form

Recall that, for a Lie algebra
\mathfrak{a}
over a
k
-algebra
R
,
a
p
-power operation
on
\mathfrak{a}
is
a map
(-)^{[p]}:\mathfrak{a}\rightarrow\mathfrak{a}
satisfying the following conditions:

•

(\mathrm{ad}(v))^{p}=\mathrm{ad}(v^{[p]})
for all
v\in\mathfrak{a}
;

•

(a\cdot v)^{[p]}=a^{p}\cdot v^{[p]}
for all
a\in R
,
v\in\mathfrak{a}
;

•

(v+u)^{[p]}=v^{[p]}+u^{[p]}+\sum_{i=1}^{p-1}\frac{s_{i}(v,u)}{i}
for all
v,u\in\mathfrak{a}
, where
s_{i}(v,u)
denotes the coefficient of
t^{i-1}
in the expression
\mathrm{ad}(vt+u)^{p-1}(v)\in\mathfrak{a}[t]\left(:=\mathfrak{a}\otimes_{R}R[t]\right)
.

A Lie algebra over
R
together with a
p
-power operation is called a

restricted Lie algebra
(or a
p
-Lie algebra
) over
R
.
For example,
the space of
R
-linear endomorphisms
\mathrm{End}_{R}(V)
of
R
-module
V

gives a typical example of a restricted Lie algebra over
R
by putting

[v,u]:=vu-uv
as a bracket operation and putting
v^{[p]}:=v^{p}
as a
p
-power operation.

In the natural fashion, we can define the notion of
a
restricted
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-Lie algebra
on
X
(i.e., a sheaf of restricted
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-Lie algebra in the sense of
[
31
]
, § 4.1).
Just as in the case of
\mathrm{End}_{R}(V)
above,
the sheaf
\mathcal{E}nd_{f^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{S})}(\mathcal{V})
defined for each
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{V}
has a natural structure of restricted
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-Lie algebra on
X
with

p
-power operation
v^{[p]}:=v^{p}
(= the
p
-th iteration of
v
) for any local section
v\in\mathcal{E}nd_{f^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{S})}(\mathcal{V})
.
Recall that the tangent bundle
\mathcal{T}_{X/S}
may be identified with the subsheaf of

\mathcal{E}nd_{f^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{S})}(\mathcal{O}_{X})
consisting of
locally defined derivations on
\mathcal{O}_{X}
relative to
S
.
The
p
-power operation on
\mathcal{E}nd_{f^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{S})}(\mathcal{O}_{X})
is restricted to

\mathcal{T}_{X/S}
.
In particular,
we obtain
the
p
-power operation

(-)^{[p]}:\mathfrak{g}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}

on the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
arising from that on
\mathcal{T}_{G}
via the identification of
\mathfrak{g}=\Gamma(G,\mathcal{T}_{G})^{G}
;
it induces naturally a
p
-power operation on
\mathcal{O}_{X}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
, by which
\mathcal{O}_{X}\otimes_{k}\mathfrak{g}
forms a restricted
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-Lie algebra.

Now, let

\omega
be a flat
\mathfrak{g}
-valued
1
-form on
X/S
.
For each local section
\partial\in\mathcal{T}_{X/S}
,
we shall write

(119)

\displaystyle{{}^{p}}\psi_{\omega,\partial}:=\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial)^{[p]}-\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial^{[p]})+\sum_{i=1}^{p-1}\frac{s_{i}(\partial,\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial))}{i}\in\mathcal{O}_{X}\otimes\mathfrak{g}.

Lemma 3.7.1
.

Let

\nabla_{\omega}

be the flat
S
-connection on the trivial
G
-bundle
X\times G

corresponding to
\omega
(cf. (
84
)).
Then, for each local section
\partial\in\mathcal{T}_{X/S}
, the following equality holds:

(120)

\displaystyle{{}^{p}}\psi_{\omega,\partial}=\langle F^{-1}_{X}(\partial),{{}^{p}}\psi_{\nabla_{\omega}}\rangle

(cf. (
420
) for the definition of
{{}^{p}}\psi_{\nabla_{\omega}}
), where
\langle-,-\rangle
denotes the natural pairing
F_{X}^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{X/S})\times F^{*}_{X}(\Omega_{X/S})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathfrak{g}_{X\times G}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{X\times G}\left(=\mathcal{O}_{X}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\right)
.

Proof.

Under the identification
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{X\times G/S}=\mathcal{T}_{X/S}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{X}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
given by
\tau
(cf. (
82
)),
we have

(121)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \langle F^{-1}_{X}(\partial),{{}^{p}}\psi_{\nabla_{\omega}}\rangle

\displaystyle=(\partial,\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial))^{[p]}-(\partial^{[p]},\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial^{[p]}))

\displaystyle=((\partial,0)+(0,\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial)))^{[p]}-(\partial^{[p]},\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial^{[p]}))

\displaystyle=(\partial^{[p]},0)+(0,\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial)^{[p]})+\left(0,\sum_{i=1}^{p-1}\frac{s_{i}((\partial,0),(0,\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial)))}{i}\right)-(\partial^{[p]},\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial^{[p]}))

\displaystyle=\left(0,\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial)^{[p]}-\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial^{[p]})+\sum_{i=1}^{p-1}\frac{s_{i}(\partial,\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial))}{i}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(0,{{}^{p}}\psi_{\omega,\partial}\right).

This completes the proof of the assertion.
∎

By the above lemma and a property of
{{}^{p}}\psi_{\nabla_{\omega}}
,
we obtain the section

(122)

\displaystyle{{}^{p}}\psi_{\omega}\in\Gamma(X,F^{*}_{X}(\Omega_{X/S})\otimes\mathfrak{g})

determined by
\langle F^{-1}_{X}(\partial),{{}^{p}}\psi_{\omega}\rangle={{}^{p}}\psi_{\omega,\partial}
for any local section
\partial\in\mathcal{T}_{X/S}
.

Definition 3.7.2
.

We shall refer to
{{}^{p}}\psi_{\omega}
as the
p
-curvature
of
\omega
.
Also, we shall say that
\omega
is
p
-flat
if
{{}^{p}}\psi_{\omega}=0
.
(Hence, the above lemma implies that
\omega
is
p
-flat if and only if
\nabla_{\omega}
is
p
-flat in the sense of §
7.1
).

Example 3.7.3
.

Suppose that
G
is abelian, which
implies that the Lie bracket operation on
\mathfrak{g}
is trivial.
Let us write
\omega=\sum_{i=1}^{l}\omega_{i}\otimes e_{i}
(
l\geq 1
)
for
\omega_{i}\in\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X/S})
and
e_{i}\in\mathfrak{g}
.
Then,
\omega
is flat if and only if, for every
i=1,\cdots,l
, the
1
-form
\omega_{i}
is flat, or equivalently, closed.
Moreover, if
\mathfrak{g}=k
(i.e.,
\mathrm{dim}(G)=1
) and
\omega
is flat, then
it follows from
[
28
, § 7, Proposition 7.1.2]
that
the
p
-curvature
{{}^{p}}\psi_{\omega}
of
\omega
satisfies

(123)

\displaystyle\langle F_{X}^{-1}(\partial),{{}^{p}}\psi_{\omega}\rangle\left(=\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial)^{[p]}-\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial^{[p]})+\partial^{p-1}(\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial))\right)=\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial)^{p}-\langle F^{*}_{S}(\partial),C(\omega)\rangle

for any
\partial\in\mathcal{T}_{X/S}
, where
C
denotes the usual Cartier operator
F_{X/S*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{0}^{(2)}))\rightarrow\Omega_{X^{(1)}/S}
(cf. (
408
) for the definition of
(-)^{(2)}
) and
F^{*}_{S}(\partial)
denotes the local section of
\mathcal{T}_{X^{(1)}/S}
corresponding to
\partial
via the isomorphism
(\mathrm{id}_{X}\times F_{S})^{*}(\Omega_{X/S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\Omega_{X^{(1)}/S}
.

3.8.
p
-flatness of the Maurer-Cartan form

In the differential geometric setting,
the Maurer-Cartan equation (i.e., the flatness condition) associated with a
\mathfrak{g}
-valued
1
-form gives the local obstruction to its integrability
(cf.
[
47
, Chap. 3, § 6, Theorem 6.1]
).
By considering the
p
-flatness condition in addition,
we can prove the positive characteristic version of this fact, as described in Proposition
3.8.2
below.
To begin with, let us observe the following proposition.

Proposition 3.8.1
.

The Maurer-Cartan form
\omega_{G}
is
p
-flat.

Proof.

Let us take an element
v
of
\mathfrak{g}
.
The isomorphism
\omega_{G}^{\triangleright}:\mathcal{T}_{G}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{G}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
(cf. (
56
)) preserves the
p
-power operations, so we have

\omega_{G}^{\triangleright}(v^{\dagger[p]})=\omega_{G}^{\triangleright}(v^{\dagger})^{[p]}=1\otimes v^{[p]}
.
Also, note that
\partial(\omega_{G}^{\triangleright}(v^{\dagger}))\left(=\partial(1\otimes v)=\partial(1)\otimes v\right)=0
for any
\partial\in\mathcal{T}_{G}
,
in particular, the equality
s_{i}(v^{\dagger},\omega^{\triangleright}_{G}(v^{\dagger}))=0
holds for every
i=1,\cdots,p-1
.
Hence,

(124)

\displaystyle{{}^{p}}\psi_{\omega_{G},v^{\dagger}}=\omega^{\triangleright}_{G}(v^{\dagger})^{[p]}-\omega^{\triangleright}_{G}(v^{\dagger[p]})+\sum_{i=1}^{p-1}\frac{s_{i}(v^{\dagger},\omega_{G}^{\triangleright}(v^{\dagger}))}{i}=1\otimes v^{[p]}-1\otimes v^{[p]}+0=0.

Since
F^{*}_{G}(\mathcal{T}_{G})
is generated by the sections
F_{G}^{-1}(v^{\dagger})
for various
v\in\mathfrak{g}
, (
124
) implies
{{}^{p}}\psi_{\omega_{G}}=0
.
This completes the proof of the assertion.
∎

Proposition 3.8.2
.

Suppose that
G
is affine.

(i)

Let
\omega
be a
\mathfrak{g}
-valued
1
-form on
X/S
.
Then,
\omega
is
p
-flat if and only if each geometric point
x
of
X
admits an étale neighborhood
U\rightarrow X
together with an
S
-morphism
h:U\rightarrow G
such that
h^{*}(\omega_{G})=\omega
(or equivalently, its differential
dh:\mathcal{T}_{U/S}\rightarrow h^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{G})
composed with
h^{*}(\omega_{G}^{\triangleright}):h^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{G})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{U}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
coincides with
\omega^{\triangleright}
).

(ii)

Let
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
be
S
-morphisms
X\rightarrow G
with
h_{1}^{*}(\omega_{G})=h_{2}^{*}(\omega_{G})
.
Then, there exists a unique global section
g
of
G_{X/S}^{(1)}
with
h_{1}=(g\circ F_{X/S})\cdot h_{2}
, where the right-hand side of this equality denotes the morphism
X\rightarrow G
given by
a\mapsto(g\circ F_{X/S})(a)\cdot h_{2}(a)
.

Proof.

First, let us consider assertion (i).
The “if” part of the required equivalence follows from Proposition
3.8.1
.
To prove the “only if” part, let us suppose that
\omega
is
p
-flat.
Then, Lemma
3.7.1
implies
{{}^{p}}\psi_{\nabla_{\omega}}=0
.
By Proposition
7.7.3
, (i), together with the affineness of
G
, there exist a
G
-bundle
\mathcal{G}
on
X^{(1)}
and an isomorphism of flat
G
-bundles
\alpha:(X\times G,\nabla_{\omega})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(F_{X/S}^{*}(\mathcal{G}),\nabla^{\mathrm{can}}_{\mathcal{G}})
(cf. (
484
) for the definition of
\nabla_{\mathcal{G}}^{\mathrm{can}}
).
For each geometric point
x
of
X
, we can find an étale neighborhood
U\rightarrow X
of
x
such that the
G
-bundle
\mathcal{G}|_{U^{(1)}}
admits a trivialization
\beta:\mathcal{G}|_{U^{(1)}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}U^{(1)}\times G
.
The pull-back

(125)

\displaystyle\beta^{F}:F^{*}_{U/S}(\mathcal{G}|_{U^{(1)}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\left(F^{*}_{U/S}(U^{(1)}\times G)=\right)U\times G

of this trivialization by
F_{U/S}

defines an isomorphism
(F^{*}_{U/S}(\mathcal{G}|_{U^{(1)}}),\nabla^{\mathrm{can}}_{\mathcal{G}}|_{U})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(U\times G,\nabla_{0})
.
The composite automorphism
\beta^{F}\circ\alpha
of the trivial
G
-bundle
U\times G
may be expressed as the left-translation
\mathrm{L}_{h}
by some
h\in G(U)
.
In particular, the gauge transformation
\mathrm{L}_{h}^{*}(\nabla_{0})
of the trivial connection
\nabla_{0}
on
U\times G
coincides with
\nabla_{\omega}
.
On the other hand, it follows from
[
54
, Chap. 1, Proposition 1.22]
that

\nabla_{\omega_{G}}
coincides with
\mathrm{L}_{\mathrm{id}_{G}}^{*}(\nabla_{0})
, i.e., the gauge transformation of
\nabla_{0}
using the left-translation
\mathrm{L}_{\mathrm{id}_{G}}
by
\mathrm{id}_{G}:G\rightarrow G
.
Since the diagram

\textstyle{U\times G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h\times\mathrm{id}_{G}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{L}_{h}:(a,b)\mapsto(a,h(a)\cdot b)}
\textstyle{G\times G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{L}_{\mathrm{id}_{G}}:(a,b)\mapsto(a,a\cdot b)}
\textstyle{U\times G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h\times\mathrm{id}_{G}}
\textstyle{G\times G}

is commutative, the following sequence of equalities holds:

(130)

\displaystyle h^{*}(\nabla_{\omega_{G}})=h^{*}(\mathrm{L}_{\mathrm{id}_{G}}^{*}(\nabla_{0}))=\mathrm{L}_{h}^{*}(h^{*}(\nabla_{0}))=\mathrm{L}_{h}^{*}(\nabla_{0})=\nabla_{\omega}

(cf. (
414
) for the definition of pull-back of a connection
h^{*}(-)
).
This implies
h^{*}(\omega_{G})=\omega
, and hence, completes the proof of the “only if” part.

Next, we shall consider assertion (ii).
Let us write
h:=h_{1}\cdot h_{2}^{-1}
, i.e., the morphism
X\rightarrow G
given by
a\mapsto h_{1}(a)\cdot h_{2}^{-1}(a)
for each
a\in X
.
Then,
h
decomposes as

(131)

\displaystyle X\xrightarrow{(h_{1},h_{2})}G\times G\xrightarrow{\mathrm{id}\times\iota}G\times G\xrightarrow{\mu}G

(cf. Lemma
3.8.3
for the definitions of
\mu
and
\iota
).
By the following Lemma
3.8.3
, (i) and (ii), we have

(132)

\displaystyle h^{*}(\omega_{G})=h_{2}^{*}(\mathrm{Ad}_{G})(h_{1}^{*}(\omega_{G})-h_{2}^{*}(\omega_{G}))=0

(cf.
[
47
, Chap. 3, § 4, Corollary 4.11]
).
Since
\omega_{G}^{\triangleright}:\mathcal{T}_{G}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{G}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
is an isomorphism, (
132
) shows that the differential
dh:\mathcal{T}_{X/S}\rightarrow h^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{G})
vanishes identically.
Hence, it follows from Lemma
3.8.4
below that

h
lies in
G_{X/S}^{(1)}\left(\subseteq G_{X}\right)
.
This completes the proof of assertion (ii).
∎

The following two lemmas were used in the proof of the above proposition.

Lemma 3.8.3
.

We shall abuse notation by writing
\mathrm{Ad}_{G}
for the
\mathcal{O}_{G}
-linear automorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{G}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
given by the adjoint representation
\mathrm{Ad}_{G}:G\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}(\mathfrak{g})
.

(i)

Let
\mu:G\times G\rightarrow G
denote the multiplication of
G
.
Then,
the following equality holds:

(133)

\displaystyle\mu^{*}(\omega_{G})=\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}(\mathrm{Ad}_{G}^{-1})(\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{*}(\omega_{G}))+\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}(\omega_{G}),

where
\mathrm{pr}_{i}
(
i=1,2
) denotes the
i
-th projection
G\times G\rightarrow G
.

(ii)

Let
\iota:G\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}G
denote the inverse of
G
.
Then, the following equality holds

(134)

\displaystyle\iota^{*}(\omega_{G})=-\mathrm{Ad}_{G}(\omega_{G}).

Proof.

The proofs are entirely similar to
[
47
, Chap. 3, S 4, Proposition 4.10]
.
∎

Lemma 3.8.4
.

Let
Y
be a scheme over
S
and
g
an
S
-morphism
X\rightarrow Y
.
Then, the morphism
dg^{\vee}:g^{*}(\Omega_{Y/S})\rightarrow\Omega_{X/S}

induced by
g
(i.e., the dual of the differential of
g
)
is the zero map if and only if
g
factors through
F_{X/S}:X\rightarrow X^{(1)}
.

Proof.

The “if” part of the required equivalence is clear since
dF_{X/S}=0
.
Next, let us consider the “only if” part.
Observe that the following diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{g^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{Y})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{*}(d)}
\scriptstyle{g^{\sharp}}
\textstyle{g^{*}(\Omega_{Y/S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{dg^{\vee}}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F_{X/S}^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{X^{(1)}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{inclusion}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{\Omega_{X/S},}

where the lower horizontal sequence is exact because of
the smoothness of
X/S
.
If
dg^{\vee}=0
, then the morphism
g^{\sharp}:g^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{Y})\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}
satisfies
d\circ g^{\sharp}\left(=dg^{\vee}\circ g^{*}(d)\right)=0
, so it factors through the inclusion
F_{X/S}^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{X^{(1)}})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}
.
The resulting morphism
g^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{Y})\rightarrow F_{X/S}^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{X^{(1)}})
determines a factorization of
g
as
X\xrightarrow{F_{X/S}}X^{(1)}\rightarrow Y
.
This completes the proof of this lemma.
∎

3.9.
p
-flat Cartan geometries

Let us introduce
p
-flat Cartan geometries and give the bijective correspondence with
F
-
(G,P)
-structures (cf. Theorem
3.9.3
); the construction of this correspondence is a part of Theorem
A
.

Definition 3.9.1
.

Let
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega)
be a
(G,H)
-Cartan geometry on
X/S
.
We shall say that
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}
is
p
-flat
if

\omega
is flat and
p
-flat.

Example 3.9.2
.

Recall the flat Cartan geometry
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{triv},\clubsuit}
on
P_{S}/S

defined in Example
3.4.3
.
Then, the result of Proposition
3.8.1
says that it is
p
-flat.

Denote by

(139)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Car}^{p\text{-}\mathrm{flat}}_{(G,H),X/S}

the subset of

\mathrm{Car}^{\mathrm{flat}}_{(G,H),X/S}
consisting of

p
-flat Cartan geometries.
Here, we shall observe the following assertion, which will be used in the proof of Theorem
3.9.3
described next.

Theorem 3.9.3
.

Suppose that
G
is affine.
Then, the image of (
110
) is contained in
\mathrm{Car}_{(G,H),X/S}^{p\text{-}\mathrm{flat}}
.
Moreover, the resulting map

(140)

\displaystyle\zeta^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\clubsuit}:F\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(G,P),X/S}\rightarrow\mathrm{Car}^{p\text{-}\mathrm{flat}}_{(G,H),X/S}

is bijective.
In particular, a Cartan geometry with model
(G,H)
on
X/S
comes from an
F
-
(G,P)
-structure if and only if it is
p
-flat.

Proof.

The first assertion follows from Corollary
7.8.2
and the construction of
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\clubsuit}
for a
F
-
(G,P)
-structure
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
.
Hence, the remaining portion is to prove the bijectivity of
\zeta^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\clubsuit}
.
To this end, we shall construct the inverse to that map.
Let
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega)
be a
p
-flat Cartan geometry with model
(G,H)
on
X/S
.
By Lemma
3.7.1
and the
p
-flatness of
\omega
, the
S
-connection
\nabla_{\omega}
on the trivial
G
-bundle
\mathcal{E}_{H}\times G
has vanishing
p
-curvature.
It follows that
the
p
-flat
G
-bundle
(\mathcal{E}_{H}\times G,\nabla_{\omega})
corresponds to a
G
-bundle
\mathcal{F}
on
\mathcal{E}_{H}^{(1)}\left(:=\mathcal{E}_{H}\times_{F_{S},S}S\right)
via
(
483
).
That is to say,
there exists
an isomorphism of
G
-bundles
\widetilde{\alpha}:\mathcal{E}_{H}\times G\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}F^{*}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}(\mathcal{F})
compatible with the respective
S
-connections, i.e.,
\nabla_{\omega}
and
\nabla^{\mathrm{can}}_{\mathcal{F}}
(cf. (
484
)).
For each
h\in H(k)
,
we denote by
\widetilde{\mathrm{R}}_{h}^{F}
the automorphism of
\mathcal{E}_{H}\times G
defined as
\widetilde{\mathrm{R}}_{h}^{F}:=\mathrm{R}_{h}\times\mathrm{L}_{h^{-1}}
.
It is
G
-equivariant with respect to the right
G
-action arising from the second factor
G
, and the following square diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{H}\times G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathrm{R}}_{h}^{F}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{H}\times G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{H}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{R}_{h}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{H}.}

When we regard, via
\widetilde{\mathrm{R}}_{h}^{F}
, the base-change
\mathrm{R}_{h}^{*}(\nabla_{\omega})
of
\nabla_{\omega}
as
an

-connection on
\mathcal{E}_{H}\times_{k}G
(i.e., the upper left corner of (

)),
the equality

holds because of the commutativity of (

‣
(i)
) (cf.

).
Hence, it follows from Corollary

again that

\widetilde{\mathrm{R}}_{h}^{F}

induces a

-equivariant automorphism
\widetilde{\mathrm{R}}_{h}
of
\mathcal{F}
whose

coincides with

under the identification
\mathcal{E}_{H}\times G=F^{*}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}(\mathcal{F})
given by
\widetilde{\alpha}
and which makes the following square diagram commute:

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathrm{R}}_{h}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{projection}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{projection}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{H}^{(1)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\mathrm{R}_{h}^{(1)}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{H}^{(1)},}

where
\mathrm{R}_{h}^{(1)}
denotes the base-change of
\mathrm{R}_{h}
by
F_{S}
.
The quotient
\mathcal{G}:=\mathcal{F}/H
of
\mathcal{F}
by the resulting

-action

forms a

-bundle on

, and
\widetilde{\alpha}
induces an isomorphism
\alpha:\left((\mathcal{E}_{H}\times G)/H=\right)\mathcal{E}_{G}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}F^{*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})
.

Now, let us take an étale
X
-scheme

and a trivialization
\beta:\mathcal{G}|_{U^{(1)}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}U^{(1)}\times G
of the
G
-bundle
\mathcal{G}
over
U^{(1)}
.
The pull-back

(149)

\displaystyle\beta^{F}:F^{*}_{U/S}(\mathcal{G}|_{U})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\left(F^{*}_{U/S}(U^{(1)}\times G)=\right)U\times G

of this trivialization by
F_{U/S}
gives the
H
-equivariant composite

(150)

\displaystyle h:\mathcal{E}_{H}|_{U}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{inclusion}}\mathcal{E}_{G}|_{U}\xrightarrow{\alpha|_{U}}F^{*}_{U/S}(\mathcal{G})|_{U}\xrightarrow{\beta^{F}}U\times G\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}G.

In what follows, we shall prove the claim that
the morphism
h_{S}:\mathcal{E}_{H}|_{U}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{H}^{\mathrm{triv}}\left(:=S\times G\right)
induced naturally by
h
is étale.
Let us consider the commutative square diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{H}^{(1)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X^{(1)}}

consisting of natural projections.
This diagram is cartesian because the two horizontal (resp., vertical) arrows are the structure morphisms of
G
-bundles (resp.,
H
-bundles).
In particular, we obtain an isomorphism
\mathcal{F}|_{U^{(1)}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{E}_{H}^{(1)}|_{U^{(1)}}\times_{U^{(1)}}\mathcal{G}|_{U^{(1)}}
.
The base-change of
\beta
by the projection
\mathcal{E}_{H}^{(1)}|_{U^{(1)}}\rightarrow U^{(1)}
defines a
G
-equivariant isomorphism
\widetilde{\beta}:\mathcal{F}|_{U^{(1)}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{E}^{(1)}_{H}|_{U^{(1)}}\times G
, making the following diagram commute:

\textstyle{\mathcal{F}|_{U^{(1)}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\beta}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{projection}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{H}^{(1)}|_{U^{(1)}}\times G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{projection}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}|_{U^{(1)}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{U^{(1)}\times G.}

By pulling-back this diagram by relative Frobenius morphisms,
we obtain a commutative square diagram

\textstyle{F^{*}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}(\mathcal{F})|_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\beta}^{F}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{projection}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{H}|_{U}\times G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{projection}}
\textstyle{F^{*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})|_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{F}}
\textstyle{U\times G.}

This square extends to
the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{H}|_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{v\mapsto(v,e)}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{H}|_{U}\times G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\alpha}|_{U}}
\textstyle{F^{*}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}(\mathcal{F})|_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\beta}^{F}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{projection}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{H}|_{U}\times G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{projection}}
\textstyle{G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{H}|_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{inclusion}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{G}|_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\alpha|_{U}}
\textstyle{F^{*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})|_{U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{F}}
\textstyle{U\times G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}
\textstyle{G,}

where
the second vertical arrow from the left denotes the natural projection
\mathcal{E}_{H}|_{U}\times G\twoheadrightarrow\left((\mathcal{E}_{H}|_{U}\times G)/H=\right)\mathcal{E}_{G}|_{U}
.
The commutativity of this diagram implies that the composite of all the arrows in the upper horizontal sequence coincides with
h
.
In particular,
h
is a morphism resulting from the “only if” part of the equivalence asserted in Proposition
3.8.2
, (i) (with
X
replaced by
\mathcal{E}_{H}
), and hence, satisfies
h^{*}(\omega_{G})=\omega
.
That is to say, the differential
dh:\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}|_{U}/S}\rightarrow h_{S}^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}^{\mathrm{triv}}/S})

of
h
makes the diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}|_{U}/S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{dh}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\omega^{\triangleright}|_{U}}
\textstyle{h_{S}^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}^{\mathrm{triv}}/S})\left(=h^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{G})\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{h^{*}(\omega^{\triangleright}_{G})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}|_{U}}\otimes\mathfrak{g}}

commutes, so

dh
is an isomorphism.
This implies that, since both
\mathcal{E}_{H}|_{U}
and
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{triv}}_{H}
are smooth over
S
,
the morphism
h_{S}:\mathcal{E}_{H}|_{U}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{H}^{\mathrm{triv}}
turns out to be étale.
This completes the proof of the claim.

Moreover,
the étale morphism
h_{S}
induces, via taking the quotients by the
H
-actions, an étale morphism
\phi:U\rightarrow P_{S}
over
S
, i.e., a section of
\mathcal{P}^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{P,X/S}
over
U
.
The collection of
\phi
’s defined for various pairs
(U,\beta)
forms a subsheaf
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit}
of
\mathcal{P}_{P,X/S}^{\text{\'{e}t}}
, which specifies an
F
-
(G,P)
-structure on
X/S
.
One may verify that the resulting assignment
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}\mapsto\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit}
defines the inverse to
\zeta^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\clubsuit}
.
This completes the proof of this theorem.
∎

3.10.
F^{N}
-Cartan geometries for
N<\infty

We shall extend both the notion of a Cartan geometry and the bijection
\zeta^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\clubsuit}
to finite level.

In the rest of this section, we suppose that
G
is affine.
Let
N
be a positive integer and

\mathcal{G}
a
G
-bundle on
X^{(N)}
.
In what follows, we shall construct a canonical
\mathfrak{g}
-valued
1
-form on the pull-back
F^{(N)*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})
.
Denote by
\pi
the structure morphism of
F^{(N)*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})
over
X
and
by
F_{\mathcal{G}/X/S}^{(N)}:F^{(N)*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})\rightarrow\mathcal{G}
the natural projection.
The differential

(171)

\displaystyle dF_{\mathcal{G}/X/S}^{(N)}:\mathcal{T}_{F^{(N)*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})/S}\rightarrow F_{\mathcal{G}/X/S}^{(N)*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{G}/S})

of
F_{\mathcal{G}/X/S}^{(N)}

fits into the following morphism of short exact sequences:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{F^{(N)*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})/X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{F^{(N)*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})/S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{dF_{\mathcal{G}/X/S}^{(N)}}
\scriptstyle{dF_{\mathcal{G}/X/S}^{(N)}}
\textstyle{\pi^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{X/S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F_{\mathcal{G}/X/S}^{(N)*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{G}/X^{(N)}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F_{\mathcal{G}/X/S}^{(N)*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{G}/S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi^{*}(F^{(N)*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{T}_{X^{(N)}/S}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

where

•

the upper horizontal sequence is obtained by differentiating
\pi
, and
the lower horizontal sequence is obtained by differentiating
the natural projection
\mathcal{G}\rightarrow X^{(N)}
and successively pulling-back by
F_{\mathcal{G}/X/S}^{(N)}
;

•

the right-hand vertical arrow denotes the pull-back by
\pi
of the differential of
F_{X/S}^{(N)}
, and the left-hand vertical arrow denotes the isomorphism arising from the base-change by
F_{X/S}^{(N)}
.

Since the right-hand vertical arrow becomes the zero map because of the definition of
F_{X/S}^{(N)}
,
the morphism
dF_{\mathcal{G}/X/S}^{(N)}
factors through
the inclusion
F^{(N)*}_{\mathcal{G}/X/S}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{G}/X^{(N)}})\hookrightarrow F^{(N)*}_{\mathcal{G}/X/S}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{G}/S})
.
The resulting morphism
\mathcal{T}_{F^{(N)*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})/S}\rightarrow F^{(N)*}_{\mathcal{G}/X/S}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{G}/X})

induces the
\mathcal{O}_{F^{(N)*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})}
-linear composite

(176)

\displaystyle\omega^{\mathrm{can}(N),\triangleright}_{\mathcal{G}}:\mathcal{T}_{F^{(N)*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})/S}\rightarrow F^{(N)*}_{\mathcal{G}/X/S}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{G}/X})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{T}_{F^{(N)*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})/X}\xrightarrow{\omega^{\triangleright}_{F^{(N)*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})/X}}\mathcal{O}_{F^{(N)*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})}\otimes\mathfrak{g},

where the second arrow denotes the inverse of the left-hand vertical arrow in (
3.10
).
Hence,
we obtain the
\mathfrak{g}
-valued
1
-form

(177)

\displaystyle\omega^{\mathrm{can}(N)}_{\mathcal{G}}\in\Gamma(F^{(N)*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{G}),\Omega_{F^{(N)*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})/S}\otimes\mathfrak{g})

on
F^{(N)*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})/S
corresponding to
\omega^{\mathrm{can}(N),\triangleright}_{\mathcal{G}}
.
If
N^{\prime}
is a positive integer with
N^{\prime}\leq N
, then the equality
\omega_{\mathcal{G}}^{\mathrm{can}(N)}=\omega_{\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{can}(N^{\prime})}
holds, where
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}:=F^{(N-N^{\prime})*}_{X^{(N^{\prime})}/S}(\mathcal{G})
, under the natural identification
F^{(N)*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})=F^{(N^{\prime})*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
.

Lemma 3.10.1
.

The
\mathfrak{g}
-valued
1
-form
\omega^{\mathrm{can}(N)}_{\mathcal{G}}
satisfies

\psi_{\omega^{\mathrm{can}(N)}_{\mathcal{G}}}=0
and
{{}^{p}}\psi_{\omega^{\mathrm{can}(N)}_{\mathcal{G}}}=0
.

Proof.

The problem is of local nature,
so it suffices to consider the case where
\mathcal{G}=X^{(N)}\times G
.
In this case, one may verify that
the
S
-connection
\nabla_{\omega^{\mathrm{can}(N)}_{\mathcal{G}}}
on
\mathcal{O}_{X\times G}
(cf. (
84
)) coincides with the trivial connection.
Hence,
since both the curvature and
p
-curvature of
\nabla_{\omega^{\mathrm{can}(N)}_{\mathcal{G}}}
vanish identically,
(
85
) implies
\psi_{\omega^{\mathrm{can}(N)}_{\mathcal{G}}}=0
and Lemma
3.7.1
implies
{{}^{p}}\psi_{\omega^{\mathrm{can}}_{\mathcal{G}}}=0
.
∎

Definition 3.10.2
.

(i)

An
F^{N}
-Cartan geometry with model
(G,H)
on
X/S
is a quadruple

(178)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}_{N}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega,\mathcal{G},\kappa)

consisting of
a Cartan geometry
(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega)
with model
(G,H)
on
X/S
, a
G
-bundle
\mathcal{G}
on
X^{(N)}
, and an isomorphism of
G
-bundles
\kappa:\mathcal{E}_{G}\left(:=\mathcal{E}_{H}\times^{H}G\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}F_{X/S}^{(N)*}(\mathcal{G})
with
\kappa^{*}(\omega^{\mathrm{can}(N)}_{\mathcal{G}})|_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}=\omega
.

(ii)

Let
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}_{N}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega,\mathcal{G},\kappa)
and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime\clubsuit}_{N}:=(\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{H},\omega^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\kappa^{\prime})
be
F^{N}
-Cartan geometries with model
(G,H)
on
X/S
.
An
isomorphism of
F^{N}
-Cartan geometries with model
(G,H)
from
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}_{N}

to
\mathcal{E}^{\prime\clubsuit}_{N}
is
a pair

(179)

\displaystyle(\eta_{\mathcal{E}},\eta_{\mathcal{G}})

consisting of an isomorphism
\eta_{\mathcal{E}}:(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{H},\omega^{\prime})
of Cartan geometries with model
(G,H)

and
an isomorphism of
G
-bundles
\eta_{\mathcal{G}}:\mathcal{G}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
satisfying

F_{X/S}^{(N)*}(\eta_{\mathcal{G}})\circ\kappa=\kappa^{\prime}\circ(\eta_{\mathcal{E}}\times^{H}G)
, where
\eta_{\mathcal{E}}\times^{H}G
denotes the isomorphism
\mathcal{E}_{G}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{G}
induced by
\eta_{\mathcal{E}}
.

Just as in the case of a Cartan geometry (cf. §
3.5
), we can define the pull-back and the base-change of an
F^{N}
-Cartan geometry (but leave the details to the reader).

We shall denote by

(180)

\displaystyle F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Car}_{(G,H),X/S}

the set of isomorphism classes of
F^{N}
-Cartan geometries with model
(G,H)
on
X/S
.

Let
N
and
N^{\prime}
be positive integers with
N\leq N^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}_{N^{\prime}}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega,\mathcal{G},\kappa)
an
F^{N^{\prime}}
-Cartan geometry with model
(G,H)
on
X/S
.
Then, under the canonical identification
F^{(N^{\prime})*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})=F^{(N)*}_{X/S}(F^{(N^{\prime}-N)*}_{X^{(N)}/S}(\mathcal{G}))
,
the quadruple

(181)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}_{N^{\prime}}|^{\langle N\rangle}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega,F^{(N^{\prime}-N)*}_{X^{(N)}/S}(\mathcal{G}),\kappa)

specifies an
F^{(N)}
-Cartan geometry with model
(G,H)
on
X/S
.
We refer to
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}_{N^{\prime}}|^{\langle N\rangle}
as the
N
-th truncation
of
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}_{N^{\prime}}
.
The resulting assignments
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}_{N^{\prime}}\mapsto\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}_{N^{\prime}}|^{\langle N\rangle}

for various pairs
(N,N^{\prime})
gives a projective system

(182)

\displaystyle\cdots\rightarrow F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Car}_{(G,H),X/S}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow F^{2}\text{-}\mathrm{Car}_{(G,H),X/S}\rightarrow F^{1}\text{-}\mathrm{Car}_{(G,H),X/S}.

Example 3.10.3

(Standard example)
.

We shall consider the standard example of an
F^{N}
-
(G,H)
-Cartan geometry as an extension of (
97
).
The identity morphism
\mathrm{id}_{P\times G}
of
P\times G
may be considered as an isomorphism
P\times G\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}F^{(N)*}_{P_{S}/S}(P^{(N)}_{S}\times G)
.
Then,
the quadruple

(183)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\mathrm{triv},\clubsuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H}^{\mathrm{triv}},\omega_{S\times G/S},P^{(N)}_{S}\times G,\mathrm{id}_{P\times G})

forms an
F^{N}
-Cartan geometry with model
(G,H)
on
P_{S}/S
.
To verify this, we shall prove the equality

(184)

\displaystyle\omega_{P^{(N)}_{S}\times G}^{\mathrm{can}(N)}|_{\mathcal{E}_{H}^{\mathrm{triv}}}=\omega_{S\times G/S}.

Under the identification
\mathcal{T}_{P_{S}\times G/S}=\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{P_{S}/S})\oplus\mathcal{O}_{P_{S}\times G}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\left(=\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{P_{S}/S})\oplus\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{G})\right)
given by (
81
),
the
\mathcal{O}_{P_{S}\times G}
-linear morphism
(\omega_{P^{(N)}_{S}\times G}^{\mathrm{can}(N)})^{\triangleright}:\mathcal{T}_{P_{S}\times G/S}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{P_{S}\times G}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
coincides with the second projection
\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{P_{S}/S})\oplus\mathcal{O}_{P_{S}\times G}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{P_{S}\times G}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
.
Hence, since
(\omega_{P^{(N)}_{S}\times G}^{\mathrm{can}(N)}|_{\mathcal{E}_{H}^{\mathrm{triv}}})^{\triangleright}
may be obtained as
the restriction of
(\omega_{P^{(N)}_{S}\times G}^{\mathrm{can}(N)})^{\triangleright}
via
(\mathrm{id}_{S}\times\pi_{G},\mathrm{pr}_{2}):\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{triv}}_{H}\hookrightarrow P_{S}\times G
(cf. (
96
)),
we see that it coincides with
\omega_{S\times G/S}^{\triangleright}
.
This implies (
184
),
as desired.

Proposition 3.10.4
.

Let
\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\clubsuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega,\mathcal{G},\kappa)
be an
F^{1}
-Cartan geometry with model
(G,H)
on
X/S
.
Then, the underlying Cartan geometry
(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega)
is
p
-flat.
Moreover,
the map of sets

(185)

\displaystyle F^{1}\text{-}\mathrm{Car}_{(G,H),X/S}\rightarrow\mathrm{Car}^{p\text{-}\mathrm{flat}}_{(G,H),X/S}.

given by
\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\clubsuit}\mapsto(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega)
is bijective.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\clubsuit}
be as in the statement.
Since the
p
-curvature of
\omega_{\mathcal{G}}^{\mathrm{can}(N)}
vanishes (cf. Lemma
3.10.1
),
the equality
\kappa^{*}(\omega_{\mathcal{G}}^{\mathrm{can}(N)})|_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}=\omega
implies
{{}^{p}}\psi_{\omega}=0
.
Hence,
(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega)
turns out to be
p
-flat.

Conversely,
let
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega)
be a
p
-flat Cartan geometry with model
(G,H)
on
X/S
.
Just as in the proof of Theorem
3.9.3
, the data
(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega)
yields a
G
-bundle
\mathcal{G}
on
X^{(1)}
and an isomorphism of
G
-bundles
\alpha:\mathcal{E}_{G}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}F^{*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})
.
By the construction of
\mathcal{G}
together with
(
184
), the equality

\alpha^{*}(\omega^{\mathrm{can}(1)}_{\mathcal{G}})|_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}=\omega
holds.
That is to say, the quadruple
(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega,\mathcal{G},\alpha)
forms an
F^{1}
-Cartan geometry.
One may verify that the resulting assignment
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}\mapsto(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega,\mathcal{G},\alpha)
determines the inverse to (
185
).
This completes the proof of this proposition.
∎

Proposition 3.10.5
.

(Recall that
G
is assumed to be affine.)
There exists a canonical bijection

(186)

\displaystyle\zeta_{N}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\clubsuit}:F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(G,P),X/S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Car}_{(G,H),X/S},

and the formation of this bijection commutes with truncation to lower levels, pull-back to étale
X
-schemes, and base-change to
S
-schemes.
If
N=1
, then this bijection coincides with (
140
) under the identification
F^{1}\text{-}\mathrm{Car}_{(G,H),X/S}=\mathrm{Car}^{p\text{-}\mathrm{flat}}_{(G,H),X/S}
given by (
185
).

Proof.

First, we shall construct
\zeta_{N}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\clubsuit}
.
Let
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
be an
F^{N}
-
(G,P)
-structure on
X/S
.
The
1
-st truncation
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}|^{\langle 1\rangle}
of
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
corresponds
to
an
F^{1}
-Cartan geometry
(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega,\mathcal{G},\kappa)
with model
(G,H)
via
\zeta^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\clubsuit}
(cf. (
140
)) and (
185
).
Let us set
\mathcal{G}_{N}
to be the
G
-bundle on
X^{(N)}
defined as the inverse of the left
G_{X/S}^{(N)}
-torsor
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
.
Since
\mathcal{G}\cong F^{(N-1)*}_{X^{(1)}/S}(\mathcal{G}_{N})
by construction,

\kappa
may be regarded as an isomorphism
\mathcal{E}_{G}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}F^{(N)*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{G}_{N})\left(=F^{*}_{X/S}(F^{(N-1)*}_{X^{(1)}/S}(\mathcal{G}_{N}))\right)
.
Then,
the quadruple
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\clubsuit}_{N}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega,\mathcal{G}_{N},\kappa)
specifies an
F^{N}
-Cartan geometry with model
(G,H)
on
X/S
.
The resulting assignment
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}\mapsto\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\clubsuit}_{N}
defines a map of sets

F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(G,P),X/S}\rightarrow F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Car}_{(G,H),X/S}
, which we denote by
\zeta_{N}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\clubsuit}
.

Next, we shall construct the inverse to this map.
Let
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}_{N}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega,\mathcal{G},\kappa)
be an
F^{N}
-Cartan geometry
belonging to
F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Car}_{(G,H),X/S}
.
The
1
-st truncation
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}_{N}|^{\langle 1\rangle}
of
\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\clubsuit}
corresponds to an
F^{1}
-
(G,P)
-structure
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit}_{1}
via
\zeta^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\clubsuit}
and (
185
).
The
G
-bundle
\mathcal{G}

induces a left
G_{X/S}^{(N)}
-torsor
\breve{\mathcal{E}}^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit}_{N}
equipped with an isomorphism
\alpha_{\mathcal{S}}:\breve{\mathcal{E}}^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit}_{N}\times^{G_{X/S}^{(N)}}G_{X/S}^{(1)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit}_{1}
.
Then, the subsheaf
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit}_{N}
of
\mathcal{P}_{P,X/S}^{\text{\'{e}t}}
consisting of
the local sections of the form
\alpha_{\mathcal{S}}((v,e))
for various
v\in\breve{\mathcal{E}}^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit}_{N}

determines an
F^{N}
-
(G,P)
-structure on
X/S
.
The resulting assignment
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}_{N}\mapsto\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\heartsuit}_{N}
forms the inverse to the map
\zeta_{N}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\clubsuit}
, so
this completes the proof of this proposition.
∎

3.11.
F^{N}
-Cartan geometries for
N=\infty

In this final subsection of §
3
, we generalize
F^{N}
-Cartan geometries to
N=\infty
in terms of
F
-divided
G
-bundles.

Definition 3.11.1
.

An
F^{\infty}
-Cartan geometry with model
(G,H)
on
X/S
is a collection

(187)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}_{\infty}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega,\{(\mathcal{G}_{l},\varpi_{l})\}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}},\kappa)

such that
(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega,\mathcal{G}_{1},\kappa)
forms an
F^{1}
-Cartan geometry with
(G,H)
on
X/S
and the collection
\{(\mathcal{G}_{l},\varpi_{l})\}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
, where
(\mathcal{G}_{0},\varpi_{0}):=(\mathcal{E}_{G},\kappa)
, forms an
F
-divided
G
-bundle on
X/S
(cf. Definition
7.7.4
).
The notion of an isomorphism between two
F^{\infty}
-Cartan geometries can be defined in an evident manner.

Denote by

(188)

\displaystyle F^{\infty}\text{-}\mathrm{Car}_{(G,H),X/S}

the set of isomorphism classes of
F^{\infty}
-Cartan geometries with model
(G,H)
on
X/S
.
Let
N
be a positive integer and

\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}_{\infty}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega,\{(\mathcal{G}_{l},\varpi_{l})\}_{l},\kappa)
an
F^{\infty}
-Cartan geometry with model
(G,H)
on
X/S
.
Then,
\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}_{N}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega,\mathcal{G}_{N},\kappa_{N})
forms an
F^{N}
-Cartan geometry, where
\kappa_{N}:=F^{(N-1)*}_{X/S}(\varpi_{N-1})\circ\cdots\circ F^{(1)*}_{X/S}(\varpi_{1})\circ\kappa
.
The resulting assignment
\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}_{\infty}\mapsto\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}_{N}
defines a map of sets
F^{\infty}\text{-}\mathrm{Car}_{(G,H),X/S}\rightarrow F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Car}_{(G,H),X/S}
compatible with the morphisms in (
182
).
Hence, we obtain a map of sets

(189)

\displaystyle F^{\infty}\text{-}\mathrm{Car}_{(G,H),X/S}\rightarrow\varprojlim_{N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Car}_{(G,H),X/S}.

Proposition 3.11.2
.

Suppose that
G
is affine,
S=\mathrm{Spec}(k)
, and
X
is proper over
k
.
Then, (
189
) is bijective.
In particular, we obtain a bijection of sets

(190)

\displaystyle\zeta_{\infty}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\clubsuit}:F^{\infty}\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(G,P),X/S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}F^{\infty}\text{-}\mathrm{Car}_{(G,H),X/S}.

Proof.

The former assertion is a direct consequence of Lemma
3.11.3
described below.
The latter assertion follows from the former assertion, (
37
), and Proposition
3.10.5
.
∎

The following lemma was already proved in
[
18
, § 1, Proposition 1.7]
when
G=\mathrm{GL}_{n}
.

Lemma 3.11.3
.

Let us keep the assumption in Proposition
3.11.2
.
For each nonnegative integer
l
,
denote by
G\text{-}\mathrm{Bun}_{X^{(l)}}
the set of isomorphism classes of
G
-bundles on
X^{(l)}
.
Note that pulling-back
G
-bundles by relative Frobenius morphisms yields a projective system

(191)

\displaystyle\cdots\rightarrow G\text{-}\mathrm{Bun}_{X^{(l)}}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow G\text{-}\mathrm{Bun}_{X^{(2)}}\rightarrow G\text{-}\mathrm{Bun}_{X^{(1)}}\rightarrow G\text{-}\mathrm{Bun}_{X}.

Also, denote by
G\text{-}\mathrm{Bun}_{X^{(\infty)}}
the set of isomorphism classes of
F
-divided
G
-bundles on
X/k
.
Then, the map of sets

(192)

\displaystyle G\text{-}\mathrm{Bun}_{X^{(\infty)}}\rightarrow\varprojlim_{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}G\text{-}\mathrm{Bun}_{X^{(l)}}

given by
\{(\mathcal{G}_{l},\varpi_{l})\}_{l}\mapsto\{\mathcal{G}_{l}\}_{l}
is bijective.

Proof.

Since the surjectivity of (
192
) is clear, it suffices to consider the injectivity.
Let

\{(\mathcal{G}_{i,l},\varpi_{i,l})\}_{l}
(
i=1,2
) be
F
-divided
G
-bundles on
X/k
with
\mathcal{G}_{1,l}\cong\mathcal{G}_{2,l}
for every
l
.
For each
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, denote by
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{G}_{2,l})

the algebraic
k
-group classifying
G
-equivariant automorphisms of
\mathcal{G}_{2,l}
.
Notice that

\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{G}_{2,l})

is of finite type over
k
.
Indeed, let us fix a closed immersion between algebraic
k
-groups
G\hookrightarrow\mathrm{GL}_{m}
(
m\geq 1
).
(Such a morphism always exists because of the affineness assumption on
G
.)
Write
\mathcal{V}_{l}
for the vector bundle induced from

\mathcal{G}_{2,l}
via change of structure group by this closed immersion.
By the properness of
X/k
, the space
\mathrm{End}(\mathcal{V}_{l})
of endomorphisms of
\mathcal{V}_{l}
may be represented by a finite type scheme over
k
. Hence,

\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{G}_{2,l})

turns out to be of finite type because it forms a subscheme of
\mathrm{End}(\mathcal{V}_{l})
.
Now, let us consider
the map of sets

F_{X^{(l)}/k}^{\sharp}:\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{G}_{2,l+1})(k)\rightarrow\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{G}_{2,l})(k)
arising from pull-back by
F_{X^{(l)}}
.
Since
X
is reduced, we may identify
\mathcal{V}_{l+1}
as a subsheaf of
\mathcal{V}_{l}
, which implies
that
F_{X^{(l)}/k}^{\sharp}
is injective.
Moreover, since
\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{G}_{2,l})
’s are of finite type over
k
as proved above, there exists a positive integer
L
such that

F_{X^{(l)}/k}^{\sharp}
is bijective for any
l\geq L
.
If we take an isomorphism of

G
-bundles
\eta_{l}:\mathcal{G}_{1,l}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{G}_{2,l}
with
l\geq L
,
then
the bijectivity of
F_{X^{(l)}/k}^{\sharp}
implies that
there exists an isomorphism
\eta_{l+1}:\mathcal{G}_{1,l+1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{G}_{2,l+1}
with
\varpi_{2,l}\circ F_{X^{(l)}/k}^{*}(\eta_{l+1})=\eta_{l}\circ\varpi_{1,l}
.
By starting from the choice of
\eta_{L}
and applying this argument inductively, we obtain a compatible collection of isomorphisms
\{\eta_{l}\}_{l}
, specifying an isomorphism of
F
-divided
G
-bundles
\{(\mathcal{G}_{1,l},\varpi_{1,l})\}_{l}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\{(\mathcal{G}_{2,l},\varpi_{2,l})\}_{l}
.
This concludes the injectivity of (
192
), and hence, completes the proof of this lemma.
∎

4.
Indigenous bundles on smooth schemes

This section deals with indigenous bundles (including the case of finite level) on a smooth scheme, generalizing the classical notion defined on a Riemann surface (cf.
[
21
]
).
As one of the main results of this section, we prove that, for each level,
p
-flat Cartan geometries correspond bijectively to dormant indigenous bundles (cf. Propositions
4.4.2
,
4.5.4
, and Corollary
4.5.5
).
In particular, the proofs of Theorems
A
and
B
will be completed in this section.
Also, the resulting correspondence enables us to prove several properties on a smooth scheme having an
F^{\infty}
-
(G,P)
-structure (cf. Propositions
4.6.1
,
4.6.2
).
The last two subsections will be devoted to studying
F^{N}
-
(G,P)
-structures in the case where
G
is a product of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
’s and
\mathbb{G}_{a}
.

Let
S
be a scheme over
k
and
X
a smooth scheme over
S
.

4.1.
The Kodaira-Spencer map

Let
\mathcal{E}_{H}
be an
H
-bundle on
X
, and
write
\mathcal{E}_{G}:=\mathcal{E}_{H}\times^{H}G
.
The differential of the natural inclusion
\mathcal{E}_{H}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{G}
induces
an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear injection

\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
(cf. §
7.1
for the definition of
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{(-)/S}
).
We shall regard
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}
as an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-submodule of
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}/S}
via this injection.

Definition 4.1.1
.

Let
\nabla:\mathcal{T}_{X/S}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}/S}
be an
S
-connection on the
G
-bundle
\mathcal{E}_{G}
.
The
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear composite

(193)

\displaystyle\mathrm{KS}_{(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)}:\mathcal{T}_{X/S}\xrightarrow{\nabla}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}/S}\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}/S}/\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}

is called the
Kodaira-Spencer map
associated with
(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)
.

Remark 4.1.2
.

Let us keep the above notation.
Differentiating the inclusion
\mathcal{E}_{H}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{G}
gives the following morphism of short exact sequences:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{inclusion}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{inclusion}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{X/S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}/S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{X/S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

This morphism induces an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear isomorphism

(198)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}/\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}/S}/\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}.

By this isomorphism,
the Kodaira-Spencer map
\mathrm{KS}_{(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)}
may be regarded as an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear morphism
\mathcal{T}_{X/S}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}/\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}
.

Example 4.1.3

(The cases of
G=\mathbb{G}_{m}
and
\mathbb{G}_{a}
)
.

(i)

First, we shall consider the case where
G=\mathbb{G}_{m}
and
H=\{e\}
.
Let us take a
1
-form
\omega
on
X/S
.
Recall that
\omega
induces an
S
-connection
\nabla_{\omega}
on the trivial
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-bundle
X\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
(cf. (
84
)).
Then, the Kodaira-Spencer map
\mathrm{KS}_{(X\times\{e\},\nabla_{\omega})}
associated with the pair
(X\times\{e\},\nabla_{\omega})

coincides with
\omega^{\triangleright}
under the natural identification

(199)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{X}\cong\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbb{G}_{m})_{X\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}/\mathrm{Lie}(\{e\})_{X\times\{e\}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{Eoraplw2})}}{{\cong}}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{X\times\mathbb{G}_{m}/S}/\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{X\times\{e\}/S}.

Note that giving a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-bundle equipped with both an
S
-connection and an
\{e\}
-reduction is equivalent to giving an
S
-connection on the trivial
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-bundle, i.e.,
\nabla_{\omega}
for some
\omega
as above.

(ii)

Next, suppose that
G=\mathbb{G}_{a}
and
H=\{e\}
.
We shall identify
\mathbb{G}_{a}
as the subgroup of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}
consisting of matrices

\begin{pmatrix}1&a\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}
for some
a\in\mathbb{G}_{a}
.
Each
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-bundle may be thought of as a rank
2
vector bundle
\mathcal{V}
equipped with a structure of extension
0\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow 0
.
Moreover, a choice of an
\{e\}
-reduction corresponds to a splitting
\mathcal{V}\cong\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus 2}
of this extension.
The vector bundle
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus 2}
equipped with the extension
0\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}\xrightarrow{a\mapsto(a,0)}\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus 2}\xrightarrow{(a,b)\mapsto b}\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow 0
corresponds to the trivial
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-bundle
X\times\mathbb{G}_{a}
.

For each
1
-form
\chi
on
X/S
,
denote by

(200)

\displaystyle\chi^{\dagger}:\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus 2}\rightarrow\Omega_{X/S}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus 2})\left(=\Omega_{X/S}^{\oplus 2}\right)

the
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear morphism defines as
\begin{pmatrix}0&\chi\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}
.
Then, the morphism

(201)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\chi}^{\dagger}:=d^{\oplus 2}+\chi^{\dagger}

turns out to define an
S
-connection on the trivial
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-bundle.
The Kodaira-Spencer map
\mathrm{KS}_{(X\times\{e\},\nabla_{\chi}^{\dagger})}
associated with the pair
(X\times\{e\},\nabla^{\dagger}_{\chi})
coincides with

\chi^{\triangleright}
under the identification
\mathcal{O}_{X}\cong\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{X\times\mathbb{G}_{a}/S}/\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{X\times\{e\}/S}
similar to (
199
).
Also, giving a
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-bundle equipped with both an
S
-connection and an
\{e\}
-reduction is equivalent to giving an
S
-connection on
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus 2}
of the form
\nabla_{\chi}^{\dagger}
for some
\chi
as above.

Definition 4.1.4
.

(i)

An
indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle
on
X/S

is a pair

(202)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)

consisting of an
H
-bundle
\mathcal{E}_{H}
on
X
and an
S
-connection
\nabla
on
\mathcal{E}_{G}:=\mathcal{E}_{H}\times^{H}G
such that the Kodaira-Spencer map
\mathrm{KS}_{(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)}
is an isomorphism.

(ii)

Let
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)
and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{H},\nabla^{\prime})
be indigenous
(G,H)
-bundles on
X/S
.
An
isomorphism of indigenous
(G,H)
-bundles
from
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
to
\mathcal{E}^{\prime\spadesuit}
is defined as an isomorphism of
H
-bundles
\mathcal{E}_{H}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{H}
such that the induced isomorphism of
G
-bundles
\mathcal{E}_{G}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{G}
is compatible with the respective connections, i.e.,
\nabla
and
\nabla^{\prime}
.

Definition 4.1.5
.

Let
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)
be an indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle on
X
.
We shall say that
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
is
flat
if the curvature
\psi_{\nabla}
(cf. (
406
)) of
\nabla
vanishes.

Denote by

(203)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),X/S}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),X/S}^{\flat}\right)

the set of isomorphism classes of indigenous
(G,H)
-bundles (resp., flat indigenous
(G,H)
-bundles) on
X/S
.

Example 4.1.6

(Standard example)
.

Recall that
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{triv}}_{H}:=S\times G
(cf. Example
3.4.3
) specifies an
H
-reduction on
P_{S}\times G
via
(\mathrm{id}_{S}\times\pi_{G},\mathrm{pr}_{2}):\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{triv}}_{H}\hookrightarrow P_{S}\times G
.
In particular, there exists a natural isomorphism of
G
-bundles
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{triv}}_{G}\left(:=\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{triv}}_{H}\times^{H}G\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}P_{S}\times G
extending this inclusion.
Since the composite
G\xrightarrow{(\mathrm{id}_{S}\times\pi_{G},\mathrm{pr}_{2})}P_{S}\times G\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}G
coincides with the identity morphism,
the composite

(204)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}^{\mathrm{triv}}/S}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{P_{S}\times G/S}\left(\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tau}}{{\cong}}\mathcal{T}_{P_{S}/S}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{P_{S}}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\right)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}\mathcal{O}_{P_{S}}\otimes\mathfrak{g}

is an isomorphism.
This implies that the Kodaira-Spencer map
\mathrm{KS}_{(\mathcal{E}_{H}^{\mathrm{triv}},\nabla_{0})}
associated with

(\mathcal{E}_{H}^{\mathrm{triv}},\nabla_{0})
(cf. (
84
) for the definition of
\nabla_{(-)}
) is an isomorphism.
That is to say, the pair

(205)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{triv},\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{triv}}_{H},\nabla_{0})

forms a flat indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle on
P_{S}/S
.
It is clear that
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{triv},\spadesuit}
is also dormant (cf. Definition
4.4.1
described later).

4.2.
Pull-back and base-change of Indigenous
(G,H)
-bundles

Let
Y
and
y
be as in §
2.4
.
Also, let
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)
be an indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle on
X/S
.
The base-change
y^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{H})
(resp.,
y^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{G})
) of
\mathcal{E}_{H}
(resp.,
\mathcal{E}_{G}:=\mathcal{E}_{H}\times^{H}G
) has an étale morphism
y^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{H})\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{H}
(resp.,
y^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{G})\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{G}
), whose differential yields an isomorphism
d\widetilde{y}_{H}:\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{y^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{H})/S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}y^{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S})
(resp.,
d\widetilde{y}_{G}:\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{y^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{G})/S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}y^{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}/S})
).
The pull-back
y^{*}(\nabla):y^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{X/S})\rightarrow y^{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}/S})
of
\nabla
specifies an
S
-connection on
y^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{G})
under the identifications

\mathcal{T}_{Y/S}=y^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{X/S})
,
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{y^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{G})/S}=y^{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}/S})
given by
dy
,
d\widetilde{y}_{G}
respectively.
Since
d\widetilde{y}_{G}
is restricted to
d\widetilde{y}_{H}
,
we obtain the following commutative square diagram:

\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{Y/S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{KS}_{(y^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{H}),y^{*}(\nabla))}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{dy}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{y^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{G})/S}/\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{y^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{H})/S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{y^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{X/S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{y^{*}(\mathrm{KS}_{(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)})}
\textstyle{y^{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}/S})/y^{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}).}

This implies that the Kodaira-Spencer map
\mathrm{KS}_{(y^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{H}),y^{*}(\nabla))}
is an isomorphism, and hence, the pair of pull-backs

(210)

\displaystyle y^{*}(\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}):=(y^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{H}),y^{*}(\nabla))

forms an indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle on
Y/S
.
We shall refer
y^{*}(\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})
as the
pull-back
of
(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)
.
Because of the equality
y^{*}(\psi_{\nabla})=\psi_{y^{*}(\nabla)}
,
we see that
y^{*}(\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})
is flat if
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
is flat.
Hence, the assignment
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}\mapsto y^{*}(\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})
defines maps

(211)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),X/S}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),Y/S},\hskip 14.22636pt\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),X/S}^{\flat}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),Y/S}^{\flat}.

The formation of a(n) (flat) indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle on
X/S
has descent with respect to the étale topology on
X
.

Next, let
s:S^{\prime}\rightarrow S
and
X^{\prime}
be as in §
2.4
and let
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
be as above.
We shall use the notation
s^{*}(-)
to denote the result of base-change by
s
.
The base-change
s^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{H})
defines an
H
-reduction of

s^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{G})
,
and the differential of the projection
s^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{G})\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{G}
yields an isomorphism
d\widetilde{s}_{G}:\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{s^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{G})/S^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}s^{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}/S})
.
Just as in the above discussion,
the base-change
s^{*}(\nabla)
defines an
S^{\prime}
-connection on
s^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{G})
under the identification
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{s^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{G})/S^{\prime}}=s^{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}/S})
given by
d\widetilde{s}_{G}
.
Also,
the Kodaira-Spencer map associated with
(s^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{H}),s^{*}(\nabla))
may be identified with the isomorphism
s^{*}(\mathrm{KS}_{(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)})
.
Thus, the pair of base-changes

(212)

\displaystyle s^{*}(\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})\left(\text{or}\ S^{\prime}\times_{S}\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}\right):=(s^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{H}),s^{*}(\nabla))

forms an indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle on
X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}
, called the
base-change
of
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
.
Since
the equality
s^{*}(\psi_{\nabla})=\psi_{s^{*}(\nabla)}
holds,

s^{*}(\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})
is flat if
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
is flat.
Thus, the resulting assignment
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}\mapsto s^{*}(\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})
determines maps

(213)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),X/S}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}},\hskip 14.22636pt\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),X/S}^{\flat}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}^{\flat}.

4.3.
From
(G,H)
-Cartan geometries to Indigenous
(G,H)
-bundles

We shall construct an indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle by using a Cartan geometry with model
(G,H)
.

Let
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega)
be a Cartan geometry with model
(G,H)
on
X/S
.
Denote by
\pi:\mathcal{E}_{H}\rightarrow X

the structure morphism of
\mathcal{E}_{H}
and by

\jmath:\mathcal{E}_{H}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{G}:=\mathcal{E}_{H}\times^{H}G
the natural inclusion.
The composite
\jmath^{*}(\omega^{\triangleright}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}/X})^{-1}\circ\omega^{\triangleright}
(cf. (
73
)) defines an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}
-linear isomorphism
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\jmath^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}/X})
.
The direct image
\pi_{*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\pi_{*}(\jmath^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}/X}))
of this morphism preserves the
H
-actions,
so it is restricted to an isomorphism
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\pi_{*}(\jmath^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}/X}))^{H}
.
Since
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
is naturally isomorphic to
\pi_{*}(\jmath^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}/X}))^{H}
,
it may be regarded as an isomorphism

(214)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}:\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}=\right)\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}.

By the commutativity of (
–
‣
(i)
),
the restriction of
\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}
to
\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\left(\subseteq\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}\right)
coincides with the inclusion
\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
.
Observe here that
(
4.1.2
)
induces
an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear isomorphism
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}\times^{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}}\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}/S}
.
Under the identification
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}\times^{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}}\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}=\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}/S}
given by this isomorphism,
the morphism
(\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright},\mathrm{id}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}}):\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\times^{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}}\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}

determines a split
surjection
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}

of (
405
) for
\mathcal{E}_{G}
.
The corresponding split injection

(215)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}}:\mathcal{T}_{X/S}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}/S}

specifies
an
S
-connection on the
G
-bundle
\mathcal{E}_{G}
.

The direct image
\pi_{*}(\bigwedge^{2}\Omega_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}\otimes\mathfrak{g})\left(=\pi_{*}(\bigwedge^{2}\Omega_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S})\otimes\mathfrak{g}\right)

has a right
H
-action given by
(a\otimes v)\cdot h:=d\mathrm{R}_{h}^{\vee}(a)\otimes\mathrm{Ad}_{G}(h^{-1})(v)
for
a\in\pi_{*}(\bigwedge^{2}\Omega_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S})
,
v\in\mathfrak{g}
, and
h\in H
.
Because of the second condition in Definition
3.4.1
, (i),
the curvature
\psi_{\omega}
of the
1
-form
\nabla_{\omega}
, being an element of
\Gamma(\mathcal{E}_{H},\bigwedge^{2}\Omega_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}\otimes\mathfrak{g})\left(=\Gamma(X,\pi_{*}(\bigwedge^{2}\Omega_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}\otimes\mathfrak{g}))\right)
, is invariant under this
H
-action.
Hence,
\psi_{\omega}
defines an element of

\Gamma(X,\pi_{*}(\bigwedge^{2}\Omega_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}\otimes\mathfrak{g})^{H})
.

Proposition 4.3.1
.

The pair

(216)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla_{\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}})

defines an indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle on
X/S
.
Moreover,
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}
is flat if and only if

\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}
is flat.

Proof.

For simplicity, we shall write
\nabla:=\nabla_{\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}}
.
First, let us consider
the former assertion.
By the definition of
\mathrm{KS}_{(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)}
,
the following diagram forms a morphism of short exact sequences:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{X/k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{KS}_{(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{inclusion}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{quotient}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}/\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Hence, since
\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}
is an isomorphism,
the Kodaira-Spencer map
\mathrm{KS}_{(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)}
is an isomorphism.
That is to say, the pair
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)
specifies an indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle on
X/S
, which
completes the proof of the former assertion.

Next, let us consider the latter assertion.
Note that
the assignments
\mathcal{V}\mapsto\pi_{*}(\mathcal{V})^{H}
and
\mathcal{U}\mapsto\pi^{*}(\mathcal{U})
define an equivalence of categories

(221)

\displaystyle\left(\begin{matrix}\text{the category of $H$-equivariant }\\
\text{vector bundles on $\mathcal{E}_{H}$}\end{matrix}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\left(\begin{matrix}\text{the category of}\\
\text{vector bundles on $X$}\end{matrix}\right).

In particular,
since
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}=\pi_{*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S})^{H}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}=\pi_{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\otimes\mathfrak{g})^{H}
,
the natural morphisms

(222)

\displaystyle\pi^{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S})\rightarrow\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}\hskip 8.53581pt\text{and}\hskip 8.53581pt\pi^{*}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}})\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\otimes\mathfrak{g}

are isomorphisms.
We shall define
\psi_{\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}}
to be the
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear morphism
\bigwedge^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
determined by

(223)

\displaystyle\psi_{\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}}(\partial_{1},\partial_{2})=\partial_{1}(\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}(\partial_{2}))-\partial_{2}(\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}(\partial_{1}))+[\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}(\partial_{1}),\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}(\partial_{2})]-\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}([\partial_{1},\partial_{2}])

for any local sections
\partial_{1},\partial_{2}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}
.
Under the identifications
\pi^{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S})=\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}
,
\pi^{*}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}})=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
given by (
222
),
the equality
\pi^{*}(\psi_{\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}})=\psi_{\omega}
holds (cf. the last paragraph in the discussion preceding this proposition).
Hence,
\psi_{\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}}=0
if and only if
\psi_{\omega}=0
.
On the other hand,

\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}
can be expressed as
\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}=\nabla\circ d_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}-\mathrm{id}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}}
.
If we take arbitrary local sections
\partial_{1},\partial_{2}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}
and write
\overline{\partial}_{1}:=d_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}(\partial_{1})
,
\overline{\partial}_{2}:=d_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}(\partial_{2})\left(\in\mathcal{T}_{X/S}\right)
, then (
223
) induces the following sequence of equalities:

(224)

\displaystyle\ \psi_{\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}}(\partial_{1},\partial_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ [\partial_{1},\nabla(\overline{\partial}_{2})-\partial_{2}]-[\partial_{2},\nabla(\overline{\partial}_{1})-\partial_{1}]

\displaystyle\ +[\nabla(\overline{\partial}_{1})-\partial_{1},\nabla(\overline{\partial}_{2})-\partial_{2}]-\left(\nabla(d_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}([\partial_{1},\partial_{2}]))-[\partial_{1},\partial_{2}]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \left(\cancel{[\partial_{1},\nabla(\overline{\partial}_{2})]}-\cancel{[\partial_{1},\partial_{2}]}\right)-\left(\cancel{[\partial_{2},\nabla(\overline{\partial}_{1})]}-\cancel{[\partial_{2},\partial_{1}]}\right)

\displaystyle\ +\left([\nabla(\overline{\partial}_{1}),\nabla(\overline{\partial}_{2})]-\cancel{[\nabla(\overline{\partial}_{1}),\partial_{2}]}-\cancel{[\partial_{1},\nabla(\overline{\partial}_{2})]}+\cancel{[\partial_{1},\partial_{2}]}\right)-\left(\nabla([\overline{\partial}_{1},\overline{\partial}_{2}])-\cancel{[\partial_{1},\partial_{2}]}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \psi_{\nabla}(\overline{\partial}_{1},\overline{\partial}_{2}).

Thus, since
d_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}:\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}_{X/S}
is surjective, we see that
\psi_{\nabla}=0
if and only if
\psi_{\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}}=0
, or equivalently,
\psi_{\omega}=0
by
the above discussion.
This completes the proof of the latter assertion.
∎

Corollary 4.3.2
.

The assignment
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}\mapsto\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}
resulting from Proposition
4.3.1
defines a bijection of sets

(225)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Car}_{(G,H),X/S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),X/S}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathrm{Car}_{(G,H),X/S}^{\flat}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),X/S}^{\flat}\right).

Moreover, the formation of this bijection is compatible with pull-back to étale schemes over
X
, as well as with base-change to schemes over
S
.

Proof.

Since the resp’d assertion follows from the non-resp’d assertion and the latter assertion of Proposition
4.3.1
, it suffices to consider the non-resp’d assertion.
To prove this,
we shall construct the inverse to the assignment
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}\mapsto\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}
.
Let
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)
be an indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle on
X/S
.
Denote by
\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}
the composite
of the natural inclusion
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
and the split surjection
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}/S}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}/S}
of (
405
) corresponding to
\nabla
.
This morphism fits into the following morphism of short exact sequences:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
