\textstyle{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\omega}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{\triangleright}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{X/S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{KS}_{(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{inclusion}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{quotient}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}/\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

The Kodaira-Spencer map
\mathrm{KS}_{(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)}
is an isomorphism,
so the five lemma shows that

\widetilde{\omega}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{\triangleright}
is an isomorphism.
Under the identifications given by (
222
),
the pull-back of
\widetilde{\omega}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{\triangleright}
by
\pi

defines an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}
-linear isomorphism
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
.
If
\omega_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}
denotes the corresponding
\mathfrak{g}
-valued
1
-form on
\mathcal{E}_{H}/S
, then the pair
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit\Rightarrow\clubsuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}})
forms a Cartan geometry with model
(G,H)
on
X/S
.
(The commutativity of the left-hand square in (
4.3
) implies, via pull-back by
\pi
, the first condition in Definition
3.4.1
, (i).)
Moreover, the resulting assignment
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}\mapsto\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit\Rightarrow\clubsuit}
is verified to define the inverse to the assignment
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}\mapsto\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}
.
This implies the bijectivity of (
225
).
The required compatibility of the formation
follows immediately from its construction, and hence,
we finish the proof of this corollary.
∎

4.4.
Dormant indigenous
(G,H)
-bundles

Next, we make the definition of a dormant indigenous bundle.
If the underlying space is a curve, then this notion was already defined and has been studied in previous works (cf., e.g.,
[
40
]
).

Definition 4.4.1
.

We shall say that an indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)
is
dormant
if it is flat and the
p
-curvature
{{}^{p}}\psi_{\nabla}
of
\nabla
(cf. (
420
)) vanishes identically on
X
.

Denote by

(230)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),X/S}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}

the subset of
\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),X}^{\flat}
consisting of dormant indigenous
(G,H)
-bundles.
Then, we obtain
the remaining portion of Theorem
A
described below.
(The map
\zeta^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}
, which is one of the required bijections in that theorem, is defined as the composite of
\zeta^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}
constructed below and
\zeta^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\clubsuit}
.)

Proposition 4.4.2
.

The bijection
\mathrm{Car}_{(G,H),X/S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),X/S}
resulting from Corollary
4.3.2
is restricted to a bijection of sets

(231)

\displaystyle\zeta^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}:\mathrm{Car}^{p\text{-}\mathrm{flat}}_{(G,H),X/S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),X/S}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}.

Moreover, the formation of this bijection commutes with pull-back to étale schemes over
X
, as well as with base-change to schemes over
S
.

Proof.

We shall consider the former assertion.
Let
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega)
be a Cartan geometry with model
(G,H)
on
X/S
.
Denote by
\pi:\mathcal{E}_{H}\rightarrow X
the structure morphism of
\mathcal{E}_{H}
and by
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)

the indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle corresponding to
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}
via (
225
), i.e.,
\nabla:=\nabla_{\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}}
.
Under the identifications
\pi^{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S})=\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}
,
\pi^{*}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}})=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
given by (
222
),
the
p
-curvature
{{}^{p}}\psi_{\omega}
of
\omega
may be regarded as an
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\left(=\pi^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{X})\right)
-linear morphism
\left(F_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}^{*}(\pi^{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}))=\right)\pi^{*}(F^{*}_{X}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}))\rightarrow\pi^{*}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}})
.
This morphism comes from an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear morphism
{{}^{p}}\psi_{\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}}:F^{*}_{X}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S})\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
via pull-back by
\pi
.
If
\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}:\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
denotes the isomorphism constructed from
\mathcal{E}^{\clubsuit}
as in (
214
),
then
{{}^{p}}\psi_{\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}}
is defined
by

(232)

\displaystyle{{}^{p}}\psi_{\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}}(F^{-1}_{X}(\partial))=\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}(\partial)^{[p]}-\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}(\partial^{[p]})+\sum_{i=1}^{p-1}\frac{s_{i}(\partial,\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}(\partial))}{i}

for any local section
\partial\in\mathcal{T}_{X/S}
.
Let us take an arbitrary local section
\partial\in\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}
and write
\overline{\partial}:=d_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}(\partial)\in\mathcal{T}_{X/S}
.
Since
\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}=\nabla\circ d_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}-\mathrm{id}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}}
, we obtain the following sequence of equalities:

(233)

\displaystyle\ {{}^{p}}\psi_{\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}}(F^{-1}_{X}(\partial))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ (\nabla\circ d_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}-\mathrm{id})(\partial)^{[p]}-(\nabla\circ d_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}-\mathrm{id})(\partial^{[p]})+\sum_{i=1}^{p-1}\frac{s_{i}(\partial,(\nabla\circ d_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}-\mathrm{id})(\partial))}{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ (\nabla(\overline{\partial})-\partial)^{[p]}-(\nabla(\overline{\partial}^{[p]})-\partial^{[p]})+\sum_{i=1}^{p-1}\frac{s_{i}(\partial,\nabla(\overline{\partial})-\partial)}{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \cancel{(-1)^{p}\cdot\partial^{[p]}}+\nabla(\overline{\partial})^{[p]}+\cancel{\sum_{i=1}^{p-1}\frac{s_{i}(-\partial,\nabla(\overline{\partial}))}{i}}-\nabla(\overline{\partial}^{[p]})+\cancel{\partial^{[p]}}+\cancel{\sum_{i=1}^{p-1}\frac{s_{i}(\partial,\nabla(\overline{\partial})-\partial)}{i}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ {{}^{p}}\psi_{\nabla}(F^{-1}_{X}(\overline{\partial})),

where the last equality follows from Lemma
4.4.3
described below.
Hence, the surjectivity of
d_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}:\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}/S}\rightarrow\mathcal{T}_{X/S}
implies that
{{}^{p}}\psi_{\nabla}=0
if and only if
{{}^{p}}\psi_{\widetilde{\omega}^{\triangleright}}=0
, or equivalently,
{{}^{p}}\psi_{\omega}=0
by the above discussion.
This completes the proof of the former assertion.
The latter assertion follows immediately from its construction.
∎

The following lemma was used in the proof of the above proposition.

Lemma 4.4.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{a}
be a Lie algebra over a
k
-algebra
R
.
Then, for any elements
v,u\in\mathfrak{a}
,
the following equality holds:

(234)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{p-1}\frac{s_{i}(v,u-v)}{i}=-\sum_{i=1}^{p-1}\frac{s_{i}(-v,u)}{i}.

Proof.

By the definition of
s_{i}
, the following sequence of equalities holds:

(235)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{p-1}s_{i}(v,u-v)t^{i-1}=

\displaystyle\ \mathrm{ad}(vt+u-v)^{p-1}(v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathrm{ad}(v(t-1)+u)^{p-1}(v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sum_{j=1}^{p-1}s_{j}(v,u)(t-1)^{j-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sum_{i=1}^{p-1}\left(\sum_{j=i}^{p-1}(-1)^{j-i}\cdot\binom{j-1}{i-1}\cdot s_{j}(v,u)\right)t^{i-1}.

This implies
s_{i}(v,u-v)=\sum\limits_{j=i}^{p-1}(-1)^{j-i}\cdot\binom{j-1}{i-1}\cdot s_{j}(v,u)
for
i=1,\cdots,p-1
.
Thus, we have

(236)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{p-1}\frac{s_{i}(v,u-v)}{i}=

\displaystyle\ \sum_{i=1}^{p-1}\sum\limits_{j=i}^{p-1}\frac{1}{i}\cdot(-1)^{j-i}\cdot\binom{j-1}{i-1}\cdot s_{j}(v,u)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sum_{j=1}^{p-1}\frac{s_{j}(v,u)}{j}\cdot\left(\sum_{i=1}^{j}(-1)^{j-i}\cdot\binom{j}{i}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sum_{j=1}^{p-1}\frac{s_{j}(v,u)}{j}\cdot(-1)^{j+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ -\sum_{j=1}^{p-1}\frac{s_{j}(-v,u)}{j}.

This completes the proof of this lemma.
∎

4.5.
Indigenous
(G,H)
-bundles of higher level

We generalize both the notion of an indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle and the bijection
\zeta^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}
to higher level.
In the rest of this section, suppose that
G
is affine.
Let
N
be an element of
\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\sqcup\{\infty\}
.
(Whenever we deal with
(N-1)
-PD stratifications on a
G
-bundle for
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, we always suppose that the scheme
S
is equipped with an
(N-1)
-PD ideal
(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\gamma)
, as in §
7.4
, such that
\gamma
extends to
X
.)

Definition 4.5.1
.

(i)

An
indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle of level
N
on
X/S
is a pair

(237)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}_{N}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})

consisting of an
H
-bundle
\mathcal{E}_{H}
on
X
and an
(N-1)
-PD stratification
\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}
(cf. Definition
7.5.1
) on
\mathcal{E}_{G}:=\mathcal{E}_{H}\times^{H}G
such that if
\nabla
denotes the flat
S
-connection on
\mathcal{E}_{G}
induced by the
0
-PD stratification
\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}|^{\langle 0\rangle}
, i.e.,
\nabla:=\nabla_{\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}|^{\langle 0\rangle}}
(cf. (
467
) and (
479
)), then
(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)
forms an indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle on
X/S
.

(ii)

Let
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}_{N}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})
and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime\spadesuit}_{N}:=(\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{H},\varepsilon^{\prime\blacklozenge})

be indigenous
(G,H)
-bundles of level
N
on
X/S
.
An
isomorphism of indigenous
(G,H)
-bundles of level
N
from
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}_{N}
to
\mathcal{E}^{\prime\spadesuit}_{N}
is an isomorphism
\mathcal{E}_{H}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{H}
of
H
-bundles such that the induced isomorphism of
G
-bundles
\mathcal{E}_{G}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{G}
is compatible with the respective
(N-1)
-PD stratifications, i.e.,
\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}
and
\varepsilon^{\prime\blacklozenge}
.

Given an indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle
\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\blacklozenge}
of level
N
, we can define, in an obvious way, the pull-back of
\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\blacklozenge}
to an étale
X
-scheme, as well as with the base-change of
\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\blacklozenge}
to an
S
-scheme. (But we leave the details to the reader.)

Definition 4.5.2
.

Let
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}_{N}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})
be an indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle of level
N
.
If
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
(i.e.,
N\neq\infty
), then we shall say that
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}_{N}
is
dormant
if the
p
-
(N-1)
-curvature
{{}^{p}}\psi_{\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}}
of
\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}
(cf. Definition
7.7.1
) vanishes identically.
Also, if
N=\infty
, then we shall refer to any such
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}_{N}
as being
dormant
, for convenience.

Denote by

(238)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),X/S,N}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}

the set of isomorphism classes of dormant indigenous
(G,H)
-bundles of level
N
on
X/S
.
It follows from Proposition
7.8.1
that
the notion of dormant indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle is equivalent to that of level
1
.
That is to say, we have a natural identification

(239)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),X/S,1}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}=\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),X/S}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}.

Let

N^{\prime}
be another element of
\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\sqcup\{\infty\}
with
N\leq N^{\prime}
and
and
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}_{N}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})
a(n) (dormant) indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle of level
N^{\prime}
.
Then, the pair

(240)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}_{N}|^{\langle N\rangle}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}|^{(N-1)})

(cf. (
467
)) specifies a(n) (dormant) indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle of level
N
, which is called the
N
-th truncation
of
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
.
The resulting assignments
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}\mapsto\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}|^{\langle N\rangle}
for various pairs
(N,N^{\prime})
with
N\leq N^{\prime}
give a projective system

(241)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),X/S,\infty}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),X/S,N}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),X/S,2}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),X/S,1}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}.

Example 4.5.3

(Standard example)
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.
Then, the trivial
G
-bundle
P_{S}^{(N)}\times G
on
P_{S}^{(N)}
determines an
(N-1)
-PD
stratification
\varepsilon^{\mathrm{triv},\blacklozenge}_{N}
on
P_{S}\times G
via (
475
).
Then, the pair

(242)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\mathrm{triv},\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H}^{\mathrm{triv}},\varepsilon^{\mathrm{triv},\blacklozenge}_{N})

(cf. (
205
)) forms a dormant indigenous
(G,P)
-bundle of level
N
on
P_{S}/S
.
Since the formation of
\varepsilon^{\mathrm{triv},\blacklozenge}_{N}
commutes with truncation to lower levels, the collection
\{\varepsilon^{\mathrm{triv},\blacklozenge}_{N}\}_{N}
specifies an
\infty
-PD stratification
\varepsilon_{\infty}^{\mathrm{triv},\blacklozenge}
.
Thus, we obtain a dormant indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle

\mathcal{E}_{\infty}^{\mathrm{triv},\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H}^{\mathrm{triv}},\varepsilon^{\mathrm{triv},\blacklozenge}_{\infty})
of level
\infty
.
It is immediately verified that
\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\mathrm{triv},\spadesuit}
corresponds to the standard example of
F^{N}
-Cartan geometry
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{triv},\clubsuit}_{N}
(cf. (
183
)) via the bijection asserted in Proposition
4.5.4
later.

The remaining portions of Theorem
B
are completed by the following Proposition
4.5.4
and Corollary
4.5.5
.
(The map
\zeta_{N}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}
, which is one of the required bijections in that theorem, is defined as the composite of
\zeta_{N}^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}
below and the bijection
\zeta^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\clubsuit}_{N}
asserted in Propositions
3.10.5
and
3.11.2
.)

Proposition 4.5.4
.

(Recall that
G
is assumed to be affine.)
For any
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\sqcup\{\infty\}
,
there exists a canonical bijection
of sets

(243)

\displaystyle\zeta_{N}^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}:F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Car}_{(G,H),X/S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),X/S,N}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}},

and the formation of this bijection commutes with truncation to lower levels, pull-back to étale
X
-schemes, and base-change to
S
-schemes.

Proof.

If
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and
\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\clubsuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\omega,\mathcal{G},\kappa)
is an
F^{N}
-Cartan geometry with model
(G,H)
,
then the pair
\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})
, where
\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}
denotes the
(N-1)
-PD stratification on
\mathcal{E}_{G}:=\mathcal{E}_{H}\times^{H}G
corresponding to
\mathcal{G}
via (
475
), forms a dormant indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle of level
N
;
the resulting assignment
\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\clubsuit}\mapsto\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}
defines the required bijection for
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
because of Proposition
4.4.2
and Proposition
7.7.3
, (i).
Moreover, since this bijection commutes with truncation to lower levels (cf. Proposition
7.7.3
, (ii)), the case of
N=\infty
follows from Corollary
7.7.5
.
∎

Corollary 4.5.5
.

Suppose that
G
is affine,
S=\mathrm{Spec}(k)
, and
X
is proper over
k
.
Then,
the composite

(244)

\displaystyle\zeta_{\infty}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}:=\zeta_{\infty}^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}\circ\zeta_{\infty}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\clubsuit}:F^{\infty}\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(G,P),X/S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),X/S,\infty}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}

(cf. (
190
) for the definition of
\zeta_{\infty}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\clubsuit}
) is bijective.
In particular,
the map of sets

(245)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),X/S,\infty}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\rightarrow\varprojlim_{N\geq\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),X/S,N}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}

induced by (
241
) is bijective.

Proof.

The assertion follows from Propositions
3.11.2
and
4.5.4
.
∎

Example 4.5.6
.

We shall briefly recall the study of
[
53
]
, in which indigenous
(G,H)
-bundles for
(G,H)=(\mathrm{PGL}_{n+1},\mathrm{PGL}_{n+1}^{\circledcirc})
and
(\mathrm{PGL}_{n+1},\mathrm{PGL}_{n+1}^{\circledcirc})
are discussed in terms of vector bundles.
Suppose that

X
is a smooth projective scheme over
S=\mathrm{Spec}(k)
of dimension
n
, and denote by

\omega_{X}
the canonical line bundle on
X
.
Then, we obtain the composite isomorphism

(246)

\displaystyle\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{D}_{X/k,0}^{(N-1)})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)}/\mathcal{D}_{X/k,0}^{(N-1)})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{T}_{X/k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\omega_{X}^{\vee}

(cf. (
436
) for the definition of
\mathcal{D}_{X/k,(-)}^{(N-1)}
).

(i)

First, let us consider the case where
(G,H)=(\mathrm{PGL}_{n+1},\mathrm{PGL}_{n+1}^{\circledcirc})
(cf. Example
2.3.3
).
Suppose further that
p\nmid(n+1)
and
X
admits an
F^{N}
-theta characteristic (cf.
[
53
, § 3, Definition 3.5.1]
), i.e., a pair
(\varTheta,\nabla^{0})
consisting of a line bundle
\varTheta
on
X
and a
\mathcal{D}_{X/k}^{(N-1)}
-module structure
\nabla^{0}
on
\omega_{X}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\varTheta^{\otimes(n+1)}
with vanishing
p
-
(N-1)
-curvature (cf. (
470
)).
According to
[
53
, § 3, Theorem 3.6.4, (i)]
, each element of
\mathrm{Ind}_{(\mathrm{PGL}_{n+1},\mathrm{PGL}_{n+1}^{\circledcirc}),X/k,N}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
may be expressed as a
\mathcal{D}_{X/k}^{(N-1)}
-module structure

(247)

\displaystyle\nabla:\mathcal{D}_{X/k}^{(N-1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\varTheta)\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\varTheta

on the rank
(n+1)
vector bundle
\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\varTheta

with vanishing
p
-
(N-1)
-curvature satisfying the following conditions:

(a)

The following composite defines an automorphism of
\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\varTheta
(cf.
[
53
, § 2, Proposition 2.3.3]
):

(248)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\varTheta\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{X/k}^{(N-1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\varTheta)\xrightarrow{\nabla}\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\varTheta,

where the first arrow arises from the natural inclusions
\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{X/k}^{(N-1)}
and
\varTheta\left(=\mathcal{D}_{X/k,0}^{(N-1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\varTheta\right)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\varTheta
.

(b)

The determinant of
\nabla
corresponds to
\nabla^{0}
via the composite isomorphism

(249)

\displaystyle\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\varTheta)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\varTheta^{\otimes(n+1)}\xrightarrow{(\ref{Ewq1})\otimes\mathrm{id}}\omega_{X}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\varTheta^{\otimes(n+1)}.

If
X
is a curve, then we can take
\varTheta
as a theta characteristic in the usual sense and
\nabla^{0}
as the trivial connection
\nabla_{0}\left(=d\right)
under a fixed identification
\omega_{X}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\varTheta^{\otimes 2}=\mathcal{O}_{X}
.
In this situation,
a connection
\nabla
as above is nothing but what we call a
dormant
\mathrm{SL}_{2}
-oper
.

(ii)

Next, let us recall the case of
(G,H)=(\mathrm{Aff}_{n},\mathrm{Aff}_{n}^{\circledcirc})
.
According to
[
53
, § 3, Theorem 3.6.4, (i)]
, each element of
\mathrm{Ind}_{(\mathrm{Aff}_{n},\mathrm{Aff}_{n}^{\circledcirc}),X/k,N}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
may be expressed as a pair

(250)

\displaystyle(\nabla,\delta)

consisting of a
\mathcal{D}_{X/k}^{(N-1)}
-module structure
\nabla
on the vector bundle
\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)}
with vanishing
p
-
(N-1)
-curvature and a left inverse morphism
\delta:\mathcal{D}_{X/k}^{(N-1)}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}
of the natural inclusion
\mathcal{O}_{X}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)}

satisfying the following conditions:

(c)

The following composite defines an automorphism of
\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)}
:

(251)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)}\left(=\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X}\right)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{X/k}^{(N-1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)}\xrightarrow{\nabla}\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)},

where the first arrow arises from the natural inclusions

\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{X/k}^{(N-1)}
and
\mathcal{O}_{X}\left(=\mathcal{D}_{X/k,0}^{(N-1)}\right)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)}
.

(d)

The subbundle
\mathrm{Ker}(\delta)
of
\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)}
is closed under the
\mathcal{D}_{X/k}^{(N-1)}
-action
\nabla
and
the
\mathcal{D}_{X/k}^{(N-1)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{O}_{X}
induced from
\nabla
via
\delta
coincides with the trivial one.

Now, suppose that
p\nmid(n+1)
and there exists an
F^{N}
-theta characteristic
(\varTheta,\nabla^{0})
on
X
.
Let us take a pair
(\nabla,\delta)
as above.
Since
\mathrm{det}((\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)})^{\vee})\cong\omega_{X}
(cf. (
246
)), we have the
\mathcal{D}_{X/k}^{(N-1)}
-module structure
\nabla_{\omega_{X}}
on
\omega_{X}
induced by (the determinant of the dual of)
\nabla
.
Then, it follows from

[
53
, § 3, Lemma 3.6.3]
that there exists uniquely
a
\mathcal{D}_{X/k}^{(N-1)}
-module structure
\nabla_{\varTheta}
on
\varTheta

such that its
(n+1)
-st tensor product coincides, via
\varTheta^{\otimes(n+1)}\cong\omega_{X}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\omega_{X}^{\vee}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\varTheta^{\otimes(n+1)})
, with the tensor product of
\nabla_{\omega_{X}}
and
\nabla^{0}
(cf.
[
53
, § 2, (70)]
).
Then, the tensor product of
\nabla
and
\nabla_{\varTheta}
defines a
\mathcal{D}_{X/k}^{(N-1)}
-module structure
\nabla^{\prime}
on the vector bundle
\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\varTheta
, satisfying the two conditions (a) and (b) in (i).
One may verify that the resulting assignment
\nabla\mapsto\nabla^{\prime}
coincides with
(
52
) under the identification
F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(G,H),X/S}=\mathrm{Ind}_{(G,H),X/S,N}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
given by the bijection
\zeta_{N}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}:=\zeta_{N}^{\clubsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}\circ\zeta_{N}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\clubsuit}
.

Let us keep the assumption in §
7.4

and take
an
F^{N}
-
(G,P)
-structure
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
on
X/S
with
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.
Then, we obtain the indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle
\mathcal{E}^{\heartsuit}_{N}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})
of level
N
corresponding to
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
via
\zeta_{N}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}
.
Then, the
(N-1)
-PD stratified
G
-bundle
(\mathcal{E}_{G},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})
, where
\mathcal{E}_{G}:=\mathcal{E}_{H}\times^{H}G
, detemines an
(N-1)
-crystal of
G
-bundles
\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}
on
X/S
(cf. Proposition
7.6.2
).

Definition 4.5.7
.

We shall refer to
\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}
as the
monodromy crystal
associated with
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
.

4.6.
Basic properties on
F^{\infty}
-
(G,P)
-structures

By using the result of Theorem
B
, we prove several properties concerning smooth varieties admitting an
F^{\infty}
-
(G,P)
-structure.

Recall that a
rational curve
in a
k
-scheme
X
is a non-constant
k
-morphism
f:\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow X
.
We shall say that a rational curve
f:\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow X
is

free
if
f^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{X})
is spanned by its global sections.
Also, a
k
-scheme is called
convex
if every rational curve in it is free.

Proposition 4.6.1
.

Suppose that
G
is affine.
Then,
any proper smooth
k
-scheme admitting an
F^{\infty}
-
(G,P)
-structure is convex.

Proof.

Let
X
be a proper smooth
k
-scheme admitting an
F^{\infty}
-
(G,P)
-structure
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
.
Denote by
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})
the indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle of level
\infty
corresponding to
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
via
\zeta_{\infty}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}
asserted in Theorem
B
and by

\mathcal{G}_{\infty}:=\{(\mathcal{G}_{l},\varpi_{l})\}_{l}
the
F
-divided
G
-bundle (cf. Definition
7.7.4
) corresponding to the
\infty
-PD stratified
G
-bundle
(\mathcal{E}_{G},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})
via (
478
).
Now, let us take an arbitrary rational curve
f:\mathbb{P}^{1}\rightarrow X
in
X
.
By pulling-back
\mathcal{G}_{\infty}
by
f
, we obtain an
F
-divided
G
-bundle
f^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\infty})
on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
.
Recall from
[
18
, § 2, Theorem 2.2]
that there are no nontrivial
F
-divided vector bundles on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
.
Hence, for each finite-dimensional representation
V
of
G
over
k
,
the associated
F
-divided vector bundle
f^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\infty})\times^{G}V
may be trivialized.
By
[
6
, § 3, Lemma 3.5, (ii)]
,
we see that the
F
-divided
G
-bundle
f^{*}(\mathcal{G}_{\infty})
itself may be trivialized.
Let us fix an isomorphism
f^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{G})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{P}^{1}\times G
preserving the
F
-divided structures, where
\mathbb{P}^{1}\times G
is equipped with the trivial
F
-divided structure.
It gives
an
H
-equivariant composite

(252)

\displaystyle f^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{H})\hookrightarrow f^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{G})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{P}^{1}\times G\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}G.

This morphism induces, via taking the quotients with respect to the
H
-actions, a morphism
h:\mathbb{P}^{1}\left(:=f^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{H})/H\right)\rightarrow P\left(:=G/H\right)
.
Since the commutative diagram

\textstyle{f^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{H})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\ref{Ef01})}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{projection}}
\textstyle{G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{G}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{P}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{P}

is cartesian, we obtain
an isomorphism of
H
-bundles
f^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{H})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}h^{*}(G)\left(=\mathbb{P}^{1}\times_{h,P,\pi_{G}}G\right)
.
Thus, we have

(257)

\displaystyle f^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{X})

\displaystyle\cong f^{*}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}/\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}})

\displaystyle\cong f^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{H}\times^{H}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{h}))

\displaystyle\cong f^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{H})\times^{H}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{h})

\displaystyle\cong h^{*}(G)\times^{H}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{h})

\displaystyle\cong h^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{H}^{\mathrm{triv}}\times^{H}(\mathfrak{g}/\mathfrak{h}))

\displaystyle\cong h^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{P}),

where the first and last “
\cong
” follow from the Kodaira-Spencer maps
\mathrm{KS}_{(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)}
and
\mathrm{KS}_{(\mathcal{E}_{H}^{\mathrm{triv}},\nabla_{0})}
(cf. Example
4.1.6
) respectively.
Observe here that

\mathcal{T}_{P}
, as well as its pull-back
h^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{P})
,
is spanned by the global sections because
the following composite is surjective:

(258)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{P}\otimes\mathfrak{g}\left(\cong\pi_{G*}(\mathcal{O}_{G}\otimes\mathfrak{g})^{H}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\pi_{G*}(\mathcal{T}_{G})^{H}\xrightarrow{d_{\mathcal{E}_{H}^{\mathrm{triv}}}}\mathcal{T}_{P},

where the first arrow arises from
\omega_{G}^{\triangleright}
.
Hence, by (
257
),
f^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{X})
turns out to be spanned by the global sections, i.e.,

f
is free.
This completes the proof of the assertion.
∎

Next, a connected
k
-scheme
X
is called
simply connected
if the étale fundamental group
of
X
is trivial.
Then, we obtain the following assertion, generalizing
[
53
, Theorem C, (ii)]
.

Proposition 4.6.2
.

Let
X
be a simply connected, projective, and smooth scheme over
k
.
Suppose that
G
is affine.
Then, the following assertions hold:

(i)

Suppose that
X/k
admits an
F^{\infty}
-
(G,P)
-structure.
Then, there exists a finite étale covering
X\rightarrow P
over
k
.
In particular, if, moreover,
P
is simply connected,
then
X
is isomorphic to
P
.

(ii)

Suppose that
P
is affine
and not isomorphic to
\mathrm{Spec}(k)
.
Then, there are no
F^{\infty}
-
(G,P)
-structures on
X/k
.

Proof.

We shall consider the former assertion of (i).
Let
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}:=\{\mathcal{S}_{N}^{\heartsuit}\}_{N}
be an
F^{\infty}
-
(G,P)
-structure on
X/k
.
Denote by
\mathcal{G}_{\infty}:=\{(\mathcal{G}_{l},\varpi_{l})\}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
the
F
-divided
G
-bundle induced by
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
, i.e., the underlying
F
-divided
G
-bundle of the
F^{\infty}
-Cartan geometry corresponding to
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
.
Once one chooses a morphism of algebraic
k
-groups
G\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}_{n}
(
n\geq 1
),

\mathcal{G}_{\infty}
induces
an
F
-divided vector bundle
\mathcal{V}_{\infty}
via change of structure group by this morphism.
But, according to
[
14
, Theorem 1.1]
, the simple connectedness of
X
implies that there are no nontrivial
F
-divided vector bundles on
X
, so
\mathcal{V}_{\infty}
must be trivial.
Hence, by
[
6
, § 3.4, Lemma 3.5, (ii)]
,
the
G
-bundle
\mathcal{G}_{1}
is trivial, or equivalently,
there exists
a global section of
\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\heartsuit}
.
(Note that the result in
loc. cit.
is asserted under the assumption that
X
is separably rationally connected.
But, this assumption was not used in its proof.)
This global section specifies an étale
k
-morphism
f:X\rightarrow P
; it is finite since

X
is projective.
This completes the proof of the former assertion (i).
Moreover, both the latter assertion of (i) and assertion (ii) follow immediately from this fact.
∎

4.7.
F^{N}
-
(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\times r_{m}}\times\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\times r_{a}},\{e\})
-structures

This subsection is devoted to studying
the case where
G
is the product of finite copies of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
and
\mathbb{G}_{a}
(and
H=\{e\}
).

4.7.1.

Let
N
be an element of
\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.
First, we discuss
(N-1)
-PD stratified
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-bundles equipped with an
\{e\}
-reduction.
Denote by
C
the Cartier operator
C:F_{X/S*}(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{0}^{(2)}))\rightarrow\Omega_{X^{(1)}/S}
(cf. (
408
) for the definition of
(-)^{(2)}
).
Also, denote by

(259)

\displaystyle\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X/S})^{C-1}

the submodule of
\Gamma(X,\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{0}^{(2)}))\left(\subseteq\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X/S})\right)
consisting of closed
1
-forms
\omega
with
C(\omega)=F^{*}_{S}(\omega)\left(:=(\mathrm{id}_{X}\times F_{S})^{*}(\omega)\right)
.
Recall from
[
28
, § 7, Corollary 7.1.3]
that, for a
1
-form
\omega
on
X/S
, the
S
-connection
\nabla_{\omega}:=d+\omega
is
p
-flat if and only if
\omega\in\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X/S})^{C-1}
.

Now, let us consider a triple

(260)

\displaystyle\omega^{+}:=(\omega,\mathcal{L},\eta),

where
\omega
denotes a
1
-form on
X/S
,
\mathcal{L}
denotes a line bundle on
X^{(N)}
, and
\eta
denotes an isomorphism of flat line bundles
(\mathcal{O}_{X},\nabla_{\omega})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(F^{(N)}_{X/S}(\mathcal{L}),\nabla^{\mathrm{can}(N)}_{\mathcal{L}})
(cf. (
472
)).
The
p
-flatness of
\nabla_{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{can}(N)}
implies that of
\nabla_{\omega}
.
Hence, the above discussion shows that
\omega
automatically belongs to
\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X/S})^{C-1}
.
Given two such triples
\omega^{+}_{i}:=(\omega_{i},\mathcal{L}_{i},\eta_{i})
(
i=1,2
),
we define an isomorphism
\omega_{1}^{+}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\omega_{2}^{+}
to be an isomorphism
\iota:\mathcal{L}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{L}_{2}
with
F^{(N)}_{X/S}(\iota)\circ\eta_{1}=\eta_{2}
.
Since the morphism

\mathcal{O}_{X^{(N)}}^{\times}\rightarrow F_{X/S*}^{(N)}(\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\times})
induced by
F_{X/S}^{(N)}
is injective,
an isomorphism between
\omega_{1}^{+}
and
\omega_{2}^{+}
is, if it exists, uniquely determined.
We shall denote by

(261)

\displaystyle B_{m,X/S}^{(N)}

the set consisting of isomorphism classes of triples
(\omega,\mathcal{L},\eta)
as above.
By Proposition
7.7.3
, (i), and the last comment in Example
4.1.3
, (i), the set
B_{m,X/S}^{(N)}

is naturally identified with the set of isomorphism classes of
(N-1)
-PD stratified
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-bundles on
X/S
with vanishing
p
-
(N-1)
-curvature whose underlying
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-bundle is trivial.
If
N=1
, then
we obtain the canonical bijection

(262)

\displaystyle B_{m,X/S}^{(1)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{Coker}\left(\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\times})\xrightarrow{u\mapsto d\mathrm{log}(u)}\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X/S})^{C-1}\right)

given by
(\omega,\mathcal{L},\eta)\mapsto\omega
.

If
N^{\prime}
is another positive integer with
N\leq N^{\prime}
and
\omega^{+}:=(\omega,\mathcal{L},\eta)\in B_{m,X/S}^{(N^{\prime})}
, then the
N
-th truncation of
\omega^{+}
is defined as the element

(263)

\displaystyle\omega^{+}|^{\langle N\rangle}:=(\omega,F^{(N^{\prime}-N)*}_{X^{(N)}/S}(\mathcal{L}),\eta)

of
B_{m,X/S}^{(N)}
.
Truncations to lower levels gives rise to a projective system

(264)

\displaystyle\cdots\rightarrow B_{m,X/S}^{(N)}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow B_{m,X/S}^{(2)}\rightarrow B_{m,X/S}^{(1)}.

4.7.2.

Next,
we shall consider
(N-1)
-PD stratified
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-bundles equipped with an
\{e\}
-reduction.
Recall from the discussion in Example
4.1.3
that
\mathbb{G}_{a}
is identified with the subgroup of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}
consisting of matrices of the form

\begin{pmatrix}1&a\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}
for
a\in\mathbb{G}_{a}
.
Denote by

(265)

\displaystyle\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X/S})^{C}

the submodule of
\Gamma(X,\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{0}^{(2)}))\left(\subseteq\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X/S})\right)
consisting of closed
1
-forms
\chi
with
C(\chi)=0
.
Here, let us observe the following assertion.

Lemma 4.7.1
.

Let
\chi
be a closed
1
-form on
X/S
, i.e., an element of
\Gamma(X,\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{0}^{(2)}))
.
Then, the
p
-curvature
{{}^{p}}\psi_{\nabla_{\chi}^{\dagger}}
of
\nabla^{\dagger}_{\chi}
(cf. (
201
)) is given by

(266)

\displaystyle{{}^{p}}\psi_{\nabla^{\dagger}_{\chi}}(F_{X}^{-1}(\partial))=-\begin{pmatrix}0&F_{X/S}^{-1}(\langle F_{S}^{*}(\partial),C(\chi)\rangle)\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}

for any local section
\partial\in\mathcal{T}_{X/S}
.
In particular,
\nabla^{\dagger}_{\chi}
has vanishing
p
-curvature if and only if
\chi\in\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X/S})^{C}
.

Proof.

Let us take a local section
\partial\in\mathcal{T}_{X/S}
.
Then, we have the following sequence of equalities:

(267)

\displaystyle{{}^{p}}\psi_{\nabla^{\dagger}_{\chi}}(F_{X}^{-1}(\partial))

\displaystyle=\nabla(\partial)^{p}-\nabla(\partial^{p})

\displaystyle=\left(\partial^{\oplus 2}+\begin{pmatrix}0&\langle\partial,\chi\rangle\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\right)^{p}-(\partial^{p})^{\oplus 2}-\begin{pmatrix}0&\langle\partial^{p},\chi\rangle\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\left((\partial^{p})^{\oplus 2}+\begin{pmatrix}0&\partial^{p-1}(\langle\partial,\chi\rangle)\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\right)-(\partial^{p})^{\oplus 2}-\begin{pmatrix}0&\langle\partial^{p},\chi\rangle\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}0&\partial^{p-1}(\langle\partial,\chi\rangle)-\langle\partial^{p},\chi\rangle\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=-\begin{pmatrix}0&F_{X/S}^{-1}(\langle F_{S}^{*}(\partial),C(\chi)\rangle)\\
0&0\end{pmatrix},

where the third equality follows from Jacobson’s formula and the last equality follows from
[
28
, § 7, (7.1.2.6)]
.
This completes the proof of this lemma.
∎

Now, we shall consider a triple

(268)

\displaystyle\chi^{+}:=(\chi,\mathcal{V},\lambda)

consisting of
a
1
-form
\chi
on
X/S
, a rank
2
vector bundle
\mathcal{V}
on
X^{(N)}
equipped with a structure of extension
0\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X^{(N)}}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X^{(N)}}\rightarrow 0
, and an isomorphism of flat bundles

\lambda:(\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus 2},\nabla_{\chi}^{\dagger})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(F_{X/S}^{(N)*}(\mathcal{V}),\nabla^{\mathrm{can}(N)}_{\mathcal{V}})
preserving the extension structures.
The
p
-flatness of
\nabla_{\mathcal{V}}^{\mathrm{can}(N)}
implies that of
\nabla_{\omega}^{\dagger}
, so the above lemma shows that
\chi
belongs to
\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X/S})^{C}
.
An isomorphism between such triples can be defined naturally and is, if it exists, uniquely determined for the same reason as the case of
(G,H)=(\mathbb{G}_{m},\{e\})
.
Denote by

(269)

\displaystyle B_{a,X/S}^{(N)}

the set of isomorphism classes of triples
(\chi,\mathcal{V},\lambda)
as above.
By Proposition
7.7.3
, (i), and the last comment in Example
4.1.3
, (ii), this set is naturally identified with the set of isomorphism classes of
(N-1)
-PD stratified
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-bundles on
X/S
with vanishing
p
-
(N-1)
-curvature whose underlying
\mathbb{G}_{a}
-bundle is trivial.
If
N=1
, then we obtain the canonical bijection

(270)

\displaystyle B_{a,X/S}^{(1)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{Coker}\left(\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})\xrightarrow{u\mapsto du}\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X/S})\right)

given by
(\chi,\mathcal{V},\lambda)\mapsto\chi
.

Just as in the case of
B_{m,X/S}^{(N)}
,
the
N
-th truncation
\chi^{+}|^{\langle N\rangle}
of
\chi^{+}\in B_{a,X/S}^{(N^{\prime})}
with
N\leq N^{\prime}
can be defined.
In particular, truncations to various lower levels yield a projective system consisting of
B_{a,X/S}^{(N)}
’s.

4.7.3.

Finally, we combine the above discussions to study
(N-1)
-PD stratified bundles whose structure group is the product of finite copies of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
and
\mathbb{G}_{a}
.
Let
s
and
t
be nonnegative integers with
s+t=n\left(:=\mathrm{dim}(X/S)\right)
.
Write
G:=\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\times s}\times\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\times t}
.
The product of sets
(B_{m,X/S}^{(N)})^{\times s}\times(B_{a,X/S}^{(N)})^{\times t}
is in bijection with
the set of isomorphism classes of
(N-1)
-PD stratified
G
-bundles with vanishing
p
-
(N-1)
-curvature equipped with an
\{e\}
-reduction (i.e., whose underlying
G
-bundles are trivial).
Denote by

(271)

\displaystyle B_{X/S}^{(N),s,t}

the elements of

(B_{m,X/S}^{(N)})^{\times s}\times(B_{a,X/S}^{(N)})^{\times t}

represented by collections

(\omega^{+}_{1},\cdots,\omega_{s}^{+},\chi_{1}^{+},\cdots,\chi_{t}^{+})
, where
\omega_{i}^{+}:=(\omega_{i},\mathcal{L}_{i},\eta_{i})
and
\chi_{j}^{+}:=(\chi_{j},\mathcal{V}_{j},\lambda_{j})
(
i=1,\cdots,s
and
j=1,\cdots,t
),
such that the global sections
\omega_{1},\cdots,\omega_{s},\chi_{1},\cdots,\chi_{t}
forms a basis of the
\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
-module
\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X/S})
.
Truncations to lower levels defined before give rise to a projective system
consisting of
B_{X/S}^{(N),s,t}
’s for various
N
.
Hence, we obtain the set

(272)

\displaystyle B_{X/S}^{(\infty),s,t}:=\varprojlim_{N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}B_{X/S}^{(N),s,t}.

Proposition 4.7.2
.

Let us keep the above notation.

(i)

For any
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\sqcup\{\infty\}
, there exists a canonical bijection of sets

(273)

\displaystyle F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(G,G),X/S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}B_{X/S}^{(N),s,t}.

In particular,

X/S
admits no
F^{N}
-
(G,G)
-structures
unless
\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X/S})\cong\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})^{\oplus n}
.

(ii)

The following two conditions are equivalent to each other:

(a)

There exists an
F^{1}
-
(G,G)
-structure on
X/S
;

(b)

There exists a basis
\{\omega_{1},\cdots,\omega_{s},\chi_{1},\cdots,\chi_{t}\}
of
\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X/S})
such that
each
\omega_{i}
(
i=1,\cdots,s
) belongs to
\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X/S})^{C-1}

and each
\chi_{j}
(
j=1,\cdots,t
) belongs to
\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X/S})^{C}
.

Moreover, if
S=\mathrm{Spec}(k)
and
X
is proper over
k
, then these conditions are equivalent to the following condition:

(c)

The following equalities hold:

(274)

\displaystyle n=\mathrm{dim}_{k}(\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X/k})),\hskip 8.53581pts=\mathrm{dim}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(H^{1}_{\mathrm{fl}}(X,\mu_{p})),\hskip 8.53581ptt=\mathrm{dim}_{k}(H^{1}_{\mathrm{fl}}(X,\alpha_{p})),

where
H^{1}_{\mathrm{fl}}
denotes the
1
-st flat cohomology.

Proof.

Let us consider assertion (i).
The problem is reduced to the case of
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
because the assertion for
N=\infty
follows immediately from this case together with (
37
) and (
272
).
First, we shall construct a map of sets

F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(G,G),X/S}\rightarrow B_{X/S}^{(N),s,t}
.
Take an
F^{N}
-
(G,G)
-structure
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
on
X/S
, and denote by
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}_{N}

the dormant indigenous
(G,\{e\})
-bundle of level
N

corresponding to
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
(cf. Theorem
B
).
As mentioned above, the data
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}_{N}
gives
a collection of data

(275)

\displaystyle(\omega_{1}^{+},\cdots,\omega_{s}^{+},\chi_{1}^{+},\cdots,\chi^{+}_{t}),

where each
\omega^{+}_{i}:=(\omega_{i},\mathcal{L}_{i},\eta_{i})

(
i=1,\cdots,s
) denotes an element of
B_{m,X/S}^{(N)}
and each
\chi^{+}_{j}:=(\chi_{j},\mathcal{V}_{j},\lambda_{j})
(
j=1,\cdots,t
) denotes an element of
B_{a,X/S}^{(N)}
.
Under the natural identifications
\mathrm{Lie}(G)\cong\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\oplus s}\oplus\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbb{G}_{a})^{\oplus t}\cong k^{\oplus(s+t)}
,
the vector bundle
\mathrm{Lie}(G)_{X\times G}/\mathrm{Lie}(\{e\})_{X\times\{e\}}
may be identified with
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus(s+t)}
.
In particular, if
\nabla
denotes the
S
-connection on the trivial
G
-bundle
defining
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}_{N}|^{\langle 1\rangle}
(cf. (
239
)), then
the Kodaira-Spencer map
\mathrm{KS}_{(X\times\{e\},\nabla)}

may be regarded as an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear morphism
\mathcal{T}_{X/S}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus(s+t)}
, or equivalently, an element of
\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X/S})^{\oplus(s+t)}
(cf. Remark
4.1.2
).
By taking account of
the discussions in Example
4.1.3
, (i) and (ii),
we see that

\mathrm{KS}_{(X\times\{e\},\nabla)}
coincides with
(\omega_{1},\cdots,\omega_{s},\chi_{1},\cdots,\chi_{t})
.
Since

\mathrm{KS}_{(X\times\{e\},\nabla)}
is an isomorphism,
the global sections
\omega_{1},\cdots,\omega_{s},\chi_{1},\cdots,\chi_{t}

form a basis of
\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X/S})
.
Thus, the collection of data
(
275
)
specifies an element of
B_{X/S}^{(N),s,t}
.
The resulting assignment
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}\mapsto(\omega_{1}^{+},\cdots,\omega_{s}^{+},\chi_{1}^{+},\cdots,\chi^{+}_{t})
defines a map of sets

F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(G,G),X/S}\rightarrow B_{X/S}^{(N),s,t}
.
Moreover, by the above discussion,
this map is verified to be bijective, so this completes the proof of assertion (i).

Next, let us consider assertion (ii).
The equivalence (a)
\Leftrightarrow
(b)
follows immediately from assertion (i) together with (
262
) and (
270
).
The equivalence (b)
\Leftrightarrow
(c) under the assumption mentioned in the statement follows from the fact that
\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X/k})^{C-1}
,
\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X/k})^{C}
are naturally isomorphic to the flat cohomology groups
H^{1}_{\mathrm{fl}}(X,\mu_{p})
,
H^{1}_{\mathrm{fl}}(X,\alpha_{p})
respectively (cf.
[
35
, Chap. III, § 4, Proposition 4.14]
).
(Note that the natural
k
-linear morphism
k\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X/k})^{C-1}\rightarrow\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X/k})
is verified to be injective.)
This completes the proof of the assertion.
∎

In the following examples, suppose that
N
is a positive integer.

Example 4.7.3
.

Let us consider
F^{N}
-
(\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathbb{G}_{m})
-structures on the
k
-curve
\mathbb{G}_{m}:=\mathrm{Spec}(k[T^{\pm 1}])
.
For each
u\in\Gamma(\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})\left(=\{a\cdot T^{n}\,|\,n\in\mathbb{Z},a\in k^{\times}\}\right)
,
we shall write
\mathrm{mult}_{u}
for the automorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
given by multiplication by
u
.
The pull-back
\mathrm{mult}_{u}^{*}(\nabla_{0})
of the trivial connection
\nabla_{0}
on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}
via
\mathrm{mult}_{u}
is the
k
-connection expressed as

\mathrm{mult}_{u}^{*}(\nabla_{0})=\nabla_{d\mathrm{log}(u)}
, where
d\mathrm{log}(u):=u^{-1}\cdot du
.
Hence,
since any line bundle on
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{(N)}
is trivial,
each element of
B_{m,\mathbb{G}_{m}/k}^{(N)}
may be represented by

(d\mathrm{log}(u),\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{(N)}},\mathrm{mult}_{u})
for some
u\in\Gamma(\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})
.
Such a triple belongs to

B_{\mathbb{G}_{m}/k}^{(N),1,0}\left(\subseteq B_{m,\mathbb{G}_{m}/k}^{(N)}\right)

if and only if
d\mathrm{log}(u)
is nowhere vanishing, i.e.,

u\in\{a\cdot T^{n}\,|\,n\in\mathbb{Z},(p,n)=1,a\in k^{\times}\}
.
Also, an isomorphism from
(d\mathrm{log}(u),\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{(N)}},\mathrm{mult}_{u})
to
(d\mathrm{log}(v),\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{(N)}},\mathrm{mult}_{v})
is given by the automorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{(N)}}

defined as multiplication by an element

w\in\Gamma(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{(N)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{(N)}}^{\times})

\left(=\{a\cdot T^{n\cdot p^{N}}\,|\,n\in\mathbb{Z},a\in k^{\times}\}\right)
satisfying
u=w\cdot v
.
This implies that each isomorphism class in
B_{\mathbb{G}_{m}/k}^{(N),1,0}

may be represented by a triple
(d\mathrm{log}(u),\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{(N)}},\mathrm{mult}_{u})
for
u=T^{m}
with
1\leq m\leq p^{N}-1
and
(p,m)=1
.
Consequently,
the assignment
m\mapsto(d\mathrm{log}(T^{m}),\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}^{(N)}},\mathrm{mult}_{T^{m}})
defines a canonical bijection

(276)

\displaystyle\left\{m\in\mathbb{Z}\,|\,1\leq m\leq p^{N}-1,(p,m)=1\right\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}B_{\mathbb{G}_{m}/k}^{(N),1,0}.

In particular,
\mathbb{G}_{m}/k
admits an
F^{N}
-
(\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathbb{G}_{m})
-structure for any
N
.

Example 4.7.4
.

Next, we shall consider
F^{N}
-
(\mathbb{G}_{a},\mathbb{G}_{a})
-structures on the affine line
\mathbb{A}^{1}:=\mathrm{Spec}(k[T])
.
For any
u\in\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{1},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}})
, we shall write
\mathrm{add}_{u}

for the automorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus 2}
given by multiplication by

\begin{pmatrix}1&u\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}
.
The pull-back
\mathrm{add}^{*}_{u}(\nabla_{0})
of the trivial connection
\nabla_{0}
on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
via
\mathrm{add}_{u}
is the
k
-connection expressed as

\mathrm{add}_{u}^{*}(\nabla_{0})=\nabla_{du}^{\dagger}
.
Hence, since any rank
2
vector bundle on
\mathbb{A}^{1(N)}
is trivial, each element of
B_{a,\mathbb{A}^{1}/k}^{(N)}
may be represented by

(du,\mathcal{O}^{\oplus 2}_{\mathbb{A}^{1(N)}},\mathrm{add}_{u})
for
u\in\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{1},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}})
.
Such a triple
belongs to
B_{\mathbb{A}^{1}/k}^{(N),0,1}\left(\subseteq B_{a,\mathbb{A}^{1}/k}^{(N)}\right)

if and only if
du
is nowhere vanishing, i.e.,
u\in\left\{a\cdot T+f(T)\,|\,a\in k^{\times},f(T)\in k[T^{p}]\right\}
.
Also, an isomorphism from
(du,\mathcal{O}^{\oplus 2}_{\mathbb{A}^{1(N)}},\mathrm{add}_{u})
to
(dv,\mathcal{O}^{\oplus 2}_{\mathbb{A}^{1(N)}},\mathrm{add}_{v})

is given by the automorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1(N)}}^{\oplus 2}

defined as multiplication by
\begin{pmatrix}1&w\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}

for some
w\in\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{1(N)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1(N)}})\left(=k[T^{p^{N}}]\right)
satisfying

u=v+w
.
This implies that each isomorphism class in
B_{\mathbb{A}^{1}/k}^{(N),0,1}

may be represented by a triple
(du,\mathcal{O}^{\oplus 2}_{\mathbb{A}^{1(N)}},\mathrm{add}_{u})
for

u=a\cdot T+\sum_{i\geq 0}a_{i}\cdot T^{i}\in k[T]
such that
a\in k^{\times}
and

a_{i}\neq 0
only if
i\in p\mathbb{Z}\setminus p^{N}\mathbb{Z}
.
Consequently, the assignment
u\mapsto(du,\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1(N)}},\mathrm{add}_{u})
defines a canonical bijection

(277)

\displaystyle\left\{a\cdot T+\sum_{i\geq 0}a_{i}\cdot T^{i}\in k[T]\,\Bigg|\,a\in k^{\times}\ \text{and}\ a_{i}\neq 0\ \text{only if}\ i\in p\mathbb{Z}\setminus p^{N}\mathbb{Z}\right\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}B_{\mathbb{A}^{1}/k}^{(N),0,1}.

In particular, the affine line
\mathbb{A}^{1}/k
admits an
F^{N}
-
(\mathbb{G}_{a},\mathbb{G}_{a})
-structure for any
N
.

4.8.
F^{N}
-
(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\times s}\times\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\times t},\{e\})
-structures on Abelian varieties

Let
X
be an Abelian variety over
k
of dimension
n
.
For each positive integer
l
, denote by
l_{X}
the endomorphism of
X
given by multiplication by
l
.
Let
\widehat{X}
denote the dual Abelian variety of
X
.
Recall that the
p
-rank
of
X
is the nonnegative integer
\mu(X):=\mathrm{dim}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(\mathrm{Hom}(\mu_{p},X))\left(=\mathrm{dim}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(\mathrm{Hom}(\mu_{p},\mathrm{Ker}(p_{X})))\right)
, which coincides with the value
\mathrm{dim}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z},X)
.
Also,
the
a
-number
of
X
is defined as
\alpha(X):=\mathrm{dim}_{k}(\mathrm{Hom}(\alpha_{p},X))\left(=\mathrm{dim}_{k}(\mathrm{Hom}(\alpha_{p},\mathrm{Ker}(p_{X})))\right)
.
These values satisfy the inequalities
0\leq\mu(X)\leq g
and
1\leq\alpha(X)+\mu(X)\leq g
.

Regarding
F^{N}
-
(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\times s}\times\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\times t},\{e\})
-structures for
N=1
, Proposition
4.7.2
implies the following assertion.

Proposition 4.8.1
.

Let us keep the above notation.
Also,
let
(s,t)
be a pair of nonnegative integers with
n=s+t
.
Then,
the Abelian variety
X
admits an
F^{1}
-
(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\times s}\times\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\times t},\{e\})
-structure if and only if
s=\mu(\widehat{X})
and
t=\alpha(\widehat{X})
.

Proof.

Observe that

(278)

\displaystyle\mathrm{dim}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(H^{1}_{\mathrm{fl}}(X,\mu_{p}))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{dim}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(\mathrm{Hom}(\varprojlim_{l}\mathrm{Ker}(l_{X}),\mu_{p}))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{dim}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(\mathrm{Hom}(\mathrm{Ker}(p_{X}),\mu_{p}))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{dim}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(\mathrm{Hom}(\widehat{\mu_{p}},\widehat{\mathrm{Ker}(p_{X})}))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{dim}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z},\mathrm{Ker}(p_{\widehat{X}}))

\displaystyle=\mu(\widehat{X}),

where
\widehat{(-)}
denotes Cartier dual and the first equality follows from
[
35
, Chap. III, § 4, Corollary 4.19, (a)]
.
By a similar discussion together with
\widehat{\alpha_{p}}=\alpha_{p}
, we have
\mathrm{dim}_{k}(H^{1}_{\mathrm{fl}}(X,\alpha_{p}))=\alpha(\widehat{X})
.
Thus,
the assertion follows from the equivalence (a)
\Leftrightarrow
(c) asserted in Proposition
4.7.2
, (ii).
∎

Recall that the Abelian variety
X
is called
ordinary
(resp.,
superspecial
) if
\mu(X)=g
(resp.,
\alpha(X)=g
).
It is verified that
X
is ordinary (resp., superspecial) if and only if the dual
\widehat{X}
is ordinary (resp., superspecial).
The following assertion concerns the cases where
G=\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\times n}
and
G=\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\times n}
.

Proposition 4.8.2
.

(i)

Let
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\sqcup\{\infty\}
.
Then,

X
admits an
F^{N}
-
(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\times n},\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\times n})
-structure
if and only if
X
is ordinary.
Moreover, when
X
is ordinary,
the map of sets

(279)

\displaystyle F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\times n},\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\times n}),X/k}/\mathfrak{S}_{n}\rightarrow F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(\mathrm{Aff}_{n},\mathbb{A}^{n}),X/k}

defined in Example
2.5.2
is injective, and moreover bijective if
N=\infty
.

(ii)

There exists an
F^{1}
-
(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\times n},\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\times n})
-structure on
X/k

if and only if
X
is superspecial.
Moreover, for any
N\geq 2
, there are no
F^{N}
-
(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\times n},\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\times n})
-structures on any Abelian variety.

Proof.

Let us consider the former assertion of (i).
The “only if” part of the required equivalence is a direct consequence of Proposition
4.8.1
for
(s,t)=(n,0)
together with the equivalence “
X
is ordinary”
\Leftrightarrow
“
\widehat{X}
is ordinary”.
To prove the “if” part,
let us
suppose that
X
, or equivalently,
\widehat{X}
is ordinary.
Also, for a positive integer
N
, suppose that
we are given an
F^{N}
-
(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\times n},\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\times n})
-structure on
X/k
; it specifies an element
(\omega_{1}^{+},\cdots,\omega_{n}^{+})
of

B_{X/k}^{(N),n,0}
, where
\omega_{i}^{+}:=(\omega_{i},\mathcal{L}_{i},\eta_{i})
(
i=1,\cdots,n
), via (
273
).
Since the map
\widehat{X^{(N+1)}}(k)\rightarrow\widehat{X^{(N)}}(k)

arising from pull-back by
F_{X^{(N)}/k}:X^{(N)}\rightarrow X^{(N+1)}
is surjective,
there exists a collection of line bundles
\mathcal{L}_{1}^{\nabla},\cdots,\mathcal{L}_{n}^{\nabla}
on
X^{(N+1)}
with
F^{*}_{X^{(N)}/k}(\mathcal{L}_{i}^{\nabla})\cong\mathcal{L}_{i}
(
i=1,\cdots,n
).
The collection

(\widetilde{\omega}_{1}^{+},\cdots,\widetilde{\omega}_{n}^{+})
consisting of
triples
\widetilde{\omega}_{i}^{+}:=(\omega_{i},\mathcal{L}_{i}^{\nabla},\eta_{i})
(
i=1,\cdots,n
)
defines an element of
B_{X/k}^{(N+1),n,0}
whose
N
-th truncation coincides with
(\omega_{1}^{+},\cdots,\omega_{n}^{+})
.
This proves the surjectivity of
the truncation map
B_{X/k}^{(N+1),n,0}\rightarrow B_{X/k}^{(N),n,0}
.
Observe here that

B_{X/k}^{(1),n,0}
is nonempty
because of the result of Proposition
4.8.1
and the ordinariness assumption on
X
.
Hence, we can apply the above argument inductively to conclude that
B_{X/k}^{(N),n,0}\neq\emptyset
for any
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\sqcup\{\infty\}
.
That is to say,
X/k
admits an
F^{N}
-
(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\times n},\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\times n})
-structure on
X/k
via (
273
).
This completes the former assertion of (i).

Next, let us consider the latter assertion of (i) under the assumption that
X
is ordinary.
Suppose here that
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, and
let us take an element

(\omega_{1}^{+},\cdots,\omega_{n}^{+})
of
B_{X/k}^{(N),n,0}
, where
\omega_{i}^{+}:=(\omega_{i},\mathcal{L}_{i},\eta_{i})
.
Write
\nabla:=\nabla_{0}\oplus\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\nabla^{\mathrm{can}(N-1)}_{\mathcal{L}_{i}}
, which specifies a
\mathcal{D}_{X/k}^{(N-1)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus(n+1)}
via
\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\oplus\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\eta_{i}
.
Since
\omega_{1},\cdots,\omega_{n}
forms a basis of
\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X})
,
the following composite turns out to be an isomorphism:

(280)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)}\left(=\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X}\right)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{X/k}^{(N-1)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}^{\oplus(n+1)}_{X}\xrightarrow{\nabla}\mathcal{O}^{\oplus(n+1)}_{X},

where the first arrow arises from both the natural inclusion
\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{X/k}^{(N-1)}
and the diagonal embedding
\mathcal{O}_{X}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus(n+1)}
.
Under the identification
\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)}=\mathcal{O}^{\oplus(n+1)}_{X}
given by this isomorphism, we shall regard
\nabla
as a
\mathcal{D}_{X/k}^{(N-1)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N)}
.
Also, we regard the
(n+1)
-st projection
\delta:=\mathrm{pr}_{n+1}:\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus(n+1)}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}
as a left inverse morphism
\mathcal{D}_{X/k}^{(N-1)}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}
of the natural inclusion
\mathcal{O}_{X}\left(:=\mathcal{D}_{X/k,0}^{(N-1)}\right)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{X/k,1}^{(N-1)}
.
By construction, the pair
(\nabla,\delta)
satisfies the conditions (c) and (d) described in Example
4.5.6
.
That is to say,

(\nabla,\delta)
specifies an elmement of
F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(\mathrm{Aff}_{n},\mathbb{A}^{n}),X/k}
.
If
B_{X/k}^{(N),n,0}/\mathfrak{S}_{n}
denotes the quotient set of
B_{X/k}^{(N),n,0}
by the
\mathfrak{S}_{n}
-action determined by permutation of factors, then
the resulting assignment
(\omega_{1}^{+},\cdots,\omega_{n}^{+})\mapsto(\nabla,\delta)
induces a map of sets

\varsigma_{N}:B_{X/k}^{(N),n,0}/\mathfrak{S}_{n}\rightarrow F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(\mathrm{Aff}_{n},\mathbb{A}^{n}),X/k}
.
One may verify that
\varsigma_{N}
coincides with (
279
) under the identification

B_{X/k}^{(N),n,0}/\mathfrak{S}_{n}=F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\times n},\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\times n}),X/k}/\mathfrak{S}_{n}
induced by (
273
).
Then, the required assertion for
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
follows from the injectivity of
\varsigma_{N}
resulting from
[
53
, § 6, Theorem 6.4.1]
.
Moreover, the case of
N=\infty
follows from the bijectivity of
\varprojlim_{N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}\varsigma_{N}
asserted in the same theorem.

Finally, we shall consider assertion (ii).
The former assertion follows directly from Proposition
4.8.1
for
(s,t)=(0,n)

together with the equivalence “
X
is superspecial”
\Leftrightarrow
“
\widehat{X}
is superspecial”.
To prove the latter assertion, it suffices, by the former assertion, to consider the case where
X
is superspecial.
Suppose, on the contrary, that there exists an
F^{N}
-
(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\times n},\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\times n})
-structure
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
on
X/k
for some
N\geq 2
; it specifies, via truncation to level
2
,
an element

(\chi^{+}_{1},\cdots,\chi_{n}^{+})
, where
\chi_{j}^{+}:=(\chi_{j},\mathcal{W}_{j},\lambda_{j})
(
j=1,\cdots,n
),
of
B_{X/k}^{(2),0,n}
via (
273
).
Let us fix
j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}
and write
\mathcal{V}_{j}:=F_{X^{(1)}/k}^{*}(\mathcal{W}_{j})
.
Also, denote by
v
(resp.,
w
) the element of
H^{1}(X^{(1)},\mathcal{O}_{X^{(1)}})
(resp.,
H^{1}(X^{(2)},\mathcal{O}_{X^{(2)}})
) determined by the extension structure of
\mathcal{V}_{j}
induced by that of
\mathcal{W}_{j}
(resp., the extension structure of
\mathcal{W}_{j}
) under the natural identification

H^{1}(X^{(1)},\mathcal{O}_{X^{(1)}})\cong\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{(1)}}}(\mathcal{O}_{X^{(1)}},\mathcal{O}_{X^{(1)}})
(resp.,
H^{1}(X^{(2)},\mathcal{O}_{X^{(2)}})\cong\mathrm{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{(2)}}}(\mathcal{O}_{X^{(2)}},\mathcal{O}_{X^{(2)}})
).
Since the
1
-form
\chi_{j}
is nozero, there are no split injections
\mathcal{O}_{X}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus 2}
of the trivial extension
0\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus 2}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow 0
whose image is closed under
\nabla_{\chi_{j}}^{\dagger}
.
It follows that the extension structure of
\mathcal{V}_{j}
is nontrivial, or equivalently,
v\neq 0
.
On the other hand,
since
X
, as well as
X^{(1)}
, is superspecial,
the morphism
F^{*}_{X^{(1)}/k}:H^{1}(X^{(2)},\mathcal{O}_{X^{(2)}})\rightarrow H^{1}(X^{(1)},\mathcal{O}_{X^{(1)}})
induced by
F_{X^{(1)}/k}
is zero.
This implies
0=F^{*}_{X^{(1)}/k}(w)=v
, so we obtain a contradiction.
This completes the proof of the latter assertion.
∎

5.
Classification for algebraic curves

The purpose of this section is to examine the classification problem of
Frobenius-Ehresmann structures on smooth proper curves.
Recall a result by B. Laurent (cf.
[
32
, Theorem 1.1, (1)]
), asserting that the list of homogenous curves, i.e., homogenous space of dimension
1
, may be described as follows:

Case (a):

G=\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
,
H=\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{\circledcirc}
;

Case (b):

G=\mathrm{Aff}_{1}
,
H=\mathrm{Aff}_{1}^{\circledcirc}
;

Case (c):

G=\mathbb{G}_{m}
,
H=\{e\}
;

Case (d):

G=\mathbb{G}_{a}
,
H=\{e\}
;

Case (e):

G
is an elliptic curve over
k
and
H=\{e\}
.

In what follows,
we determine which curves have Frobenius-Ehresmann structures with a fixed model
(G,H)
according to this classification.
Let
X
be a smooth proper curve over
k
of genus
g\geq 0
.

5.1.
Case (a):

The first case is the pair of the projective linear group
G=\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
and its subgroup
H=\mathrm{PGL}^{\circledcirc}_{2}
.
In several literatures,
F^{N}
-
(\mathrm{PGL}_{2},\mathbb{P}^{1})
-structures on curves have been studied
under the name of
Frobenius-projective structure
(cf.
[
23
]
,
[
53
]
).
If
N=1
, then
the corresponding dormant indigenous
(\mathrm{PGL}_{2},\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{\circledcirc})
-bundles are called

dormant indigenous bundles
or
dormant
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers
(cf.
[
40
]
,
[
51
]
,
[
54
]
).
Moreover, these objects are closely related to second order differential operators having a full set of roots functions (cf.
[
54
, Chap. 4, § 4.10, Proposition 4.65]
).
Here, let us pick up a previous result of
[
53
, Theorem E]
, concerning the existence of
F^{N}
-
(\mathrm{PGL}_{2},\mathbb{P}^{1})
-structures.

Proposition 5.1.1
.

For any
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\sqcup\{\infty\}
,

X
admits an
F^{N}
-
(\mathrm{PGL}_{2},\mathbb{P}^{1})
-structure.
Moreover, if
g>1
,
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, and
\Theta
is a theta characteristic of
X
(i.e., a line bundle on
X
equipped with an isomorphism
\Theta^{\otimes 2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\Omega_{X}
), then there exists a canonical bijection of sets

(281)

\displaystyle F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(\mathrm{PGL}_{2},\mathbb{P}^{1}),X/k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{Q}_{N,\Theta}^{2,\mathrm{triv}}(k),

where
\mathcal{Q}_{N,\Theta}^{2,\mathrm{triv}}
denotes the Quot-scheme over
k
classifying
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(N)}}
-submodules
\mathcal{F}
of
F_{X/k}^{(N)*}(\Theta^{\vee})
with
\mathrm{rank}(\mathcal{F})=2
and
\mathrm{det}(\mathcal{F})\cong\mathcal{O}_{X^{(N)}}
.

There are some results related to the counting problem of
F^{N}
-
(\mathrm{PGL}_{2},\mathbb{P}^{1})
-structures.
S. Mochizuki showed that
there are only finitely many
F^{1}
-
(\mathrm{PGL}_{2},\mathbb{P}^{1})
-structures on
X
(cf.
[
40
, Chap. II, § 2.3, Theorem 2.8]
).
Moreover,
the main result of
[
51
, Theorem A]
explicitly computes the number of such structures by means of the genus “
g
” of
X
and the characteristic “
p
” of the base field
k
.
To be precise, if
X
is sufficiently general in the moduli stack of smooth proper genus
g
curves, then
we have the equality

(282)

\displaystyle F\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(\mathrm{PGL}_{2},\mathbb{P}^{1}),X/k}=\frac{p^{g-1}}{2^{2g-1}}\cdot\sum_{\theta=1}^{p-1}\frac{1}{\sin^{2g-2}\big(\frac{\pi\cdot\theta}{p}\big)}.

This formula is proved by using (
281
) for
N=1
and applying a formula computing the degree of
certain Quot-schemes (i.e., a special case of the Vafa-Intriligator formula).
However, for large
N
’s,
the author does not know much about the finiteness of
F^{N}
-
(\mathrm{PGL}_{2},\mathbb{P}^{1})
-structures at the time of writing the present paper.
So far,
the finiteness of the set
F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(\mathrm{PGL}_{2},\mathbb{P}^{1}),X/k}
with
N\geq 2
has been shown only for genus
2
curves (cf.
[
53
, § 7, Theorem 7.4.2]
).
By taking account of
(
282
) and its proof, we expect that the following conjectural formula will hold
for any large
N
; this conjecture may be thought of as a generalization of Joshi’s conjecture for the rank
2
case (cf.
[
26
, Conjecture 8.1]
).

Conjecture 5.1.2
.

Suppose that
X
is a sufficiently general smooth proper curve of genus
g
over
k
.
Then, for each
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, the cardinality of the set
F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(\mathrm{PGL}_{2},\mathbb{P}^{1}),X/k}
is given by the following formula:

(283)

\displaystyle F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(\mathrm{PGL}_{2},\mathbb{P}^{1}),X/k}=\frac{p^{n(g-1)}}{2^{2g-1}}\cdot\sum_{\theta=1}^{p^{n}-1}\frac{1}{\sin^{2g-2}\big(\frac{\pi\cdot\theta}{p^{n}}\big)}.

5.2.
Case (b):

Next,
we briefly review some results of the case where
G=\mathrm{Aff}_{1}
and
H=\mathrm{Aff}_{1}^{\circledcirc}
.
Recall that
F^{N}
-
(\mathrm{Aff}_{1},\mathrm{Aff}_{1}^{\circledcirc})
-structures on curves have been studied in
[
24
]
and
[
53
]
under the name of
Frobenius-affine structure
.
If
N=1
, then these objects are equivalent to what we call
dormant (generic) Miura
\mathrm{PGL}_{2}
-opers
or
Tango structures
(cf.
[
41
]
,
[
52
]
,
[
55
]
).

The existence of such an object is very restrictive because
if there is a Tango curve (= a curve admitting a Tango structure) of genus
g
, then

p
must divide
g-1
.
M. Raynaud provided an explicit example of a
Tango curve (cf.
[
46
]
or
[
41
, § 1.1, Example 1.3]
).
Also, Tango curves can be constructed by means of solutions to the Bethe ansatz equations for Gaudin model over finite fields (cf.
[
55
]
).
According to
[
52
, Theorem B]
,
the moduli stack
parametrizing Tango curves of genus
g
forms, if it is nonempty,
an equidimensional smooth Deligne-Mumford stack of dimension
2\cdot g-2+\frac{2\cdot g-2}{p}
.
Regarding higher-level cases,
Y. Hoshi proved (cf.
[
25
, Theorem 3]
) that if an integer
g>1
satisfies
p^{N}\mid(g-1)
for a positive integer
N
, then there exists a smooth proper curve of genus
g
admitting an
F^{N}
-
(\mathrm{Aff}_{1},\mathbb{A}^{1})
-structure.
The following two propositions describe some results
proved previously (cf.
[
24
]
,
[
53
, Theorem D and § 7, Propositions 7.5.1, 7.5.2]
).

Proposition 5.2.1
.

(i)

For
N=1
,

X/k
admits an
F^{1}
-
(\mathrm{Aff}_{1},\mathbb{A}^{1})
-structure if and only if

X
is either a genus
1
curve or a Tango curve.

(ii)

For an integer
N
with
N\geq 2
,

X/k
admits an
F^{N}
-
(\mathrm{Aff}_{1},\mathbb{A}^{1})
-structure if and only if

X
is either an ordinary genus
1
curve or a Tango curve with
p^{N}\mid g-1
.

(iii)

For
N=\infty
,

X/k
admits an
F^{\infty}
-
(\mathrm{Aff}_{1},\mathbb{A}^{1})
-structure if and only if

X
is an ordinary genus
1
curve.

Here, for each ring
R
and a free
R
-module
M
of rank
1
, we shall denote by

\mathbb{G}_{m}(R;M)
the set of generators in
M
.

Proposition 5.2.2
.

Suppose that
X
is an ordinary genus
1
curve, and
denote by
T_{p}X
the
p
-adic Tate module of
X
.
Then, there exists a bijection of sets

(284)

\displaystyle F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(\mathrm{Aff}_{1},\mathbb{A}^{1}),X/k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{G}_{m}(R_{N};T_{p}X\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}R_{N}),

where
R_{N}:=\mathbb{Z}/p^{N}\mathbb{Z}
if
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
and
R_{N}:=\mathbb{Z}_{p}
if
N=\infty
.
In particular, if
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, then the set
F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(\mathrm{Aff}_{1},\mathbb{A}^{1}),X/k}
is finite and its cardinality can be given by the following formula:

(285)

\displaystyle\sharp(F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(\mathrm{Aff}_{1},\mathbb{A}^{1}),X/k})=p^{N-1}(p-1).

5.3.
Cases (c) and (d):

The classification problems for the cases (c) and (d) were already finished in the discussion of §
4.8
.
The following assertions are the results for curves obtained there.

Proposition 5.3.1
.

Let

N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\sqcup\{\infty\}
.
Then,
X
admits an
F^{N}
-
(\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathbb{G}_{m})
-structure if and only if
X
is an ordinary genus
1
curve.
If
X
is an ordinary genus
1
curve, then
the map of sets

(286)

\displaystyle F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathbb{G}_{m}),X/k}\rightarrow F^{N}\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(\mathrm{Aff}_{1},\mathbb{A}^{1}),X/k}

defined in Example
2.5.2
is bijective.

Proof.

The assertion follow from
Proposition
4.8.2
, (i), for
n=1
.
Note that the surjectivity of (
286
) for
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
can be verified from the discussion in the proof of Proposition
4.8.2
, (i).
∎

Proposition 5.3.2
.

(i)

There exists an
F^{1}
-
(\mathbb{G}_{a},\mathbb{G}_{a})
-structure on
X/k

if and only if
X
is a supersingular genus
1
curve.
Moreover, for any
N\geq 2
, there are no
F^{N}
-
(\mathbb{G}_{a},\mathbb{G}_{a})
-structures on
X/k
.

(ii)

Suppose that
X
is a supersingular genus
1
curve.
Then, there exists a canonical bijection

(287)

\displaystyle F\text{-}\mathrm{Ehr}_{(\mathbb{G}_{a},\mathbb{G}_{a}),X/k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{G}_{m}(k;\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X/k}))\left(=\Gamma(X,\Omega_{X/k})\setminus\{0\}\right).

Proof.

The assertions follow from (
270
), the latter assertion of Proposition
4.7.2
, (i), and Proposition
4.8.2
, (ii).
∎

5.4.
Case (e):

Finally, let us consider the case where
G
is an elliptic curve and
H=\{e\}
.

Proposition 5.4.1
.

For each
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\sqcup\{\infty\}
,
there exists an
F^{N}
-
(G,G)
-structure on
X/k
if and only if
X
is of genus
1
and isogenous to
G
.
In particular, there are no
F^{N}
-
(G,G)
-structures on any smooth proper curve of genus
>1
.

Proof.

The latter assertion is a direct consequence of the former assertion, so we only discuss the former assertion.
First, let us consider the “if” part of the required equivalence.
Suppose that
X
is of genus
1
and there exists an isogeny morphism
X\rightarrow G
.
This morphism decomposes as
X\xrightarrow{F_{X/k}^{(m)}}X^{(m)}\xrightarrow{w}G
for some
m\geq 0
, where
w
denotes a finite étale
k
-covering.
Then, the pull-back
w^{*}(\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{triv},\heartsuit}_{N})
of the standard
F^{N}
-
(G,G)
-structure
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{triv},\heartsuit}_{N}
on
G/k
(cf. (
38
)) forms
an
F^{N}
-
(G,G)
-structure on
X^{(m)}
.
Moreover, it induces
an
F^{N}
-
(G,G)
-structure on
X/k
via base-change by
(F_{k}^{-1})^{m}:\mathrm{Spec}(k)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{Spec}(k)
.
This completes the proof of the “if” part.

Next,
we shall consider the “only if” part.
To prove this,
it suffices to consider the case of
N=1
.
Suppose that
X
is not isogenous to
G
and admits an
F^{1}
-
(G,G)
-structure
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
.
Denote by
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(X\times\{e\},\nabla)
the dormant indigenous
(G,\{e\})
-bundle corresponding to
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
.
Under the natural identification
\mathrm{Lie}(G)_{X\times G}/\mathrm{Lie}(\{e\})_{X\times\{e\}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\left(\mathcal{O}_{X}\otimes\mathrm{Lie}(G)=\right)\mathcal{O}_{X}
,
the Kodaira-Spencer map
\mathrm{KS}_{(X\times\{e\},\nabla)}
may be regarded as an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear morphism
\mathcal{T}_{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}
.
Since
\mathrm{KS}_{(X\times\{e\},\nabla)}
is assumed to be an isomorphism,
the equality
\mathrm{deg}(\mathcal{T}_{X})=0
holds, in particular,
X
must be of genus
1
.
Next, denote by
\mathcal{G}
the (right)
G
-bundle on
X^{(1)}
corresponding to
the left
G_{X/S}^{(1)}
-torsor
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
.
Let us fix an isomorphism of
G
-bundles
\alpha:X\times G\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}F^{*}_{X/k}(\mathcal{G})
.
Note that there exist a smooth proper curve
Y
and a finite étale covering
y:Y\rightarrow X
such that
\mathcal{G}
can be trivialized after pull-back by the finite étale covering
y^{(1)}:Y^{(1)}\rightarrow X^{(1)}
induced by
y
(cf.
[
37
, Chap II, § 5, Lemma 5.5]
).
That is to say, we can find an isomorphism
\beta:Y^{(1)}\times_{X^{(1)}}\mathcal{G}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}Y^{(1)}\times G
.
Both
\alpha
and
\beta
yield the composite

(288)

\displaystyle\gamma:Y\xrightarrow{a\mapsto(a,e)}Y\times G\xrightarrow{y^{*}(\alpha)}y^{*}(F^{*}_{X/k}(\mathcal{G}))\left(=F^{*}_{Y/k}(y^{(1)*}(\mathcal{G}))\right)\xrightarrow{F^{*}_{Y/k}(\beta)}Y\times G\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}G.

The curve
Y
is not isogenous to
G
because
X
is assumed not to be isogenous to
G
.
Hence, the image of
\gamma
consists exactly of one point.
It follows that its differential
d\gamma:\mathcal{T}_{Y}\rightarrow\gamma^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{G})\left(=\mathcal{O}_{Y}\otimes\mathrm{Lie}(G)\right)
is the zero map.
This contradicts the fact that

d\gamma

coincides with the pull-back of the isomorphism
\mathrm{KS}_{(X\times\{e\},\nabla)}
by
y
.
This completes the proof of the “only if” part.
∎

6.
Deformation theory of (dormant) indigenous bundles

The topic of this section is the deformation theory of (dormant) indigenous
(G,H)
-bundles.
After proving basic facts on various deformation functors, we formulate and prove the Ehresmann-Weil-Thurston principle for Frobenius-Ehresmann structures (cf. Theorem
6.9.1
), which is the main result of this section.

Denote by

\mathfrak{S}\mathfrak{e}\mathfrak{t}
the category of (small) sets and by

\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}
the category of local Artinian
k
-algebras with residue field
k
.
We shall write
k_{\epsilon}:=k[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^{2})
, which is an object of
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}
.
For each
R\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k})
, we write
\mathfrak{m}_{R}
for the maximal ideal of
R
.
Throughout this section, we fix a smooth scheme
X
of finite type over
k
.
Note that each morphism of sheaves
\nabla:\mathcal{K}^{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{1}
on a scheme may be regarded as a complex concentrated at degrees
0
and
1
; we shall denote this complex by
\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla]
.
Also, for each sheaf
\mathcal{F}
, we shall write
\mathcal{F}[0]
for the complex defined to be
\mathcal{F}
concentrated at degree
0
.

6.1.
Deformation functors

By a
functor on
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}
(or sometimes just a
functor
when there is no fear of confusion), we shall mean a covariant functor

D:\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}\rightarrow\mathfrak{S}\mathfrak{e}\mathfrak{t}
such that
D(k)
is a singleton.
Also, following
[
16
, §  Definition 2.8]
, we refer to a functor
D
on
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}
as
a
functor with good deformation theory
(or simply, a
functor with gdt
) if it satisfies the following two of the Schlessinger conditions:

(H1)

For any morphism
\sigma^{\prime}:R^{\prime}\rightarrow R
and any surjective morphism
\sigma^{\prime\prime}:R^{\prime\prime}\twoheadrightarrow R
both of which belong to
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}
,
the map

(289)

\displaystyle D(R^{\prime}\times_{R}R^{\prime\prime})\rightarrow D(R^{\prime})\times_{D(R)}D(R^{\prime\prime})

induced naturally by
\sigma^{\prime}
and
\sigma^{\prime\prime}
is surjective.
(This condition remains equivalent even if
\sigma^{\prime\prime}
is moreover assumed to be a principal small extension.)

(H2)

With the above notation,
(
289
) becomes bijective when
R=k
.
(This condition remains equivalent even if
R^{\prime\prime}
is moreover assumed to be
k_{\epsilon}
.)

Let
D:\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}\rightarrow\mathfrak{S}\mathfrak{e}\mathfrak{t}
be a deformation functor with gdt and write
TD:=D(k_{\epsilon})
.
Then, according to
[
16
, § 2, Lemma 2.12]
,
the following properties hold:

•

TD
has a canonical structure of
k
-vector space;

•

For any small extension in
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}
of the form
e:=(0\rightarrow M\rightarrow R\rightarrow k\rightarrow 0)
,
there exists a canonical bijection
M\otimes TD\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}D(R)
;

•

For any small extension
e:=(0\rightarrow M\rightarrow R\xrightarrow{\sigma}R_{0}\rightarrow 0)
in
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}
, there exists an
M\otimes TD
-action on
D(R)
which is restricted to a transitive action on the fibers of
D(\sigma):D(R)\rightarrow D(R_{0})
.
Moreover, the formation of this action is compatible, in an evident sense, with morphisms of small extensions.

Here, we recall several notions concerning deformation functors used in our discussion.
A morphism of functors
\gamma:D\rightarrow D^{\prime}
is called
formally smooth
if for every small extension
e:=(0\rightarrow M\rightarrow R\xrightarrow{\sigma}R_{0}\rightarrow 0)
in
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}
, the map of sets
D(R)\rightarrow D(R_{0})\times_{D^{\prime}(R_{0})}D^{\prime}(R)
induced by
D(\sigma)
and
\gamma(R)
is surjective.
If
\gamma:D\rightarrow D^{\prime}
is a morphism between functors with gdt,
then the map
d\gamma:=\gamma(k_{\epsilon}):TD\rightarrow TD^{\prime}
induced by it becomes a
k
-linear morphism of vector spaces. We shall refer to
d\gamma
as the
differential
of
\gamma
.

Next, an
obstruction theory
for a functor
D
is defined as a collection
(O,\{o_{e}\}_{e})
consisting of a
k
-vector space
O
and a map
o_{e}:D(R_{0})\rightarrow M\otimes O
for every small extension
e:=(0\rightarrow M\rightarrow R\rightarrow R_{0}\rightarrow 0)
such that an element
a\in D(R_{0})
satisfies
o_{e}(a)=0
if and only if
a
lifts to
D(R)
.
The
k
-vector space
O
is called an
obstruction space
of
D
.
We shall often abuse notation by just writing
O
for
(O,\{o_{e}\}_{e})
.

To every complete local
k
-algebra
R
with residue field
k
,
we can associate a functor with gdt
h_{R}:\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}\rightarrow\mathfrak{S}\mathfrak{e}\mathfrak{t}
defined by
h_{R}(R^{\prime}):=\mathrm{Hom}_{k}(R,R^{\prime})
.
Recall that a functor on
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}
is called
pro-representable
if it is isomorphic to
h_{R}
for such an
R
.

Definition 6.1.1
.

(i)

A
deformation triple
is a collection

(290)

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}:=(D,T,O),

consisting of a functor
D
with gdt, a
k
-vector space
T
equipped with an identification
T\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}TD
, and an obstruction theory
O
for
D
.

(ii)

Let
\mathbb{D}:=(D,T,O)
and
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}:=(D^{\prime},T^{\prime},O^{\prime})
be deformation triples.
A
morphism of deformation triples
from
\mathbb{D}
to
\mathbb{D}^{\prime}
is defined as a triple

(291)

\displaystyle(\gamma_{D},\gamma_{T},\gamma_{O}),

where

–

\gamma_{D}
denotes a morphism of functors
\gamma_{D}:D\rightarrow D^{\prime}
;

–

\gamma_{T}
denotes a
k
-linear morphism
T\rightarrow T^{\prime}

such that for any small extension
e:=(0\rightarrow M\rightarrow R\xrightarrow{\sigma}R_{0}\rightarrow 0)
in
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}
, the
M\otimes T
-action on
D(R)
is compatible with
the
M\otimes T^{\prime}
-action on
D^{\prime}(R)
via
\gamma_{T}
and
\gamma_{D}
;

–

\gamma_{O}
denotes a
k
-linear morphism
O\rightarrow O^{\prime}
such that for any
e
as above, the following square diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{D(R_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{o_{e}}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{D}(R_{0})}
\textstyle{M\otimes O\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes\gamma_{O}}
\textstyle{D^{\prime}(R_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{o^{\prime}_{e}}
\textstyle{M\otimes O^{\prime}.}

Definition 6.1.2
.

Let
D
be a functor with gdt.
We shall say that
D
is
rigid

if for any small extension
e:=(0\rightarrow M\rightarrow R\xrightarrow{\sigma}R_{0}\rightarrow 0)
in
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}
,
the transitive
M\otimes TD
-action on every fiber of
D(\sigma):D(R)\rightarrow D(R_{0})
is free, i.e., the fibers form
M\otimes TD
-torsors.

Remark 6.1.3
.

Let
D:\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}\rightarrow\mathfrak{S}\mathfrak{e}\mathfrak{t}
be a functor with gdt.

(i)

It follows from
[
48
, § 2, Theorem 2.11]

that

D
is pro-representable if and only if
D
is rigid and satisfies
\mathrm{dim}_{k}(TD)<\infty
.

(ii)

Suppose that
D
is associated with a deformation category
\mathcal{D}
in the sense of
[
49
, § 2, Definition 2.16]
.
For each
R\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k})
and
\xi\in\mathcal{D}(R)
, we shall write
\mathrm{Aut}_{R}(\xi)
for the automorphism of
\xi
over the identity morphism of
R
.
Then,
the rigidity of
D
holds if, for any small extension
0\rightarrow M\rightarrow R\xrightarrow{\sigma}R_{0}\rightarrow 0
in
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}
, the group homomorphism
\mathrm{Aut}_{R}(\xi)\rightarrow\mathrm{Aut}_{R_{0}}(\sigma^{*}(\xi))
is surjective.

Definition 6.1.4
.

Let
\gamma:D\rightarrow D^{\prime}
be a morphism of functors with gdt.
We shall say that
\gamma
is a
semi-isomorphism

if it is formally smooth and its differential
