d\gamma:TD\rightarrow TD^{\prime}
is bijective.
(Recall that a
pro-representable hull
for a functor
D
is a semi-isomorphism
h_{R}\rightarrow D
, where
R
is a completes local
k
-algebra
R
with residue field
k
.)

Proposition 6.1.5
.

Let
\gamma:D\rightarrow D^{\prime}
be a morphism of functors with gdt.

(i)

Suppose that
D^{\prime}
is rigid.
Then,
\gamma
is a semi-isomorphism if and only if it is an isomorphism.

(ii)

Suppose that
\gamma
is a semi-isomorphism
and there exists
a pro-representable hull
\nu:h_{R}\rightarrow D
for
D
.
Then, the composite
\gamma\circ\nu:h_{R}\rightarrow D^{\prime}
is a pro-representable hull for
D^{\prime}
.

(iii)

Suppose that there exist pro-representable hulls
\nu:h_{R}\rightarrow D
,

\nu^{\prime}:h_{R^{\prime}}\rightarrow D^{\prime}

for

D
,
D^{\prime}
respectively.
Then, the following conditions are equivalent to each other:

–

The morphism
\gamma
is a semi-isomorphism.

–

There exists an isomorphism
\sigma:R\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}R^{\prime}
of complete local
k
-algebras such that the induced isomorphism
h_{\sigma}:h_{R}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}h_{R^{\prime}}
makes the following diagram commute:

\textstyle{h_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{h_{\sigma}}
\textstyle{D\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma}
\textstyle{h_{R^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu^{\prime}}
\textstyle{D^{\prime}.}

Proof.

The assertions follow immediately from various definitions involved together with routine arguments of deformation functors.
∎

Remark 6.1.6
.

Let
\gamma:D\rightarrow D^{\prime}
be a morphism of functors.
Then, without the rigidity assumption on
D^{\prime}
,
the morphism
\gamma
can be a semi-isomorphism, yet
\gamma
need not be an isomorphism.
It is because of this subtle difference that a semiuniversal family of a deformation functor can fail to be universal (see
[
42
, § 3, Example 12]
for an example).

Proposition 6.1.7
.

Let
(\gamma_{D},\gamma_{T},\gamma_{O}):(D,T,O)\rightarrow(D^{\prime},T^{\prime},O^{\prime})
be a morphism of deformation triples.
Suppose that
\gamma_{T}:T\rightarrow T^{\prime}
is an isomorphism and
\gamma_{O}:O\rightarrow O^{\prime}
is injective.
Then,
\gamma_{D}:D\rightarrow D^{\prime}
is a semi-isomorphism.

Proof.

The formal smoothness of
\gamma_{D}

can be proved by a routine diagram-chasing argument using the commutative diagram

defined for every small extension
e:=(0\rightarrow M\rightarrow R\xrightarrow{\sigma}R_{0}\rightarrow 0)
in
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}
.
∎

6.2.
Deformation of smooth schemes

For
R\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k})
,
a
deformation of
X
over
R
is a smooth scheme
X_{R}
over
R
together with a
k
-isomorphism
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}k\times_{R}X_{R}
.
The notion of an isomorphism between two such deformations can be defined in an evident manner.
The
trivial deformation of
X
over
R
is the smooth
R
-scheme

(304)

\displaystyle X_{R}^{\circ}:=R\times X

together with the natural isomorphism
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}k\times_{R}(R\times X)
.
The assignment from each
R\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k})
to the set
\mathrm{Def}_{X}(R)
of isomorphism classes of deformations of
X
over
R
defines a functor with gdt

(305)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Def}_{X}:\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}\rightarrow\mathfrak{S}\mathfrak{e}\mathfrak{t}.

For each
i\geq 0
, we shall write

(306)

\displaystyle H^{i}:=H^{i}(X,\mathcal{T}_{X}).

Then, it is well-known that

(\mathrm{Def}_{X},H^{1},H^{2})
forms a deformation triple, and
the group of infinitesimal automorphisms of
X
is isomorphic to
H^{0}
.

6.3.
Deformation of indigenous
(G,H)
-bundles

We shall fix an indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)
on
X/k
.

Definition 6.3.1
.

(i)

Let
R\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k})
.
A
deformation of
(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})
over
R
is
a collection of data

(307)

\displaystyle(X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit},\varrho),

where

X_{R}
denotes a deformation of
X
over
R
,
\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit}
denotes an indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle on
X_{R}/R
, and
\varrho
denotes an isomorphism of indigenous
(G,H)
-bundles

\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}k\times_{R}\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit}
.
A
deformation of
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
over
R
is defined as
a pair

(308)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit},\varrho)

so that

(X_{R}^{\circ},\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit},\varrho)
forms a deformation of
(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})
over
R
.
We shall often abuse notation by just writing
(X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit})
(resp.,
\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit}
) for
(X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit},\varrho)
(resp.,
(\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit},\varrho)
).

(ii)

Let
\sigma:R^{\prime}\rightarrow R
be a morphism in
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}
and denote by

\sigma^{\sharp}
the morphism
\mathrm{Spec}(R)\rightarrow\mathrm{Spec}(R^{\prime})
induced by
\sigma
.
Also, let
(X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit},\varrho)
(resp.,
(X_{R^{\prime}},\mathcal{E}_{R^{\prime}}^{\spadesuit},\varrho^{\prime})
) be a deformation of
(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})
over
R
(resp.,
R^{\prime}
).
A
morphism over
\sigma
from
(X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit},\varrho)

to
(X_{R^{\prime}},\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}_{R^{\prime}},\varrho^{\prime})
is a pair

(309)

\displaystyle(\xi_{X},\xi_{\mathcal{E}})

consisting of a morphism
\xi_{X}:X_{R}\rightarrow X_{R^{\prime}}
over
\sigma^{\sharp}

and an isomorphism of indigenous
(G,H)
-bundles
\xi_{\mathcal{E}}:\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\xi^{*}_{X}(\mathcal{E}_{R^{\prime}}^{\spadesuit})
whose reduction modulo
\mathfrak{m}_{R}
coincides with the identity morphism of
(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})
via
\varrho
and
\varrho^{\prime}
.
If
\sigma=\mathrm{id}_{R}
, then we shall refer to such a pair as a
morphism over
R
.
Finally, we can define the notion of a morphism between deformations of

\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
in an evident manner.

For each
R\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k})
,
we shall write

(310)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}(R):=\left(\begin{matrix}\text{the set of isomorphism classes}\\
\text{of deformations of $\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}$ over $R$}\end{matrix}\right)\hskip 34.1433pt

\displaystyle\left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}(R):=\left(\begin{matrix}\text{the set of isomorphism classes}\\
\text{of deformations of $(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})$ over $R$}\end{matrix}\right)\right).

If
\sigma:R^{\prime}\rightarrow R
is a morphism in
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}
,
then changing the base by the induced morphism
\mathrm{Spec}(R)\rightarrow\mathrm{Spec}(R^{\prime})
gives a map of sets
\sigma^{*}:\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}(R^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}(R)
(resp.,
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}(R^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}(R)
).
Thus,
the assignments
R\mapsto\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}(R)
(resp.,
R\mapsto\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}(R)
) and
\sigma\mapsto\sigma^{*}
determine
a functor

(311)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}\right):\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}\rightarrow\mathfrak{S}\mathfrak{e}\mathfrak{t}.

One may verify from various definitions involved that
this functor specifies
a functor with gdt.
The assignment

\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}_{R}\mapsto(X_{R}^{\circ},\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit})
(resp.,
(X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit})\mapsto X_{R}
)
determines a morphism of functors

(312)

\displaystyle\gamma_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}:\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \delta_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}:\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}\rightarrow\mathrm{Def}_{X}\right).

6.4.
Cohomological descriptions of
\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}
and
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}

In what follows, let us describe the tangent and obstruction spaces of the deformation functors introduced above in terms of cohomology groups.
The corresponding discussion
for holomorphic Cartan geometries
can be found in
[
9
]
(cf. Remark
6.4.5
).

Let
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)
be as above
and write
\mathcal{E}_{G}:=\mathcal{E}_{H}\times^{H}G
.
Denote by
\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}_{G}
the
k
-connection
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}\rightarrow\Omega_{X}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
on the adjoint bundle
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
induced by
\nabla
via change of structure group by
\mathrm{Ad}_{G}:G\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}(\mathfrak{g})
.
Denote by

(313)

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}_{H}:\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\rightarrow\Omega_{X}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}

the morphism obtained by restricting the domain of
\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}}
to
\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\left(\subseteq\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}\right)
.
For any local sections

v\in\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}\left(:=\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}/k}\right)
and

\partial\in\mathcal{T}_{X}
,
we shall write

(314)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\partial}(v):=[\nabla(\partial),v]-\nabla([\partial,d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}(v)])\in\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}.

Lemma 6.4.1
.

The local section
\widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\partial}(v)

lies in

\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}\left(\subseteq\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}\right)
.

Proof.

Note that
d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
preserves the Lie bracket operations.
Hence, for any local sections
v\in\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
and
\partial\in\mathcal{T}_{X}
, the following sequence of equalities holds:

(315)

\displaystyle d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}(\widetilde{\nabla}_{\partial}^{\mathrm{ad}}(v))

\displaystyle=d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}([\nabla(\partial),v]-\nabla([\partial,d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}(v)]))

\displaystyle=[d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}\circ\nabla(\partial),d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}(v)]-d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}\circ\nabla([\partial,d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}(v)])

\displaystyle=[\partial,d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}(v)]-[\partial,d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}(v)]

\displaystyle=0.

This means that the image of
\widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\partial}(v)
lies in

\mathrm{Ker}(d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}})\left(=\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}\right)
.
∎

Let
\Box
denote either
G
or
H
.
By the above lemma, the assignment

v\mapsto\widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{(-)}(v)
defines
a
k
-linear morphism

(316)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\nabla}_{\Box}^{\mathrm{ad}}:\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{\Box}}\rightarrow\left(\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathcal{T}_{X},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}})=\right)\Omega_{X}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}.

This morphism fits into the following morphism of short exact sequences:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Lie}(\Box)_{\mathcal{E}_{\Box}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{inclusion}}
\scriptstyle{\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\Box}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{\Box}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d_{\mathcal{E}_{\Box}}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\Box}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega_{X}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{\Omega_{X}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

In other words,
we have the following short exact sequence of complexes:

(321)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\Box}]\longrightarrow\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\Box}]\longrightarrow\mathcal{T}_{X}[0]\longrightarrow 0.

For each
i\geq 0
, we shall write

(322)

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}^{i}_{\Box}:=\mathbb{H}^{i}(X,\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{\Box}^{\mathrm{ad}}]),\hskip 14.22636pt\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{\Box}^{i}:=\mathbb{H}^{i}(X,\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{\Box}^{\mathrm{ad}}]).

By applying the hypercohomology functor
\mathbb{H}^{i}(X,-)
(
i\geq 0
) to (
321
), we obtain morphisms

(323)

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}^{i}_{\Box}\xrightarrow{\gamma^{i}_{\Box}}\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{\Box}^{i}\xrightarrow{\delta^{i}_{\Box}}H^{i}.

If
X
is proper over
k
, then
the
k
-vector spaces appearing in (
323
) are all finite-dimensional.

Lemma 6.4.2
.

Let us consider the
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear endomorphism
\eta
of
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
given by
v\mapsto v-(\nabla\circ d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}})(v)
for each local section
v\in\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
.
Then, this endomorphism is restricted to a morphism (resp., an isomorphism)

(324)

\displaystyle\eta_{G}:\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \eta_{H}:\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}\right).

Moreover, for
\Box\in\{G,H\}
, the following diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{\Box}/k}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\Box}}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\Box}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}_{G}}
\textstyle{\Omega_{X}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}.}

In particular,
the isomorphism
\eta^{\bullet}_{H}:\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{H}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}_{G}]
determined by
the pair of morphisms
(\eta_{H},\mathrm{id}_{\Omega_{X}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}})
induces, for each
i\geq 0
, an isomorphism of
k
-vector spaces
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{H}^{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{H}^{i}_{G}
.

Proof.

First, let us prove the former assertion.
To this end, we only consider the case of
\eta_{H}
because the other case is entirely similar.
If we take a local section
v\in\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
,
then we have

(329)

\displaystyle d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}(\eta(v))

\displaystyle=d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}(v-(\nabla\circ d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}})(v))

\displaystyle=d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}(v)-(d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}\circ\nabla)(d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}(v))

\displaystyle=d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}(v)-d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}(v)

\displaystyle=0.

This implies that
\eta(v)\in\mathrm{Ker}(d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}})=\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
, so the image of
\eta
is contained in
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
.
By restricting the domain and codomain of
\eta
,
we obtain an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear morphism
\eta_{H}:\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
.
One may verify that
\eta_{H}
fits into the following morphism of short exact sequences:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{inclusion}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{H}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{KS}_{(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)}}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{inclusion}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{quotient}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}/\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\left(\cong\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}/\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Since the Kodaira-Spencer map
\mathrm{KS}_{(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)}
is an isomorphism,

\eta_{H}
turns out to be an isomorphism by the five lemma.

Next, we shall prove the latter assertion, i.e., the commutativity of (
6.4.2
).
Clearly, it suffices to consider the case of
\eta_{G}
.
Denote by
\langle-,-\rangle
the pairing
\mathcal{T}_{X}\times(\Omega_{X}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}})\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
arising from the natural pairing
\mathcal{T}_{X}\times\Omega_{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}
.
Then,
for each local section
v\in\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
, we have

(334)

\displaystyle\langle\partial,(\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}}\circ\eta_{G})(v)\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\partial,\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}}(v-(\nabla\circ d_{\mathcal{E}_{H}})(v))\rangle

\displaystyle=[\nabla(\partial),v-(\nabla\circ d_{\mathcal{E}_{H}})(v)]

\displaystyle=[\nabla(\partial),v]-[\nabla(\partial),(\nabla\circ d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}})(v)]

\displaystyle=[\nabla(\partial),v]-\nabla([\partial,d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}(v)])

\displaystyle=\langle\partial,\widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{G}(v)\rangle.

This implies
\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}}\circ\eta_{G}=\widetilde{\nabla}_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}}
, completing the proof of this lemma.
∎

Proposition 6.4.3
.

(i)

The collection
(\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}},\mathbb{H}^{1}_{H},\mathbb{H}^{2}_{H})
forms a deformation triple.
Moreover, the group of infinitesimal automorphisms of
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
(i.e., the group of automorphisms of
k_{\epsilon}\times_{k}\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
inducing the identity morphism of
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
via reduction modulo
(\epsilon)
) is isomorphic to
\mathbb{H}^{0}_{H}
.

(ii)

The collection
(\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})},\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{H},\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{H})
(
\cong(\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})},\mathbb{H}^{1}_{G},\mathbb{H}^{2}_{G})
by Lemma
6.4.2
) forms a deformation triple.
Moreover, the group of infinitesimal automorphisms of
(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})
is isomorphic to
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{H}^{0}
(
\cong\mathbb{H}^{0}_{G}
).

(iii)

Suppose that
X
is proper over
k
.
Then, there exist pro-representable hulls for
\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}
and
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}
.

Proof.

We will omit the proof of assertion (i)
because it is entirely similar to
the proof of assertion (ii).

First, we shall consider the tangent space of
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}
.
Let
e:=(0\rightarrow M\rightarrow R\xrightarrow{\sigma}R_{0}\rightarrow 0)
be a small extension in
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}
and
(X_{R},\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}_{R})
(where
\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{R,H},\nabla_{R})
)
a deformation of
(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})
over
R
.
Also, let us take
an element
v
of
\mathbb{H}^{1}(X,\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes\widetilde{\nabla}_{H}^{\mathrm{ad}}])\left(\cong M\otimes\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{H}\right)
, where
\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes\widetilde{\nabla}_{H}^{\mathrm{ad}}
denotes the tensor product
M\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\rightarrow M\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
of
\mathrm{id}_{M}
and
\widetilde{\nabla}_{H}^{\mathrm{ad}}
.
Note here that
\mathbb{H}^{1}(X,\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes\widetilde{\nabla}_{H}^{\mathrm{ad}}])
can be realized as the total cohomology of the Čech double complex of

\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes\widetilde{\nabla}_{H}^{\mathrm{ad}}]
associated with an affine covering.
That is to say, we can find an affine open covering
\mathcal{U}:=\{U_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in I}
(where

I
is a finite index set) of
X
and
v
may be represented
by
a collection of data

(335)

\displaystyle v=(\{\widetilde{\partial}_{\alpha\beta}\}_{(\alpha,\beta)\in I_{2}},\{\delta_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in I}),

where

•

I_{2}
denotes the subset of
I\times I
consisting of pairs
(\alpha,\beta)
with
U_{\alpha\beta}:=U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta}\neq\emptyset
;

•

\{\widetilde{\partial}_{\alpha\beta}\}_{(\alpha,\beta)\in I_{2}}
is
a Čech
1
-cocycle in

\check{C}^{1}(\mathcal{U},M\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}})
, where
\widetilde{\partial}_{\alpha\beta}\in\Gamma(U_{\alpha\beta},M\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}})
;

•

\{\delta_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in I}
is a Čech
0
-cochain in
\check{C}^{0}(\mathcal{U},M\otimes(\Omega_{X}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}))
, where
\delta_{\alpha}\in\Gamma(U_{\alpha},M\otimes(\Omega_{X}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}))=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{U_{\alpha}}}(\mathcal{T}_{X}|_{U_{\alpha}},M\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}|_{U_{\alpha}})
,

such that

\{\widetilde{\partial}_{\alpha\beta}\}_{(\alpha,\beta)\in I_{2}}
and
\{\delta_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in I}

agree under
\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes\widetilde{\nabla}_{H}^{\mathrm{ad}}
and the Čech coboundary map.
For each
(\alpha,\beta)\in I_{2}
,
we set
\partial_{\alpha\beta}:=d_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}(\widetilde{\partial}_{\alpha\beta})
.
We shall write
U_{R,\alpha}:=X_{R}|_{U_{\alpha}}
for each
\alpha\in I
and write

U_{R,\alpha\beta}:=X_{R}|_{U_{\alpha\beta}}
for each
(\alpha,\beta)\in I_{2}
.
The element
\partial_{\alpha\beta}
(resp.,
\widetilde{\partial}_{\alpha\beta}
) may be regarded as a
k
-linear morphism

\partial_{\alpha\beta}:\mathcal{O}_{U_{\alpha\beta}}\rightarrow M\otimes\mathcal{O}_{U_{\alpha\beta}}

(resp.,
\widetilde{\partial}_{\alpha\beta}:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}|_{U_{\alpha\beta}}}\rightarrow M\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}|_{U_{\alpha\beta}}}
) satisfying
the Leibniz rule.
Then,

\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{O}_{U_{R,\alpha\beta}}}+\partial_{\alpha\beta}
(resp.,
\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{R,H}|_{U_{R,\alpha\beta}}}}+\widetilde{\partial}_{\alpha\beta}
) defines a well-defined automorphism
\partial^{\sharp}_{\alpha\beta}
(resp.,
\widetilde{\partial}^{\sharp}_{\alpha\beta}
) of
U_{R,\alpha\beta}
(resp.,
\mathcal{E}_{R,H}|_{U_{R,\alpha\beta}}
).
The automorphism
\widetilde{\partial}^{\sharp}_{\alpha\beta}
is
H
-equivariant and
the following square diagram is commutative and cartesian:

\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{R,H}|_{U_{R,\alpha\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\partial}^{\sharp}_{\alpha\beta}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{projection}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}_{R,H}|_{U_{R,\alpha\beta}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{projection}}
\textstyle{U_{R,\alpha\beta}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{\sharp}_{\alpha\beta}}
\textstyle{U_{R,\alpha\beta}.}

By means of
\partial_{\alpha\beta}^{\sharp}
’s (
(\alpha,\beta)\in I_{2}
), we can glue together
U_{R,\alpha}
’s (
\alpha\in I
) to
obtain a smooth scheme
X^{v}_{R}
over
R
.
Moreover,
the pairs
(\mathcal{E}_{R,H}|_{U_{R,\alpha}},\nabla|_{U_{R,\alpha}}+\delta_{\alpha})

may be glued together by means of

\widetilde{\partial}^{\sharp}_{\alpha\beta}
’s.
Thus, we obtain an
H
-bundle
\mathcal{E}_{R,H}^{v}
on
X_{R}^{v}
and an
R
-connection
\nabla^{v}_{R}
on
\mathcal{E}_{R,G}^{v}:=\mathcal{E}_{R,H}^{v}\times^{H}G
.
The Kodaira-Spencer map
\mathrm{KS}_{(\mathcal{E}_{R,H}^{v},\nabla^{v}_{R})}

is an isomorphism because its reduction modulo
\mathfrak{m}_{R}
coincides with the isomorphism
\mathrm{KS}_{(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)}
.
It follows that the resulting pair
\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit,v}:=(\mathcal{E}_{R,H}^{v},\nabla^{v}_{R})
forms an indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle on
X^{v}_{R}
and the pair
(X_{R}^{v},\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit,v})
specifies an element of
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}(R)
.
The assignment
((X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit}),v)\mapsto(X_{R}^{v},\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit,v})
defines a transitive action of
M\otimes\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{H}^{1}
on
the fibers of
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}(\sigma):\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}(R)\rightarrow\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}(R_{0})
.
(To verify the transitivity of this action, we may need the fact that
M\otimes\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{H}^{1}
is canonically isomorphic to the
1
-st hypercohomology group of the complex of
étale
sheaves associated with
\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes\widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{H}]
, each of whose elements may be represented by a Čech
1
-cocycle with respect to an étale covering; this is because
H
-bundles are locally trivial in the étale topology, but not necessarily in the Zariski topology.)
This action is, by construction, verified to be free if
R=k
.
Consequently,
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{H}^{1}
is isomorphic to the tangent space of
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}
.

Next, we shall consider the assertion about an obstruction space.
Let
e
be as above and

a
an element of
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}(R_{0})
.
Choose a deformation
(X_{R_{0}},\mathcal{E}_{R_{0}}^{\spadesuit})
(where

\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}_{R_{0}}:=(\mathcal{E}_{R_{0},H},\nabla_{R_{0}})
) of
(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})
representing
a
.
Observe that
there exists an affine open covering

\mathcal{V}:=\{V_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in J}
of
X
such that, for each
\alpha\in J
,
the
R_{0}
-scheme
V_{R_{0},\alpha}:=X_{R_{0}}|_{V_{\alpha}}
has a deformation
V_{R,\alpha}
over
R
and

(\mathcal{E}_{R_{0},H}|_{V_{\alpha}},\nabla_{R_{0}}|_{V_{\alpha}})
may be lifted to a pair
\mathcal{E}_{R,\alpha}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{R,H,\alpha},\nabla_{R,\alpha})
defined over
V_{R,\alpha}
.
After possibly replacing
\mathcal{V}
by its refinement,
we can find a collection
\{\eta_{\alpha\beta}\}_{(\alpha,\beta)\in J_{2}}
, where each
\eta_{\alpha\beta}
denotes an
H
-equivariant isomorphism
\mathcal{E}_{R,H,\alpha}|_{V_{\alpha\beta}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{E}_{R,H,\beta}|_{V_{\alpha\beta}}
inducing the identity morphism of
\mathcal{E}_{R_{0},H}|_{V_{\alpha\beta}}
via reduction modulo
M
.
There exists an element
\delta_{\alpha\beta}\in\Gamma(U_{\alpha\beta},M\otimes(\Omega_{X}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}))
with
\eta^{*}_{\alpha\beta}(\nabla_{R,\alpha})=\nabla_{R,\beta}+\delta_{\alpha\beta}
.
Moreover, for each triple
(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)\in I\times I\times I
with
U_{\alpha\beta\gamma}:=U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta}\cap U_{\gamma}\neq\emptyset
, there exists an element
\widetilde{\partial}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}\in\Gamma(U_{\alpha\beta\gamma},M\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}})
with

\eta_{\gamma\alpha}\circ\eta_{\beta\gamma}\circ\eta_{\alpha\beta}=\mathrm{id}+\widetilde{\partial}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}
.
The collection
(\{\widetilde{\partial}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}\}_{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)},\{\delta_{\alpha\beta}\}_{(\alpha,\beta)})
represents an element
o_{e}(a)
of
M\otimes\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{H}^{2}
.
In this way, to each small extension
e
as above, we associate a map of sets
o_{e}:\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}(R_{0})\rightarrow M\otimes\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{H}
.
By construction, the equality
o_{e}(a)=0
holds if and only if the pairs
(V_{R,\alpha},\mathcal{E}_{R,\alpha}^{\spadesuit})
may be glued together, by means of
\{\eta_{\alpha\beta}\}_{(\alpha,\beta)\in J_{2}}
, to obtain a deformation of
(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})
over
R
lifting
(X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit})
.
That is to say,
(\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{H},\{o_{e}\}_{e})
forms an obstruction theory for
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}
.

The last assertion of (ii) follows immediately from the Čech cohomological description of deformations discussed above.
Finally, assertion (iii) follows from
[
48
, § 2, Theorem 2.11]
together with assertions (i), (ii), and the fact that
\mathrm{dim}_{k}(\mathbb{H}_{H}^{1})<\infty
,
\mathrm{dim}_{k}(\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{H}^{1})<\infty
under the properness assumption on
X
.
∎

Corollary 6.4.4
.

Suppose that
\mathbb{H}^{0}_{H}=0
(resp.,
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{H}^{0}=0
) and
X
is proper over
k
.
Then,
\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}
(resp.,
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}
) is pro-representable.
If, moreover,
\mathbb{H}^{2}_{H}=0
(resp.,
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{H}^{2}=0
), then this functor
may be pro-representable by
k[\![x_{1},\cdots,x_{d}]\!]
, where
d:=\mathrm{dim}_{k}(\mathbb{H}^{1}_{H})
(resp.,
d:=\mathrm{dim}_{k}(\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{H})
).

Proof.

The assertion follows from Proposition
6.4.3
and
[
15
, Chap. 6, § 6.2, Corollary 6.2.5, and § 6.3, Corollary 6.3.5]
.
∎

Remark 6.4.5
.

The space of infinitesimal automorphisms, as well as
the infinitesimal deformations, of a
holomorphic
Cartan geometry
are computed in
[
9
, § 3, Propositions 3.1, 3.3, and Theorem 3.4]
.
As S. Dumitrescu commented in our personal communication, the statement of Theorem 3.4 in
loc. cit.
will need to be corrected.
(The updated version may be available at a later date in some way.)
These results after correction are consistent with the assertion in
Proposition
6.4.3
, (i), under the correspondence between Cartan geometries and indigenous bundles by (
225
).

Remark 6.4.6
.

We here recall well-known facts concerning the deformation theory of
G
-bundles and connections. (These facts will be used in the proofs of some propositions described later and hold without the assumption that
\mathcal{E}_{G}
has an
H
-reduction defining the structure of indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle, i.e.,
\mathcal{E}_{H}
.)
Denote by

(340)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{G})}\ (\text{resp.,}\ \mathrm{Def}_{(\mathcal{E}_{G},\nabla)};\text{resp.,}\ \mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{G},\nabla)})

the deformation functor classifying deformations of the pair
(X,\mathcal{E}_{G})
(resp., the pair
(\mathcal{E}_{G},\nabla)
; resp., the triple
(X,\mathcal{E}_{G},\nabla)
), viewed as
a smooth
k
-scheme and a
G
-bundle (resp.,
a
G
-bundle equipped with a
k
-connection; resp., a smooth
k
-scheme and a
G
-bundle on it equipped with a
k
-connection).
Then, by a similar argument in the proof of Proposition
6.4.3
,
we see that

(\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{G})},H^{1}(X,\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}),H^{2}(X,\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}))

(resp.,
(\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathcal{E}_{G},\nabla)},\mathbb{H}^{1}_{G},\mathbb{H}^{2}_{G})
; resp.,
(\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{G},\nabla)},\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{G}^{1},\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{G}^{2})
)
forms a deformation triple.
Moreover, the group of infinitesimal automorphisms of
(X,\mathcal{E}_{G})
(resp.,
(\mathcal{E}_{G},\nabla)
; resp.,
(X,\mathcal{E}_{G},\nabla)
) is isomorphic to
H^{0}(X,\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}})
(resp.,
\mathbb{H}^{0}_{G}
; resp.,
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{0}_{G}
).

By the above proposition and various constructions involved,
the triple

(341)

\displaystyle(\gamma_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}},\gamma^{1}_{H},\gamma_{H}^{2}):(\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}},\mathbb{H}^{1}_{H},\mathbb{H}_{H}^{2})\rightarrow(\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})},\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{H}^{1},\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{H}^{2})

\displaystyle\left(\text{resp.,}\ (\delta_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}},\delta_{H}^{1},\delta_{H}^{2}):(\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})},\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{H}^{1},\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{H}^{2})\rightarrow(\mathrm{Def}_{X},H^{1},H^{2})\right)

(cf. (
312
), (
323
)) forms a morphism of deformation triples.

6.5.
Deformation of flat indigenous
(G,H)
-bundles

Next, suppose that the indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)
is flat.

Definition 6.5.1
.

A deformation
\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit}
(resp.,
(X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit})
) of
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
(resp.,
(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})
) over
R\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k})

is called
flat
if the indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle
\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit}
is flat.

For each
R\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k})
,
we shall
denote by

(342)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{\flat}(R)\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}^{\flat}(R)\right)

the subset of
\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}(R)
(resp.,
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}(R)
) consisting of flat deformations.
The assignment
R\mapsto\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{\flat}(R)
(resp.,
R\mapsto\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}^{\flat}(R)
)
defines
a functor with gdt

(343)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{\flat}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}^{\flat}\right):\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}\rightarrow\mathfrak{S}\mathfrak{e}\mathfrak{t}.

Also, we have a natural inclusion of functors
\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{\flat}\rightarrow\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}
(resp.,
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}^{\flat}\rightarrow\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}
).

In what follows, we compute the tangent and obstruction spaces of
\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{\flat}
,
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}^{\flat}
.
Let
\Box\in\{G,H\}
.
The flatness of
\nabla
implies that of
\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}_{G}
, so the equality

\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}(2)}_{G}\circ\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\Box}=0
holds (cf. (
408
)).
It follows that we can restrict the codomain (resp., the domain and codomain) of
\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}_{G}
to obtain a
k
-linear morphism

(344)

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\Box,Z}:\mathrm{Lie}(\Box)_{\mathcal{E}_{\Box}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}(2)})

The commutativity of (
6.4.2
)
implies
\mathrm{Im}(\widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\Box})\subseteq\mathrm{Im}(\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}})\subseteq\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}(2)})
.
Hence,
the morphism
\widetilde{\nabla}_{\Box}^{\mathrm{ad}}:\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{\Box}}\rightarrow\Omega_{X}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
induces
a
k
-linear morphism

(345)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\nabla}_{\Box,Z}^{\mathrm{ad}}:\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{\Box}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}(2)}).

For each
i\geq 0
, we shall write

(346)

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}^{i}_{\Box,Z}:=\mathbb{H}^{i}(X,\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{\Box,Z}^{\mathrm{ad}}]),\hskip 14.22636pt\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\Box,Z}:=\mathbb{H}^{i}(X,\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{\Box,Z}^{\mathrm{ad}}]).

Then,
the pair of morphisms
(\eta_{\Box},\mathrm{id}_{\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}(2)})})
defines
a morphism
\eta^{\bullet}_{\Box,Z}:\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\Box,Z}]\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{G,Z}^{\mathrm{ad}}]
, and hence, induces a morphism of
k
-vector spaces

(347)

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}^{i}(\eta^{\bullet}_{\Box,Z}):\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{\Box,Z}\rightarrow\mathbb{H}^{i}_{G,Z}.

In the case of
\Box=H
, this morphism in fact is an isomorphism.

Proposition 6.5.2
.

Recall that
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}

is assumed to be flat.

(ii)

The collections
(\mathrm{Def}^{\flat}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}},\mathbb{H}^{1}_{H,Z},\mathbb{H}^{2}_{H,Z})

and
(\mathrm{Def}^{\flat}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})},\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{H,Z}^{1},\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{H,Z}^{2})
(
\cong(\mathrm{Def}^{\flat}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})},\mathbb{H}^{1}_{G,Z},\mathbb{H}^{2}_{G,Z})
by (
347
)) form deformation triples.

(ii)

Suppose that
X
is proper over
k
.
Then,
there exist pro-representable hulls for

\mathrm{Def}^{\flat}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}
and
\mathrm{Def}^{\flat}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}
.

(iii)

Regarding the pro-representability of
\mathrm{Def}^{\flat}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}
(resp.,
\mathrm{Def}^{\flat}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}
),
the statement of Corollary
6.4.4
with
\mathbb{H}^{i}_{H}
’s (resp.,
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{H}
’s) replaced by
\mathbb{H}_{H,Z}^{i}
’s (resp.,
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{H,Z}^{i}
’s) holds for
\mathrm{Def}^{\flat}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}
(resp.,
\mathrm{Def}^{\flat}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}
).

Proof.

The assertions follow from Proposition
6.4.3
(and its proof) together with the following lemma.
∎

Lemma 6.5.3
.

Let
0\rightarrow M\rightarrow R\rightarrow R_{0}\rightarrow 0
be a small extension in
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}
and

(X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit})
(where
\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{R,H},\nabla_{R})
) a flat deformation of
(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})
over
R
.
We shall write
\mathcal{E}_{R,G}:=\mathcal{E}_{R,H}\times^{H}G
.
Also, let

a
be an element of

\Gamma(X,M\otimes\Omega_{X}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}})\left(\subseteq\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathcal{T}_{X},M\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}})\right)
,
and we regard it as an element of
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{R}}}(\mathcal{T}_{X_{R}/R},\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{R,G}/R})
in a natural manner.
Then, the curvature
\psi_{\nabla_{R}+a}
of the sum
\nabla_{R}+a
is given by

(348)

\displaystyle\psi_{\nabla_{R}+a}=(\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}(2)})(a)

via the natural inclusion

(349)

\displaystyle\Gamma(X,M\otimes(\bigwedge^{2}\Omega_{X})\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}})\subseteq\Gamma(X_{R},(\bigwedge^{2}\Omega_{X_{R}/R})\otimes_{R}\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{R,G}}).

In particular,
\psi_{\nabla_{R}+a}=0
if and only if
a\in\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}(2)})\left(=\mathrm{Ker}(\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}(2)})\right)
.

Proof.

For any local sections
\partial_{1},\partial_{2}\in\mathcal{T}_{X_{R}/R}
,
we have

(350)

\displaystyle\psi_{\nabla_{R}+a}^{\triangleright}(\partial_{1},\partial_{2})=

\displaystyle\ [(\nabla_{R}+a)(\partial_{1}),(\nabla_{R}+a)(\partial_{2})]-(\nabla_{R}+a)([\partial_{1},\partial_{2}])

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \cancel{[\nabla_{R}(\partial_{1}),\nabla_{R}(\partial_{2})]}+[\nabla_{R}(\partial_{1}),a(\partial_{2})]+[a(\partial_{1}),\nabla_{R}(\partial_{2})]

\displaystyle\ +[a(\partial_{1}),a(\partial_{2})]-\cancel{\nabla_{R}([\partial_{1},\partial_{2}])}-a([\partial_{1},\partial_{2}])

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ [\nabla_{R}(\partial_{1}),a(\partial_{2})]+[a(\partial_{1}),\nabla_{R}(\partial_{2})]+\cancel{[a(\partial_{1}),a(\partial_{2})]}-a([\partial_{1},\partial_{2}])

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ [\nabla_{R}(\partial_{1}),a(\partial_{2})]+[a(\partial_{1}),\nabla_{R}(\partial_{2})]-a([\partial_{1},\partial_{2}]),

where the third equality follows from the flatness assumption on
\nabla_{R}

and the last equality follows from the fact that
\mathrm{Im}(a)\subseteq M\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{R,G}/R}
and
M^{2}=0
.
In particular, the assignment
a\mapsto\psi_{\nabla_{R}+a}
is additive.
Hence, in order to prove (
348
), it suffices to consider the case where
a=\omega\otimes v
for
\omega\in\Omega_{X}
,
v\in M\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}
.
For
\partial_{1}
and
\partial_{2}
as above (and we occasionally regard them as local sections of
\mathcal{T}_{X}
via reduction via
\mathfrak{m}_{R}
),
the following sequence of equalities holds:

(351)

\displaystyle\ (\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}(2)})(\omega\otimes v)^{\triangleright}(\partial_{1},\partial_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ (d\omega\otimes v)^{\triangleright}(\partial_{1},\partial_{2})-(\omega\wedge(\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}})(v))^{\triangleright}(\partial_{1},\partial_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \left(\partial_{1}(\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial_{2}))-\partial_{2}(\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial_{1}))-\omega^{\triangleright}([\partial_{1},\partial_{2}])\right)\otimes v

\displaystyle\ -\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial_{1})\cdot(\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}})(v)^{\triangleright}(\partial_{2})+\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial_{2})\cdot(\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}})(v)^{\triangleright}(\partial_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \partial_{1}(\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial_{2}))\otimes v-\partial_{2}(\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial_{1}))\otimes v-\omega^{\triangleright}([\partial_{1},\partial_{2}])\otimes v-\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial_{1})\cdot[\nabla(\partial_{2}),v]

\displaystyle\ +\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial_{2})\cdot[\nabla(\partial_{2}),v]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \left(\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial_{2})\cdot[\nabla_{R}(\partial_{1}),v]+d_{\mathcal{E}_{R^{\prime},G}}(\nabla_{R}(\partial_{1}))(\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial_{2})\otimes v)\right)

\displaystyle\ -\left(\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial_{1})\cdot[\nabla_{R}(\partial_{2}),v]+d_{\mathcal{E}_{R^{\prime},G}}(\nabla_{R}(\partial_{2}))(\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial_{1})\otimes v)\right)-\omega^{\triangleright}([\partial_{1},\partial_{2}])\otimes v

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ [\nabla_{R}(\partial_{1}),\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial_{2})\otimes v]+[\omega^{\triangleright}(\partial_{1})\otimes v,\nabla_{R}(\partial_{2})]-\omega^{\triangleright}([\partial_{1},\partial_{2}])\otimes v

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{Eddw})}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\ \psi_{\nabla_{R}+\omega\otimes v}^{\triangleright}(\partial_{1},\partial_{2}),

where the fifth equality follows from the fact that
(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{R,G}/R},d_{\mathcal{E}_{R,G}})
forms a Lie algebroid.
This completes the proof of this lemma.
∎

Remark 6.5.4
.

We shall denote by

(352)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathcal{E}_{G},\nabla)}^{\flat}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{G},\nabla)}^{\flat}\right)

the subfunctor of
\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathcal{E}_{G},\nabla)}
(resp.,
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{G},\nabla)}^{\flat}
) introduced in (
340
) classifying flat deformations.
Note that the result of Lemma
6.5.3
holds without the assumption that
\mathcal{E}_{G}
has an
H
-reduction defining the structure of indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle, i.e.,
\mathcal{E}_{H}
.
Hence, this lemma implies that

(\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathcal{E}_{G},\nabla)}^{\flat},\mathbb{H}_{G,Z}^{1},\mathbb{H}_{G,Z}^{2})

(resp.,
(\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{G},\nabla)}^{\flat},\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{G,Z}^{1},\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{G,Z}^{2})
) forms a deformation triple.

6.6.
Deformation of dormant indigenous
(G,H)
-bundles

Next, suppose further that the indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
is dormant.

Definition 6.6.1
.

A deformation
\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit}
(resp.,
(X_{R},\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}_{R})
) of
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
(resp.,
(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})
) over
R\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k})
is called
dormant
if
\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit}
is dormant.

For each
R\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k})
, we shall denote by

(353)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Def}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}\!(R)\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathrm{Def}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}(R)\right)

the subset of
\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{\flat}(R)
(resp.,
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}^{\flat}(R)
) consisting of dormant deformations.
One may verify that the assignment
R\mapsto\mathrm{Def}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}\!(R)
(resp.,
R\mapsto\mathrm{Def}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}(R)
) defines a functor with gdt

(354)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Def}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathrm{Def}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}\right):\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}\rightarrow\mathfrak{S}\mathfrak{e}\mathfrak{t}.

Also, there exists a natural inclusion of functors
\mathrm{Def}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}\!\rightarrow\mathrm{Def}^{\flat}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}

(resp.,
\mathrm{Def}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}\rightarrow\mathrm{Def}^{\flat}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}
).

In what follows, let us describe the tangent and obstruction spaces of
\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\!
and
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
.
Note that the morphism
\nabla_{G,Z}^{\mathrm{ad}}
(resp.,
\nabla_{H,Z}^{\mathrm{ad}}
; resp.,
\widetilde{\nabla}_{H,Z}^{\mathrm{ad}}
) induces, via restricting the codomain, a morphism

(355)

\displaystyle\nabla_{G,B}^{\mathrm{ad}}:\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Im}(\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}})\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \nabla_{H,B}^{\mathrm{ad}}:\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Im}(\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}});\text{resp.,}\ \widetilde{\nabla}_{H,B}^{\mathrm{ad}}:\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Im}(\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}_{G})\right).\hskip-14.22636pt

For each
i\geq 0
, we shall write

(356)

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}^{i}_{G,B}:=\mathbb{H}^{i}(X,\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{G,B}^{\mathrm{ad}}]),\hskip 14.22636pt\mathbb{H}^{i}_{H,B}:=\mathbb{H}^{i}(X,\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{H,B}^{\mathrm{ad}}]),\hskip 14.22636pt\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{H,B}:=\mathbb{H}^{i}(X,\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{H,B}^{\mathrm{ad}}]).

The isomorphism
\eta^{\bullet}_{H,Z}:\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{H,Z}^{\mathrm{ad}}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{G,Z}^{\mathrm{ad}}]
is restricted to an isomorphism of complexes

\eta^{\bullet}_{H,B}:\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{H,B}^{\mathrm{ad}}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{G,B}^{\mathrm{ad}}]
.
By applying the functor
\mathbb{H}^{i}(X,-)
to this isomorphism for each
i\geq 0
,
we obtain an isomorphism of
k
-vector spaces

(357)

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}^{1}(\eta^{\bullet}_{H,B}):\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{H,B}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{H}^{i}_{G,B}.

Moreover,
the natural inclusion
\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}})[0]\hookrightarrow\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}_{G,B}]
is a quasi-isomorphism, so it yields an isomorphism

(358)

\displaystyle H^{i}(X,\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{H}^{i}_{G,B}.

Proposition 6.6.2
.

Recall that
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
is assumed to be dormant.

(i)

The collections

(\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\!,\mathbb{H}^{1}_{H,B},\mathbb{H}^{2}_{H,B})
and

(\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}},\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{H,B},\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{H,B})
(which is isomorphic to
(\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}},H^{1}(X,\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}})),H^{2}(X,\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}})))
by (
357
) and (
358
))
form deformation triples.

(ii)

Suppose that
X
is proper over
k
.
Then,
there exist pro-representable hulls for

\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\!
and
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
.

(iii)

Regarding the pro-representability of
\mathrm{Def}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}\!
(resp.,
\mathrm{Def}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}
),
the statement of Corollary
6.4.4
with
\mathbb{H}^{i}_{H}
’s (resp.,
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{H}
’s) replaced by
\mathbb{H}_{H,B}^{i}
’s (resp.,
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{H,B}^{i}
’s) holds for
\mathrm{Def}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}\!
(resp.,
\mathrm{Def}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}
).

Proof.

The assertions follow from Proposition
6.4.3
(and its proof) together with the following lemma.
∎

Lemma 6.6.3
.

Let us keep the notation in Lemma
6.5.3
and suppose further that the deformation
(X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\spadesuit})
is dormant and the
R
-connection
\nabla_{R}+a
is flat (or equivalently,
a\in\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}(2)})
by Lemma
6.5.3
).
Then, the
p
-curvature
{{}^{p}}\psi_{\nabla_{R}+a}
of
\nabla_{R}+a
is given by

(359)

\displaystyle{{}^{p}\psi}_{\nabla_{R}+a}=-(\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes C_{(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}},\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}})})(a)

(cf. (
421
) for the definition of
C_{(-,-)}
) under the natural inclusion relation

(360)

\displaystyle\Gamma(X,M\otimes F^{*}_{X}(\Omega_{X})\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}})\subseteq\Gamma(X_{R},F^{*}_{X_{R}}(\Omega_{X_{R}/R})\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{R,G}}).

In particular,

{{}^{p}}\psi_{\nabla_{R}+a}=0
if and only if
a
may be expressed, Zariski locally on
X
, as

a=\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}}(v)
for some local section
v\in\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
.

Proof.

Let us take a local section
\partial\in\mathcal{T}_{X_{R}/R}
, which will be
occasionally regarded as a local section of
\mathcal{T}_{X}
via reduction modulo
\mathfrak{m}_{R}
.
Observe
the following sequence of equalities in the enveloping algebra of the Lie algebroid
(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}},d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}})
:

(361)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \langle F_{X}^{-1}(\partial),-(\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes C_{(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}},\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}})})(a)\rangle

\displaystyle=-\langle F_{k}^{*}(\partial),(\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes C_{(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}},\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}})})(a)\rangle

\displaystyle=-\langle\partial^{[p]},a\rangle+(\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes(\nabla_{G,\partial}^{\mathrm{ad}})^{p-1})(\langle\partial,a\rangle)

\displaystyle=-\langle\partial^{[p]},a\rangle+(\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes\mathrm{ad}(\nabla(\partial))^{p-1})(\langle\partial,a\rangle)

\displaystyle=-\langle\partial^{[p]},a\rangle+\sum_{i=0}^{p-1}(-1)^{i}\cdot\begin{pmatrix}p-1\\
i\end{pmatrix}\cdot\nabla(\partial)^{p-1-i}\cdot\langle\partial,a\rangle\cdot\nabla(\partial)^{i}

\displaystyle=-\langle\partial^{[p]},a\rangle+\sum_{i=0}^{p-1}\nabla(\partial)^{p-1-i}\cdot\langle\partial,a\rangle\cdot\nabla(\partial)^{i},

where the second equality follows from (
422
).
On the other hand, we have

(362)

\displaystyle\ \langle F_{X}^{-1}(\partial),{{}^{p}}\psi_{\nabla_{R}+a}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ (\nabla_{R}+a)(\partial)^{[p]}-(\nabla_{R}+a)(\partial^{[p]})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ (\nabla_{R}(\partial)+\langle\partial,a\rangle)^{[p]}-\nabla_{R}(\partial^{[p]})-\langle\partial^{[p]},a\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \sum_{i=0}^{p-1}\nabla(\partial)^{p-1-i}\cdot\langle\partial,a\rangle\cdot\nabla(\partial)^{i}+\cancel{\nabla(\partial)^{[p]}-\nabla(\partial^{[p]})}-\langle\partial^{[p]},a\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ -\langle\partial^{[p]},a\rangle+\sum_{i=0}^{p-1}\nabla(\partial)^{p-1-i}\cdot\langle\partial,a\rangle\cdot\nabla(\partial)^{i},

where the last equality follows from the assumption
{{}^{p}}\psi_{\nabla}=0
.
Thus, (
361
) and (
362
) together show that

(363)

\displaystyle\langle F_{X}^{-1}(\partial),{{}^{p}}\psi_{\nabla_{R}+a}\rangle=\langle F_{X}^{-1}(\partial),-(\mathrm{id}_{M}\otimes C_{(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}},\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}})})(a)\rangle.

By considering this equality for every
\partial\in\mathcal{T}_{X}
,
we obtain (
359
).
This completes the proof of the former assertion.
The latter assertion follows from
the former assertion and the commutativity of (
7.3
).
∎

Remark 6.6.4
.

If the underlying space
X
is a curve, then the above lemma was (essentially) proved in

[
54
, Chap. 6, § 6.4, Proposition 6.10]
.

Example 6.6.5

(Dormant indigenous
(\mathrm{PGL}_{2},\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{\circledcirc})
-bundles on a curve)
.

Let
X
be a smooth proper curve over
k
of genus
g>1
and
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)
be a dormant indigenous
(\mathrm{PGL}_{2},\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{\circledcirc})
-bundle on
X/k
.
We know that
H^{i}(X,\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}}))=0
for
i=0,2
and
\mathrm{dim}_{k}(H^{1}(X,\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}})))=3g-3
(cf.
[
54
, Chap. 6, § 6.2, Proposition 6.4, and § 6.5, Proposition 6.17]
).
Hence, by Proposition
6.6.2
, (iii), the deformation functor
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
of
(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})
may be pro-representable by
k[\![x_{1},\cdots,x_{3g-3}]\!]
.
This result is consistent with the fact that the moduli stack classifying such curves equipped with a dormant indigenous bundle
is
smooth and of dimension
3g-3
(cf.
[
40
, Chap. II, § 2.3, Theorem 2.8]
).

6.7.
Ordinariness of dormant indigenous
(G,H)
-bundles

In this subsection, we introduce the notion of ordinariness for dormant indigenous
(G,H)
-bundles (of level
1
) and prove a related lifting property of the underlying space.
(However, the discussion and results here will not be used after this subsection.)
Suppose that
G
is affine, and
we shall fix
a dormant indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)
on
X/k
.
Write
\mathcal{E}_{G}:=\mathcal{E}_{H}\times^{H}G
, and denote by
\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla}
the
G
-bundle on
X^{(1)}
corresponding to the
p
-flat
G
-bundle
(\mathcal{E}_{G},\nabla)
via (
483
).
In particular, there exists an isomorphism of
G
-bundles
F_{X/k}^{*}(\mathcal{E}^{\nabla}_{G})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{E}_{G}
, which induces an isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-Lie algebras
F^{*}_{X/k}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
.
For each
l\geq 0
,
we obtain the
k
-linear morphism

(364)

\displaystyle H^{l}(\varphi):H^{l}(X^{(1)},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla}})\rightarrow H^{l}(X,\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}/\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}})

induced by the composite of natural morphisms

\varphi:F_{X/k}^{-1}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla}})\left(=\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}_{G})\right)\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}/\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}
.

Definition 6.7.1
.

We shall say that

\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
(or
(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})
) is
ordinary

if

H^{1}(\varphi)
is an isomorphism and
H^{2}(\varphi)
is injective.

The ordinariness just defined is closed related to the existence of a deformation of
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
, as described in the following proposition.

Proposition 6.7.2
.

Suppose that

\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
is ordinary.
Then,
the restriction

(365)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}:\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Def}_{X}

of
\delta_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}
(cf. (
312
)) to
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}

is a semi-isomorphism.
In particular, for a small extension in
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}
of the form
0\rightarrow M\rightarrow R\rightarrow k\rightarrow 0
and a deformation
X_{R}
of
X
over
R
, there exists a unique (up to isomorphism) deformation of
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
defined over
X_{R}
.
If, moreover,
\mathrm{Def}_{X}
is rigid, then
this morphism is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Observe that the following diagram is commutative (cf. the commutativity of (
6.4
)):

\textstyle{\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{H,B}^{\mathrm{ad}}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{inclusion}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{\bullet}_{H,B}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{H}^{\mathrm{ad}}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(d_{\mathcal{E}_{H}},0)}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{\bullet}_{H}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{X}[0]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{KS}_{(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)}}
\textstyle{F_{X/k}^{-1}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla}})[0]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{inclusion}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{G,B}^{\mathrm{ad}}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{inclusion}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}_{G}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}/\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}[0],}

where the rightmost lower horizontal arrow is given by the pair of the natural quotient
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}/\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}
and the zero map
\Omega_{X}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}\rightarrow 0
.
For each
i\geq 0
, denote by
\delta_{H,B}^{i}
the morphism
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{H,B}\rightarrow H^{i}
induced by the composite of the two upper horizontal arrows in this diagram.
The composite of all the lower horizontal arrows coincides with
\varphi
.
Hence, since
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
is ordinary and (
358
) is an isomorphism,
the commutativity of (
6.7
) implies that

\delta_{H,B}^{1}
is an isomorphism and
\delta_{H,B}^{2}
is injective.
On the other hand,
by the definition of
\delta_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\!
,
the triple

(\delta_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}},\delta^{1}_{H,B},\delta^{2}_{H,B})
forms a morphism of deformation triples
(\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}},\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{H,B},\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{H,B})\rightarrow(\mathrm{Def}_{X},H^{1},H^{2})
(cf. Proposition
6.6.2
, (i)).
Thus, the assertion follows from Propositions
6.1.5
, (i), and
6.1.7
.
∎

Example 6.7.3
.

In the case where
(G,H)=(\mathrm{PGL}_{2},\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{\circledcirc})
and
X
is a smooth proper curve over
k
,
essentially the same notion of ordinariness as above can be found in
[
51
, § 3, Definition 3.2]
.
To verify the ordinariness of a given dormant indigenous
(\mathrm{PGL}_{2},\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{\circledcirc})
-bundle
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
,
we only have to consider whether
H^{1}(\varphi)
is an isomorphism or not because of the assumption
\mathrm{dim}(X)=1
(which implies
H^{2}(\varphi)=0
).
Hence, it follows from the discussion in the proof of Proposition
6.7.2
that
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
is ordinary if and only if the differential
d\delta_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
of
\delta_{\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
is an isomorphism.

Now, denote by
\mathfrak{M}_{g}
the moduli stack classifying smooth proper curves over
k
of genus
g
and by

\mathfrak{M}_{g}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\!
the moduli stack classifying pairs
(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})

consisting of such a curve
X

and a dormant indigenous
(\mathrm{PGL}_{2},\mathrm{PGL}_{2}^{\circledcirc})
-bundle
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
on
X
.
According to a result of S. Mochizuki (cf.
[
40
, Chap. II, § 2.3, Theorem 2.8]
),
the natural projection
\mathfrak{M}_{g}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\!\rightarrow\mathfrak{M}_{g}
is finite and faithfully flat, and moreover the étale locus in
\mathfrak{M}_{g}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\!
relative to this projection forms a dense open substack.
On the other hand, the above discussion implies that a pair
(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})
classified by
\mathfrak{M}_{g}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}

is ordinary if and only if the natural projection
\mathfrak{M}_{g}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\!\rightarrow\mathfrak{M}_{g}
is étale at the classifying point of
(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})
.
Consequencely, every general pair
(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})
turns out to be ordinary.

The following assertion gives another example of an ordinary dormant indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle.

Proposition 6.7.4
.

Let
X
be
an ordinary Abelian variety of dimension
n>0
.
Then, any dormant indigenous
(\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\times n},\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\times n})
-bundle
on
X/k
is ordinary.

Proof.

For convenience, we write
G:=\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\times n}
and
H=\{e\}
.
Let
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)
be a dormant indigenous
(G,G)
-bundle
on
X/k
, and write
\mathcal{E}_{G}:=\mathcal{E}_{H}\times^{H}G
.
By Proposition
4.7.2
, (i) (and the bijection
\zeta^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}
asserted in Theorem
A
),

\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
specifies an element
(\omega_{1}^{+},\cdots,\omega_{n}^{+})
of
B_{X/k}^{(1),n,0}
, where
\omega_{i}^{+}:=(\omega_{i},\mathcal{L}_{i},\eta_{i})
(
i=1,\cdots,n
).
If we regard
\nabla_{\mathcal{L}_{i}}^{\mathrm{can}(0)}
(for each
i
) as a
k
-connection on
\mathcal{O}_{X}
via
\eta_{i}
,
then the flat
G
-bundle
(\mathcal{E}_{G},\nabla)
arises from the flat vector bundle
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(\mathcal{O}_{X},\nabla_{\mathcal{L}_{i}}^{\mathrm{can}(0)})
.
In particular, we obtain a natural identification
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}=\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus n}
.
Next, since the sections
\omega_{1},\cdots,\omega_{n}
are mutually distinct,
any locally defined endomorphism
h
of
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}(\mathcal{O}_{X},\nabla_{\mathcal{L}_{i}}^{\mathrm{can}(0)})

may be expressed as
\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{mult}_{u_{i}}
, where each
\mathrm{mult}_{u_{i}}
denotes the locally defined endomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
given by multiplication by a local section
u_{i}
of
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(1)}}
(
\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{X}
via
F_{X/k}
).
The assignment
h\mapsto(u_{1},\cdots,u_{n})
gives
an identification
\left(\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}})=\right)\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla}}=\mathcal{O}_{X^{(1)}}^{\oplus n}
.
Under the identifications
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}=\mathcal{O}_{X}^{\oplus n}
,
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla}}=\mathcal{O}_{X^{(1)}}^{\oplus n}
obtained so far,

H^{l}(\varphi)
(for each
l
) coincides with the direct sum of
n
copies of
the morphism
H^{l}(X^{(1)},\mathcal{O}_{X^{(1)}})\rightarrow H^{l}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
induced by
F_{X/k}
.
Hence, since
X
is assumed to be ordinary,
this morphism turns out to be an isomorphism, meaning that
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
is ordinary.
This completes the proof of the assertion.
∎

6.8.
Deformation of dormant indigenous
(G,H)
-bundles of higher level

In the rest of this section, we suppose that
G
is affine and fix a positive integer
N
.
Suppose that the base field
k
is equipped with the trivial
(N-1)
-PD structure.

Denote by
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD}}
the category of

(N-1)
-PD rings
(R,\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\gamma)
such that
R
is a local Artinian
k
-algebras with residue field
k
.
Also, denote by
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD},\circledast}

the full subcategory of
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD}}

consisting of
(N-1)
-PD rings of the form

(R,\mathfrak{m}_{R},\mathfrak{m}_{R},\gamma_{R})
for some PD structure
\gamma_{R}
on
\mathfrak{m}_{R}
.
(In particular,
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD},\circledast}
does not depend on
N
.)
If there is no fear of confusion,
we shall often abuse notation by just writing
R
for
(R,\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\gamma)
or
(R,\mathfrak{m}_{R},\mathfrak{m}_{R},\gamma_{R})
.
The discussions conducted so far will work even when we replace all “
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}
”’s appearing there by either

\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD}}
or
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD},\circledast}
.
To verify this, the following three observations should be noted:

(i)

The
k
-algebra
k_{\epsilon}
lies in
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD},\circledast}
(hence, in
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD}}
) because its maximal ideal
(\epsilon)\left(=\mathfrak{m}_{k_{\epsilon}}\right)
has evidently an
(N-1)
-PD structure (and it is uniquely determined);

(ii)

The categories
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD}}
,
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD},\circledast}

admit fiber products.
To be precise,
for any morphisms
R^{\prime}\rightarrow R
,
R^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow R
in either
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD}}
or

\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD},\circledast}
,
the fiber product
R^{\prime}\times_{R}R^{\prime\prime}
belongs to the same category because it has the
(N-1)
-PD ideal induced from
the
(N-1)
-PD ideals of

R
,
R^{\prime}
, and
R^{\prime\prime}
;

(iii)

If
(R,\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\gamma)
is an object
of either
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD}}
or

\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD},\circledast}
,
then
\gamma
extends to any deformation of
X
over
R
(cf.
[
3
, § 1.3, Proposition 1.3.8, (iv)]
).

By these facts, it makes sense to speak of various deformation functors on
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD}}
or
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD},\circledast}
.
In what follows, let
\Box
denote either the absence or presence of “
\circledast
”.

Now, let

\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})
be a dormant indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle of level
N
on
X/k
.
Denote by
\nabla
the
k
-connection on
\mathcal{E}_{G}:=\mathcal{E}_{H}\times^{H}G
defining
\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit}|^{\langle 1\rangle}
via (
480
).
In particular,
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)
specifies a dormant indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle, and various discussions conducted so far can be applied to this
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}
.
Just as in the case of indigenous
(G,H)
-bundles,
we have the notion of a (dormant) deformation of
\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit}
(resp.,
(X,\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit})
) over each
R\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD},\Box})
.
Also, an isomorphism between such objects can be defined.
For each
R\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD},\Box}_{/k})
, we shall set

(370)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}(R):=\left(\begin{matrix}\text{the set of isomorphism classes of}\\
\text{dormant deformations of $\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit}$ over $R$}\end{matrix}\right)\hskip 39.83385pt

\displaystyle\left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit})}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}(R):=\left(\begin{matrix}\text{the set of isomorphism classes of}\\
\text{dormant deformations of $(X,\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit})$ over $R$}\end{matrix}\right)\right).

The assignments
R\mapsto\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}(R)
(resp.,
R\mapsto\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit})}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}(R)
)
defines a functor with g.d.t

(371)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit})}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\right):\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD},\Box}_{/k}\rightarrow\mathfrak{S}\mathfrak{e}\mathfrak{t}.

We shall describe the tangent and obstruction spaces of
\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
and
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit})}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
.
Denote by
\mathcal{E}^{\nabla}_{G}
(cf. (
474
)) the
G
-bundle on
X^{(N)}

corresponding to
\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}
via
(
475
).
In particular, there exists a natural isomorphism of
G
-bundles

F^{(N)*}_{X/k}(\mathcal{E}^{\nabla}_{G})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{E}_{G}
, which induces an isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-Lie algebras

F^{(N)*}_{X/k}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
.
Both
\mathfrak{h}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}
(resp.,
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{H}}
) and

\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla}}
may be regarded as subsheaves of
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
(resp.,
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
).

Lemma 6.8.1
.

The composite
\widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{G}\circ\nabla:\mathcal{T}_{X}\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
coincides with the zero map.

Proof.

Let us take local sections
\partial
,
\partial^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}_{X}
.
Then, we have

(372)

\displaystyle\langle\partial,(\widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{G}\circ\nabla)(\partial^{\prime})\rangle=

\displaystyle\ \widetilde{\nabla}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\partial}(\nabla(\partial^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ [\nabla(\partial),\nabla(\partial^{\prime})]-\nabla([\partial,d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}(\nabla(\partial^{\prime}))])

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ [\nabla(\partial),\nabla(\partial^{\prime})]-\nabla([\partial,\partial^{\prime}])

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ 0,

where
\langle-,-\rangle
denotes the natural pairing
\mathcal{T}_{X}\times(\Omega_{X}\otimes\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}})\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
and the last equality follows from the flatness of
\nabla
.
This completes the proof of this lemma.
∎

For each
i\geq 0
, we shall set
\mathbb{H}^{i}_{H,B,N}
(resp.,
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{i}_{H,B,N}
) to be the
k
-vector space making the following square diagram cartesian:

\displaystyle\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern
