where
the right-hand vertical arrow arises from the inclusion
\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{H,B}^{\mathrm{ad}}]\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}}]
(resp.,
\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{H,B}^{\mathrm{ad}}]\rightarrow\mathcal{K}^{\bullet}[\widetilde{\nabla}_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}}]
) and
the lower horizontal arrow arises from the inclusion
(F^{(N)}_{X/k})^{-1}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla}})\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
(resp., both
\nabla
and the inclusion
(F^{(N)}_{X/k})^{-1}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla}})\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
).
Then, the following assertion holds.

Proposition 6.8.2
.

Recall the assumption that
G
is affine and
\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit}
is dormant.

(i)

The collection
(\mathrm{Def}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit}},\mathbb{H}_{H,B,N}^{1},\mathbb{H}_{H,B,N}^{2})
(resp.,
(\mathrm{Def}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit})},\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{H,B,N}^{1},\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{H,B,N}^{2})
)
form a deformation triple.
Moreover, the group of infinitesimal automorphism of
\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit}
(resp.,
(X,\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit})
)
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{H}_{H,B,N}^{0}
(resp.,
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{H,B,N}^{0}
).

(ii)

Suppose that
X
is proper over
k
.
Then,
there exist pro-representable hulls for

\mathrm{Def}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit}}
and
\mathrm{Def}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit})}
.

(iii)

Regarding the pro-representability of
\mathrm{Def}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit}}

(resp.,
\mathrm{Def}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit})}
),
the statement of Corollary
6.4.4
with
\mathbb{H}^{i}_{H}
’s (resp.,
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{H}^{i}
’s) replaced by
\mathbb{H}_{H,B,N}^{i}
(resp.,
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{H,B,N}^{i}
)
holds for
\mathrm{Def}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit}}

(resp.,
\mathrm{Def}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit})}
).

Proof.

We only consider the resp’d assertion of (i) because the proofs of the others follow from this assertion (and its proof).
For each
R\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD},\Box})
, denote by
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla})}(R)
the set of isomorphism classes of pairs
(X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{G,R}^{\nabla})
consisting of a deformation
X_{R}
of
X
over
R
and a deformation
\mathcal{E}_{R,G}^{\nabla}
of the
G
-bundle
\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla}
over
X_{R}^{(1)}
.
The assignment
R\mapsto\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla})}(R)
is verified to define a functor with gdt
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla})}:\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD},\Box}_{/k}\rightarrow\mathfrak{S}\mathfrak{e}\mathfrak{t}
.
If
F_{X/k}^{(N)\sharp}
denotes the morphism of functors given by
X_{R}\mapsto X_{R}^{(N)}
, then
the following square diagram of functors is cartesian:

\textstyle{\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{R,G}^{\nabla})\mapsto X_{R}}
\scriptstyle{(X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{R,G}^{\nabla})\mapsto(X_{R}^{(N)},\mathcal{E}_{R,G}^{\nabla})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Def}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F_{X/k}^{(N)\sharp}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Def}_{(X^{(N)},\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(X_{R}^{(N)},\mathcal{E}_{R,G}^{\nabla})\mapsto X_{R}^{(N)}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Def}_{X^{(N)}}}

(cf. (
340
) for the definition of
\mathrm{Def}_{(X^{(N)},\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla})}
).
For each
i=0,1,2
,
let us write
\mathbb{H}_{G,\nabla}^{i}
for the limit of the diagram

\textstyle{H^{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H^{i}(X^{(N)},\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{H^{i}(d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla}})}
\textstyle{H^{i}(X^{(N)},\mathcal{T}_{X^{(N)}}),}

where the right-hand vertical arrow is the morphism arising from
F_{X/k}^{(N)\sharp}
.
But,
the right-hand vertical arrow
becomes the zero map by the definition of Frobenius twist, so
this diagram induces a natural isomorphism

(389)

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}_{G,\nabla}^{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}H^{i}(X^{(N)},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla}})\oplus H^{i}\left(=\mathrm{Ker}(H^{i}(d_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}))\oplus H^{i}\right).

It follows from the definitions of
\mathbb{H}^{i}_{G,\nabla}
and (
6.8
) that
the tangent space of

\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla})}
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{H}_{G,\nabla}^{1}
, and this functor has an obstruction theory
by putting
\mathbb{H}_{G,\nabla}^{2}
as the obstruction space.
Moreover, the space of infinitesimal automorphisms of
(X,\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla})
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{H}_{G,\nabla}^{0}
.
On the other hand, the equivalence of categories (
475
) gives rise to the following cartesian diagram:

\textstyle{\mathrm{Def}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{R,N}^{\spadesuit})\mapsto(X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{R,N}^{\spadesuit}|^{\langle 1\rangle})}
\scriptstyle{(X_{R},(\mathcal{E}_{R,H},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}_{R}))\mapsto(X_{R},\mathcal{E}^{\nabla}_{R,G})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(X_{R},(\mathcal{E}_{R,H},\nabla_{R}))\mapsto(X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{R,G},\nabla)}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(X_{R},\mathcal{G})\mapsto(X_{R},F^{(N)*}_{X_{R}/R}(\mathcal{G}),\nabla_{\mathcal{G}}^{\mathrm{can}(N)})}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{G},\nabla)}}

(cf. (
340
) for the definition of
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{G},\nabla)}
).
Under the identification
\mathbb{H}^{i}_{G,\nabla}=H^{i}(X^{(N)},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}^{\nabla}_{G}})\oplus H^{i}
given by (
389
),
the differential of the lower horizontal arrow coincides with the morphism

H^{i}(X^{(N)},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}^{\nabla}_{G}})\oplus H^{i}\rightarrow\mathbb{H}_{G}^{i}
arising from

(F_{X/k}^{(N)})^{-1}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}^{\nabla}_{G}})\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
and

\nabla:\mathcal{T}_{X}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}}
(cf. Lemma
6.8.1
).
Thus, we can conclude the required assertion by combining (
6.8
) and (
6.8
).
∎

Remark 6.8.3
.

Similar to the fact mentioned in Remark
6.5.4
,
the tangent and obstruction spaces of
the deformation functor of higher level
can be described in terms of cohomology groups.
With
the above notation,
we shall denote by

(394)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathcal{E}_{G},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}

the functor classifying dormant deformations of the
(N-1)
-PD stratified
G
-bundle
(\mathcal{E}_{G},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})
.
Then,
the proof of the above proposition shows
that

(\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathcal{E}_{G},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}},H^{1}(X^{(N)},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla}}),H^{2}(X^{(N)},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla}}))
forms a deformation triple, and
the group of infinitesimal automorphisms of
(\mathcal{E}_{G},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})
is isomorphic to
H^{0}(X^{(N)},\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla}})
.

If
N=1
, then
\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathcal{E}_{G},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
is naturally isomorphic to the subfunctor
\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathcal{E}_{G},\nabla)}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
of
\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathcal{E}_{G},\nabla)}
classifying deformations with vanishing
p
-curvature.
Since
F_{X/k}^{-1}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}^{\nabla}_{G}})=\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{\mathrm{ad}}_{G})
,
the tangent and obstruction spaces of this functor are isomorphic to

H^{i}(X,\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla_{G}^{\mathrm{ad}}))
(
\cong\mathbb{H}_{G,B}^{i}
by (
358
)) for
i=1
and
2
respectively.
This fact will be used in the proof of Theorem
6.9.1
.

6.9.
The Ehresmann-Weil-Thurston principle in positive characteristic

Let us keep the assumption in the previous subsection, and let

\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
be an
F^{N}
-
(G,P)
-structures on
X/k
.
Denote by

(395)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit})}:\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD},\circledast}_{/k}\rightarrow\mathfrak{S}\mathfrak{e}\mathfrak{t}

the deformation functor of the pair
(X,\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit})
.
That is to say, for each
R\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD},\circledast}_{/k})
,

\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit})}(R)
is defined as the set of pairs
(X_{R},\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}_{R})
consisting of (an isomorphism class of) a deformation
X_{R}
of
X
over
R
and an
F^{N}
-
(G,P)
-structure
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}_{R}
on
X_{R}/R
inducing
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
via reduction modulo
\mathfrak{m}_{R}
.
The pull-back
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit})}(R^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit})}(R)
induced by every morphism
R^{\prime}\rightarrow R
can be formulated by applying the discussion in §
2.4
, and thus we obtain the functor
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit})}
.

On the other hand,
denote by
\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})

the dormant indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle of level
N
corresponding to
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
, and moreover, denote by

\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}
the dormant
(N-1)
-crystal of
G
-bundles associated with
(\mathcal{E}_{G},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})
(cf. Proposition
7.6.2
, Definition
7.7.1
, (ii)).
Also. we shall denote by

(396)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}:\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD},\circledast}\rightarrow\mathfrak{S}\mathfrak{e}\mathfrak{t}

the functor
classifying, for each
R\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD},\circledast})
, dormant
(N-1)
-crystals on
\mathrm{Cris}^{(N-1)}(X/R)
inducing
\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}
via reduction modulo
\mathfrak{m}_{R}
.
Then, the bijection

\zeta_{N}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}
obtained in Theorem
B
(resp., the equivalence of categories (
469
))
induces an isomorphism (resp., a morphism) of functors

(397)

\displaystyle\mu_{N}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}:\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit})}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\ \left(\text{resp.,}\ \mu_{N}^{\spadesuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit}:\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}_{N})}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Def}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}_{\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}}\right).

In particular,

\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit})}
is a functor with gdt and has the same tangent and obstruction spaces as

\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit})}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
(cf. Proposition
6.8.2
, (i)).
The compose of
\mu_{N}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}
and
\mu_{N}^{\spadesuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit}

defines a morphism of functors

(398)

\displaystyle\mu_{N}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit}:\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit})}\rightarrow\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}.

In other words,
\mu_{N}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit}
is the morphism given by taking the monodromy crystals of
F^{N}
-
(G,P)
-structures
(cf. Definition
4.5.7
).
As explained in the Introduction, the following theorem, i.e., Theorem
D
, may be regarded as a positive characteristic version of the Ehresmann-Weil-Thurston principle.

Theorem 6.9.1
.

The morphism
\mu_{N}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit}
is a semi-isomorphism.
If, moreover,

\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
is rigid, then

\mu_{N}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Since
\mu_{N}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\spadesuit}
is an isomorphism,
it suffices to prove the same assertion with
\mu_{N}^{\heartsuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit}
replaced by
\mu_{N}^{\spadesuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit}
.
Moreover, we only consider the former assertion because the latter assertion follows directly from the former assertion and Proposition
6.1.5
, (i).

First, we shall prove
the case of
N=1
.
Denote by

\nabla
the
k
-connection on
\mathcal{E}_{G}:=\mathcal{E}_{H}\times^{H}G
corresponding to
\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}
(cf. (
482
)), which determines a dormant indigenous
(G,H)
-bundle
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{H},\nabla)
.
By the equivalence of categories (
482
),
\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
may be identified with
the deformation functor
\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathcal{E}_{G},\nabla)}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}

introduced in Remark
6.8.3
.
Also, there exists a canonical identification
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{1}^{\spadesuit})}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}=\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
arising from (
239
).
Under these identifications,

\mu_{1}^{\spadesuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit}
defines a morphism of functors
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathcal{E}_{G},\nabla)}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}
.
By combining Lemmas
6.9.3
, (ii), and
6.9.4
described below,
we see that if
the differential
d\mu_{1}^{\spadesuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit}

is regarded as
a morphism
\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{H,B}\rightarrow\mathbb{H}^{1}_{G,B}
according to the results of Proposition
6.6.2
, (i), and Remark
6.8.3
,
then it
coincides with
\mathbb{H}^{1}(\eta_{H,B}^{\bullet})
(cf. (
357
)).
Moreover, it follows from various definitions involved and the discussion in the proof of Lemma
6.9.3
, (i), that
the triple

(399)

\displaystyle(\mu_{1}^{\spadesuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit},\mathbb{H}^{1}(\eta_{H,B}^{\bullet}),\mathbb{H}^{2}(\eta_{H,B}^{\bullet})):(\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit})}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}},\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{H,B},\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{2}_{H,B})\rightarrow(\mathrm{Def}_{(\mathcal{E}_{G},\nabla)}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}},\mathbb{H}^{1}_{G,B},\mathbb{H}_{G,B}^{2})

forms an isomorphism of deformation triples.
Since
\mathbb{H}^{i}(\eta^{\bullet}_{H,B})
(
i\geq 0
) are isomorphisms,
the morphism
\mu_{1}^{\spadesuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit}

turns out to be a semi-isomorphism (cf. Proposition
6.1.7
).

Next, we shall consider the case of an arbitrary positive integer
N
.
Let us verify the formal smoothness of
\mu_{N}^{\spadesuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit}
.
Take a small extension
e:=(0\rightarrow M\rightarrow R\xrightarrow{\sigma}R_{0}\rightarrow 0)
in
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD},\circledast}
.
In the following, we shall prove the surjectivity of the map

(400)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit})}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}(R)\rightarrow\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit})}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}(R_{0})\times_{\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}(R_{0})}\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}(R)

arising from
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E}_{N}^{\spadesuit})}^{{}^{\mathrm{Zzz...}}}(\sigma)
and
\mu_{N}^{\spadesuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit}(R_{0})
.
Suppose that we are given
a dormant deformation
(X_{R_{0}},\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}_{R_{0},N})
(where
\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}_{R_{0},N}:=(\mathcal{E}_{R_{0},H},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}_{R_{0}})
and we write
\mathcal{E}_{R_{0},G}:=\mathcal{E}_{R_{0},H}\times^{H}G
) of
(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}_{N})
over
R_{0}

and an
(N-1)
-crystal of
G
-bundles
\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\diamondsuit}
on
X/R
whose reduction modulo
M

is isomorphic to
the
(N-1)
-crystal on
X/R_{0}
corresponding to

(\mathcal{E}_{R_{0},G},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}_{R_{0}})
(cf. Proposition
7.6.2
).
By the formal smoothness of
\mu_{1}^{\spadesuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit}
proved above,
there exists a deformation
(X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{R,1}^{\spadesuit})
(where
\mathcal{E}_{R,1}^{\spadesuit}:=(\mathcal{E}_{R,H},\varepsilon_{R,1}^{\blacklozenge})
) of
(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}_{N}|^{\langle 1\rangle})
over
R
which induces
\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\diamondsuit}|^{(0)}
via
\mu_{1}^{\spadesuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit}
and induces
(X_{R_{0}},\mathcal{E}_{R_{0},N}^{\spadesuit}|^{\langle 1\rangle})
via reduction modulo
M
.
Then,
\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\diamondsuit}
determines a unique (up to isomorphism)
(N-1)
-PD stratification
\varepsilon_{R,N}^{\blacklozenge}
on
\mathcal{E}_{R,G}:=\mathcal{E}_{R,H}\times^{H}G

extending
\varepsilon_{R,1}^{\blacklozenge}
(cf. Proposition
7.6.2
).
The resulting collection
(X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{R,H},\varepsilon_{R,N}^{\blacklozenge})
forms a deformation of
(X,\mathcal{E}^{\spadesuit}_{N})
over
R
inducing
\mathcal{E}_{R}^{\diamondsuit}
via
\mu_{N}^{\spadesuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit}
and
(X_{R_{0}},\mathcal{E}_{R_{0},N}^{\spadesuit})
via reduction modulo
M
.
This implies that (
400
) is surjective.

Also, the bijectivity of
d\mu_{N}^{\spadesuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit}
follows from an entirely similar argument together with the bijectivity of
d\mu_{1}^{\spadesuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit}
.
Thus, we conclude that
\mu_{N}^{\spadesuit\Rightarrow\diamondsuit}
is a semi-isomorphism, and this completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

The following corollary follows from the above theorem and the comment in Remark
6.8.3
.

Corollary 6.9.2
.

Let us keep the above notation.
Suppose further that
X
is proper over
k
and
H^{0}(X,\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla}})=0
.
Then, the functor
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit})}
is pro-representable.
If, moreover,
H^{2}(X,\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla}})=0
, then

\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit})}
may be pro-represented by
k[\![x_{1},\cdots,x_{d}]\!]
, where
d:=\mathrm{dim}_{k}(H^{1}(X,\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}_{G}^{\nabla}}))
.

Now, let us describe two lemmas, that were
used to conclude Theorem
6.9.1
.
Let
(\mathcal{E}_{G},\nabla)
be as in the proof of that theorem, but we write
\mathcal{E}:=\mathcal{E}_{G}
for simplicity.
For each deformation

(X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{R},\nabla_{R})

of
(X,\mathcal{E},\nabla)
over

R\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD},\circledast})
,
we shall denote by

(401)

\displaystyle\Xi(X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{R},\nabla_{R})

the element of
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E},\nabla)}(R)
(cf. (
340
)) represented by this deformation.
Also,
for each quasi-nilpotent flat
G
-bundle
(\mathcal{E}_{R},\nabla_{R})
on a deformation of
X
over
R\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD},\circledast})
, we shall denote by
(\mathcal{E}_{R},\nabla_{R})^{\diamondsuit}

(cf. (
485
)) the
0
-crystal of
G
-bundles on
X/R
corresponding to
(\mathcal{E}_{R},\nabla_{R})
via (
469
) and (
481
).

Lemma 6.9.3
.

Let us fix
a small extension

e:=(0\rightarrow M\rightarrow R\rightarrow R_{0}\rightarrow 0)
in
\mathfrak{A}\mathfrak{r}\mathfrak{t}_{/k}^{N\text{-}\mathrm{PD},\circledast}
and
a flat deformation
(X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{R},\nabla_{R})
of
(X,\mathcal{E},\nabla)
over
R
. (As mentioned in Remark
7.3.1
, the connection
\nabla_{R}
is automatically quasi-nilpotent because of the
p
-flatness of
\nabla
.)

(i)

Let
v
be an element of
M\otimes H^{1}
and denote by

X^{\prime}_{R}
the deformation of
X
over
R
defined as the translation by
v
of the element in
\mathrm{Def}_{X}(R)
represented by
X_{R}
.
Also, denote by
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{R},\nabla^{\prime}_{R})
a flat
G
-bundle on
X^{\prime}_{R}/R
with

(\mathcal{E}_{R},\nabla_{R})^{\diamondsuit}\cong(\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{R},\nabla^{\prime}_{R})^{\diamondsuit}
(which always exists and is uniquely determined up to isomorphism according Proposition
7.8.3
).
Then, the following equality in
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E},\nabla)}(R)
holds:

(402)

\displaystyle\Xi(X^{\prime}_{R},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{R},\nabla^{\prime}_{R})=\Xi(X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{R},\nabla_{R})+\mathbb{H}^{1}(\nabla)(v),

where
\mathbb{H}^{1}(\nabla)
denotes the morphism
H^{1}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{G}
induced by
\nabla
(cf. Lemma
6.8.1
).

(ii)

Let
u
be an element of
M\otimes\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{G,Z}^{1}\left(\subseteq M\otimes\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{G}^{1}\right)
.
Denote by
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}_{R},\nabla^{\prime\prime}_{R})
a unique (up to isomorphism) quasi-nilpotent flat
G
-bundle on
X_{R}/R
such that

(\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}_{R},\nabla^{\prime\prime}_{R})^{\diamondsuit}

is isomorphic to the
0
-crystal associated with the quasi-nilpotent flat
G
-bundle representing
the element
\Xi(X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{R},\nabla_{R})+u
of
\mathrm{Def}_{(X,\mathcal{E},\nabla)}(R)
.
Then, the following equality holds:

(403)

\displaystyle\Xi(X_{R},\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}_{R},\nabla^{\prime\prime}_{R})=\Xi(X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{R},\nabla_{R})+\mathbb{H}^{1}(\eta^{\bullet}_{G,Z})(u)

(cf. (
347
) for the definition of
\mathbb{H}^{1}(\eta^{\bullet}_{G,Z})
).

Proof.

First, we shall consider assertion (i).
We can find an open covering
\mathcal{U}:=\{U_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in I}
of
X
and
v
may be represented by a Čech
1
-cocycle
\{\partial_{\alpha\beta}\}_{(\alpha,\beta)\in I_{2}}
in
\check{C}^{1}(\mathcal{U},M\otimes\mathcal{T}_{X})
(cf. the proof of Proposition
6.4.3
for the related notations).
Write
U_{R,\alpha}:=X_{R}|_{U_{\alpha}}
,
\mathcal{E}_{R,\alpha}:=\mathcal{E}_{R}|_{U_{\alpha}}
(for each
\alpha\in I
) and

U_{R,\alpha\beta}:=X_{R}|_{U_{\alpha\beta}}
,
\mathcal{E}_{R,\alpha\beta}:=\mathcal{E}_{R}|_{U_{\alpha\beta}}
(for each
(\alpha,\beta)\in I_{2}
).
Given each
(\alpha,\beta)\in I_{2}
,
we shall write
\partial_{\alpha\beta}^{\sharp}
(resp.,
\widetilde{\partial}^{\sharp}_{\alpha\beta}
) for the
R
-automorphism
of
U_{R,\alpha\beta}
(resp.,
\mathcal{E}_{R,\alpha\beta}
) determined by the automorphism
\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{O}_{U_{R,\alpha\beta}}}+\partial_{\alpha\beta}

(resp.,
\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{R,\alpha\beta}}}+\nabla(\partial_{\alpha\beta})
)
of
\mathcal{O}_{U_{R,\alpha\beta}}
(resp.,
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}_{R,\alpha\beta}}
).
Let us regard
\partial_{\alpha\beta}^{\sharp}
as a morphism of
0
-PD thickenings
in
\mathrm{Cris}^{(0)}(\overline{X}/S)
.
Then, according to the latter half of the discussion in Remark
7.6.3
and the discussion at the beginning of §
7.8
,
the induced isomorphism
\rho_{\partial^{\sharp}_{\alpha\beta}}:(\partial^{\sharp}_{\alpha\beta})^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{R,\alpha\beta})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{E}_{R,\alpha\beta}
constituting
(\mathcal{E}_{R},\nabla_{R})^{\diamondsuit}
is given by
\widetilde{\partial}^{\sharp}_{\alpha\beta}
.
It follows that

(\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{R},\nabla^{\prime}_{R})
may be obtained by gluing together
(\mathcal{E}_{R,\alpha},\nabla_{R}|_{\mathcal{E}_{R,\alpha}})
’s by means of
\widetilde{\partial}^{\sharp}_{\alpha\beta}
’s.
This implies the required equality (
402
) because the collection
(\{\nabla(\partial_{\alpha\beta})\}_{(\alpha,\beta)\in I_{2}},\{\delta_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in I})
(cf. (
335
)) with
\delta_{\alpha}=0
for every
\alpha
represents
\mathbb{H}^{1}(\nabla)(v)\in\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}^{1}_{G}
.
We finish the proof of assertion (i).

Next, let us consider assertion (ii).
Denote by
\overline{u}
the element of
M\otimes H^{1}
defined as the image of
u
via
\delta_{G}^{1}:\left(\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{G,Z}^{1}\subseteq\right)\widetilde{\mathbb{H}}_{G}^{1}\rightarrow H^{1}
(cf. (
323
)).
Also, denote by
(X^{\prime}_{R},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{R},\nabla^{\prime}_{R})
the deformation of
(X,\mathcal{E},\nabla)
over
R
represented by
\Xi(X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{R},\nabla_{R})+u
.
Then, the assertion can be proved by applying assertion (i) to the case where
(X_{R},\mathcal{E}_{R},\nabla_{R},v)
is replaced by
(X^{\prime}_{R},\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{R},\nabla^{\prime}_{R},-\overline{u})
.
This completes the proof of this lemma.
∎

Lemma 6.9.4
.

Let
v
be an element of
\mathbb{H}^{1}_{G}
.
Also, for each
i=1,2
,
let
(X_{R,i},\mathcal{E}_{R,i},\nabla_{R,i})

be a deformation of
(X,\mathcal{E},\nabla)
over
R
, and denote by

(\mathcal{E}^{v}_{R,i},\nabla^{v}_{R,i})
a unique (up to isomorphism)
flat
G
-bundle on
X_{R,i}/R

satisfying the equality

\Xi(X_{R,i},\mathcal{E}^{v}_{R,i},\nabla^{v}_{R,i})=\Xi(X_{R,i},\mathcal{E}_{R,i},\nabla_{R,i})+v
.
Then,
(\mathcal{E}_{R,1},\nabla_{R,1})^{\diamondsuit}\cong(\mathcal{E}_{R,2},\nabla_{R,2})^{\diamondsuit}

implies
(\mathcal{E}^{v}_{R,1},\nabla^{v}_{R,1})^{\diamondsuit}\cong(\mathcal{E}^{v}_{R,2},\nabla^{v}_{R,2})^{\diamondsuit}
.
(In the proof of Theorem
6.9.1
, we apply this lemma in the case where one of the deformations
X_{R,1}
,
X_{R,2}
is trivial.)

Proof.

The assertion follows from the construction of the assignment

(\mathcal{E},\nabla)\mapsto(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime})
asserted in Proposition
7.8.3

(cf. Remark
7.8.4
).
∎

7.
Appendix: Connections and PD stratifications on a
G
-bundle

In this Appendix, we recall some definitions concerning connections on a
G
-bundle and discuss their generalizations to higher-level PD stratifications.
As a consequence of our discussion, we will prove a generalization of Cartier’s theorem (cf. Proposition
7.7.3
, Corollary
7.8.2
), asserting that a principal bundle equipped with a flat connection (or more generally, a higher-level PD stratification) with vanishing
p
-curvature comes, via Frobenius pull-back, from a principal bundle on the Frobenius twist of the underlying space.

Let
S
be a scheme over a ring
R

and
f:X\rightarrow S
a smooth
S
-scheme.
Also, let
G
be a connected smooth algebraic group over
R
, and write
\mathfrak{g}:=\mathrm{Lie}(G)
.

7.1.
Connections on a
G
-bundle

First, we briefly recall the notion of a connection on a
G
-bundle.
We refer the reader to
[
54
, Chap. 1 and Chap. 3]
for detailed discussions related to the topic of this subsection.

Denote by
\mathrm{GL}(\mathfrak{g})
the algebraic group over
R
classifying automorphism groups of
\mathfrak{g}
over
R
.
The
adjoint representation
of
G
is, by definition, the morphism of algebraic
R
-groups

(404)

\mathrm{Ad}_{G}:G\rightarrow\mathrm{GL}(\mathfrak{g})

which, to any

h\in G
, assigns the automorphism
d(\mathrm{L}_{h}\circ\mathrm{R}_{h^{-1}})|_{e}
of
\mathfrak{g}
, i.e., the differential of
\mathrm{L}_{h}\circ\mathrm{R}_{h^{-1}}
at the identity element
e
.
For an
R
-scheme
T
and a
T
-rational point
h:T\rightarrow G
,
we occasionally identify
\mathrm{Ad}_{G}(h)
with the corresponding
\mathcal{O}_{T}
-linear automorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{T}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a (right)
G
-bundle on
X
and denote by

\pi:\mathcal{E}\rightarrow X
the structure morphism of
\mathcal{E}
over
X
.
Denote by
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}
the adjoint vector bundle associated with
\mathcal{E}
, i.e., the vector bundle on
X
corresponding to the
\mathrm{GL}(\mathfrak{g})
-bundle obtained from
\mathcal{E}
via change of structure group by
\mathrm{Ad}_{G}
.
More specifically,

\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}
is obtained as the sheaf of
G
-invariant sections
\pi_{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes\mathfrak{g})^{G}
in
\pi_{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes\mathfrak{g})\left(=\pi_{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}})\otimes\mathfrak{g}\right)
with respect to the right
G
-action given by
(a\otimes v)\cdot h=\mathrm{R}^{*}_{h}(a)\otimes\mathrm{Ad}_{G}(h^{-1})(v)
for any
a\in\pi_{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}})
,
v\in\mathfrak{g}
, and
h\in G
.
In particular,
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}
may be naturally identified with
\pi_{*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}/X})^{G}
via the natural isomorphism
\omega_{\mathcal{E}/X}^{\triangleright}:\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}/X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes\mathfrak{g}
(cf. (
73
)).

Let

\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}/S}
denote the subsheaf
\pi_{*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}/S})^{G}
of
G
-invariant sections of

\pi_{*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}/S})
.
The differential of
\pi
gives rise to the following short exact sequence:

(405)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}\longrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}/S}\xrightarrow{d_{\mathcal{E}}}\mathcal{T}_{X/S}\longrightarrow 0.

Both
\mathcal{T}_{X/S}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}/S}
have natural
Lie bracket operations
[-,-]
and the surjection
d_{\mathcal{E}}
preserves these structures.
The sheaf
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}/S}
together with the surjection
d_{\mathcal{E}}
forms a Lie algebroid on
X/S
(cf.
[
54
, Chap. 1, §  1.2, Definition 1.11]
).

By
an
S
-connection

on
\mathcal{E}
, we mean a split injection of the above sequence, i.e., an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear morphism

\nabla:\mathcal{T}_{X/S}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}/S}
with

d_{\mathcal{E}}\circ\nabla=\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{T}_{X/S}}
.
Recall that, for an
S
-connection
\nabla
on
\mathcal{E}
,
the
curvature
of
\nabla
is defined as the global section

(406)

\displaystyle\psi_{\nabla}\in\Gamma(X,(\bigwedge^{2}\Omega_{X/S})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}/S})

determined uniquely by
\psi_{\nabla}(\partial_{1},\partial_{2})=[\nabla(\partial_{1}),\nabla(\partial_{2})]-\nabla([\partial_{1},\partial_{2}])
for any local sections
\partial_{1}
,
\partial_{2}\in\mathcal{T}_{X/S}
.
Since the equality
(\mathrm{id}\otimes d_{\mathcal{E}})(\psi_{\nabla})=0
holds,

\psi_{\nabla}
may be regarded as an element of
\Gamma(X,(\bigwedge^{2}\Omega_{X/S})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}})
.
We shall say that
\nabla
is
flat

if
\psi_{\nabla}=0
.

By a
flat
G
-bundle on
X/S
,
we shall means a pair
(\mathcal{F},\nabla)
consisting of a
G
-bundle
\mathcal{F}
on
X
and a flat
S
-connection
\nabla
on
\mathcal{F}
.

Definition 7.1.1
.

Let
\nabla
be a flat
S
-connection on
\mathcal{E}
.
We shall say that
\nabla
(or
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
) is
quasi-nilpotent
if
for any local section
v\in\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}/S}
and any coordinate system
x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}
of
X/S
(such that
v,x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}
are defined on a common open subset of
X
), there exists a positive integer
s
with
\mathrm{ad}(\nabla(\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}))^{s}(v)=0
for every
i=1,\cdots,n
, where
\mathrm{ad}
denotes the adjoint operator on
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}/S}
.

Next, let
\mathcal{E}
be a
\mathrm{GL}_{l}
-bundle on
X
(for
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
) and
\mathcal{V}
the rank
l
vector bundle corresponding to
\mathcal{E}
.
Note that the notion of an
S
-connection recalled above
is consistent with that of
[
27
, (1.0)]
.
That is to say,
a choice of an
S
-connection on
\mathcal{E}

can be interpreted as a choice of an
S
-connection on
\mathcal{V}
, which is
an
f^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{S})
-linear morphism

(407)

\displaystyle\nabla:\mathcal{V}\rightarrow\Omega_{X/S}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{V}

satisfying

\nabla(a\cdot v)=da\otimes v+a\cdot\nabla(v)
for local sections
a\in\mathcal{O}_{X}
and
v\in\mathcal{V}
.
Throughout the present paper, we will not distinguish these two notions of
S
-connection.
If
\partial
is a local section of
\mathcal{T}_{X/S}
, then we shall write

\nabla_{\partial}:\mathcal{V}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}
for the
f^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{S})
-linear endomorphism of
\mathcal{V}
defined as the composite of
\nabla
and
\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{V}}\otimes\partial:\mathcal{V}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\Omega_{X/S}\rightarrow\left(\mathcal{V}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X}=\right)\mathcal{V}
.

For an
S
-connection
\nabla
on
\mathcal{V}
, we shall set

(408)

\displaystyle\nabla^{(2)}:\Omega_{X/S}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{V}\rightarrow(\bigwedge^{2}\Omega_{X/S})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{V}

to be the
f^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{S})
-linear morphism determined uniquely by
\nabla^{(2)}(\omega\otimes v)=d\omega\otimes v-\omega\wedge\nabla(v)
for any local sections
\omega\in\Omega_{X/S}
,
v\in\mathcal{V}
.
If
\nabla
is flat, then we have
\nabla^{(2)}\circ\nabla=0
, namely,
the sequence

(409)

\displaystyle\nabla^{\bullet}:\mathcal{V}\xrightarrow{\nabla}\Omega_{X/S}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{V}\xrightarrow{\nabla^{(2)}}(\bigwedge^{2}\Omega_{X/S})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{V}

forms a complex of sheaves.

By a
flat (vector) bundle
on
X/S
, we mean a pair
(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
consisting of a vector bundle
\mathcal{V}
on
X
and a flat
S
-connection
\nabla
on
\mathcal{V}
.
The notion of an isomorphism between flat bundles can be defined in a natural manner.

7.2.
Pull-back and base-change of connections

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a
G
-bundle on
X
and
\nabla
an
S
-connection on
\mathcal{E}
.
First, suppose that we are given another smooth
S
-scheme
Y
and
a (not necessarily étale) morphism
y:Y\rightarrow X
over
S
.
Denote by
y^{*}(\mathcal{E})\left(:=Y\times_{X}\mathcal{E}\right)
the pull-back of
\mathcal{E}
by
y
, which forms a
G
-bundle on
Y
.
Then, we obtain the following morphism of short exact sequences:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{g}_{y^{*}(\mathcal{E})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{dy_{\mathcal{E}}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{y^{*}(\mathcal{E})/S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d_{y^{*}(\mathcal{E})}}
\scriptstyle{d\widetilde{y}_{\mathcal{E}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{Y/S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{dy}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{y^{*}(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{y^{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}/S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{y^{*}(d_{\mathcal{E}})}
\textstyle{y^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{X/S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

where
the middle and left-hand vertical arrows
dy_{\mathcal{E}}
,
d\widetilde{y}_{\mathcal{E}}
are obtained by differentiating the projection
y^{*}(\mathcal{E})\rightarrow\mathcal{E}
over
S
and
X
respectively.
Note that
dy_{\mathcal{E}}
is an isomorphism.
If
\nabla^{\prime}
denotes the split surjection
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}/S}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}}
of (
405
) corresponding to
\nabla
,
then the composite
dy_{\mathcal{E}}^{-1}\circ y^{*}(\nabla^{\prime})\circ d\widetilde{y}_{\mathcal{E}}

specifies a split surjection of the upper horizontal arrow in (
7.2
).
Hence, the corresponding split injection defines
an
S
-connection

(414)

\displaystyle y^{*}(\nabla):\mathcal{T}_{Y/S}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{y^{*}(\mathcal{E})}

on
y^{*}(\mathcal{E})
.
Since the equality
y^{*}(\psi_{\nabla})=\psi_{y^{*}(\nabla)}

holds under the identifications given by
dy_{\mathcal{E}}
and
d\widetilde{y}_{\mathcal{E}}
,
the
S
-connection
y^{*}(\nabla)
is flat
if
\nabla
is flat.
We shall refer to
y^{*}(\nabla)
as the
pull-back
of
\nabla
by
y
.

Next, let
s:S^{\prime}\rightarrow S
be a morphism of
k
-schemes and write
X^{\prime}:=S^{\prime}\times_{S}X
.
We use the notation
s^{*}(-)
to denote the result of base-change by
s
.
Differentiating the projections
X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
and
s^{*}(\mathcal{E})\rightarrow\mathcal{E}
induces the following commutative square:

\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{s^{*}(\mathcal{E})/S^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d_{s^{*}(\mathcal{E})}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{s^{*}(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}/S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s^{*}(d_{\mathcal{E}})}
\textstyle{s^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{X/S}).}

The base-change of
\nabla
by
s
specifies, via the vertical arrows in this square,
an
S^{\prime}
-connection

(419)

\displaystyle s^{*}(\nabla):\mathcal{T}_{X^{\prime}/S}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{s^{*}(\mathcal{E})/S^{\prime}}

on
s^{*}(\mathcal{E})
.
Since the equality
s^{*}(\psi_{\nabla})=\psi_{s^{*}(\nabla)}

holds, the
S
-connection
s^{*}(\nabla)
is flat
if
\nabla
is flat.
We shall refer to
s^{*}(\nabla)
as the
base-change
of
\nabla
by
s
.

7.3.
p
-curvature and Cartier operators

In this subsection, suppose that
S
is a scheme over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
.
Let
\mathcal{E}
be as above.
Recall from
[
54
, Chap. 3, § 3.3, Definition 3.8]
that, given a flat
S
-connection
\nabla
on
\mathcal{E}
, we can obtain the global section

(420)

\displaystyle{{}^{p}}\psi_{\nabla}\in\Gamma(X,F^{*}_{X}(\Omega_{X/S})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}/S})

uniquely determined by
\langle F^{-1}_{X}(\partial),{{}^{p}}\psi_{\nabla}\rangle=\nabla(\partial)^{[p]}-\nabla(\partial^{[p]})
for each local section
\partial\in\mathcal{T}_{X/S}
(cf. §
3.7
for the definition of
p
-power operations
(-)^{[p]}
).
This global section
{{}^{p}}\psi_{\nabla}
is called the
p
-curvature
of
\nabla
.
Just as in the case of curvature, we see that
{{}^{p}}\psi_{\nabla}
lies in
\Gamma(X,F^{*}_{X}(\Omega_{X/S})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{E}})
.
The
S
-connection
\nabla
is called
p
-flat
if (
\psi_{\nabla}=0
and)
{{}^{p}}\psi_{\nabla}=0
.
If
\mathcal{E}
is a
\mathrm{GL}_{l}
-bundle and
\mathcal{V}
denotes the corresponding vector bundle, then the notion of
p
-curvature is consistent with that of
[
27
, § 5, (5.0.3)]
under the natural identification
\mathcal{E}nd_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathcal{V})=\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{GL}_{l})_{\mathcal{E}}
.

Remark 7.3.1
.

Suppose that
\nabla
is
p
-flat.
Then, one may verify that it is locally quasi-nilpotent (cf.
[
5
, § 4, Remark 4.11]
).

Remark 7.3.2
.

Let
y
and
s
be as in the previous subsection.
Then, under certain identifications, we have
y^{*}({{}^{p}}\psi_{\nabla})={{}^{p}}\psi_{y^{*}(\nabla)}
(resp.,
s^{*}({{}^{p}}\psi_{\nabla})={{}^{p}}\psi_{s^{*}(\nabla)}
).
This implies that
y^{*}(\nabla)
(resp.,
s^{*}(\nabla)
) is
p
-flat if
\nabla
is
p
-flat.

Let
(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
be a flat vector bundle on
X/S
.
Since both
\nabla
and
\nabla^{(2)}
are
F^{-1}_{X/S}(\mathcal{O}_{X^{(1)}})
-linear,
the sheaves
\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla)
,
\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{(2)})
, and
\mathcal{H}^{1}(\nabla^{\bullet})\left(:=\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{(2)})/\mathrm{Im}(\nabla)\right)
may be regarded as
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(1)}}
-modules via the underlying homeomorphism of
F_{X/S}
.
Recall (cf.
[
43
, Chap.  1, Proposition 1.2.4]
) that
the
Cartier operator
associated with
(\mathcal{V},\nabla)

is a unique
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(1)}}
-linear morphism

(421)

\displaystyle C_{(\mathcal{V},\nabla)}:\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{(2)})\rightarrow\Omega_{X^{(1)}/S}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{(1)}}}F_{X/S*}(\mathcal{V})

satisfying the equality

(422)

\displaystyle\langle F_{S}^{*}(\partial),C_{(\mathcal{V},\nabla)}(a)\rangle=\langle\partial^{[p]},a\rangle-\nabla_{\partial}^{p-1}(\langle\partial,a\rangle)

(cf. Example
3.7.3
for the definition of
F_{S}^{*}(\partial)
) for any local sections
\partial\in\mathcal{T}_{X/S}
,

a\in\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{(2)})
.
Let us consider the composite isomorphism

(423)

\displaystyle\Omega_{X^{(1)}/S}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{(1)}}}F_{X/S*}(\mathcal{V})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}F_{X/S*}(F_{X/S}^{*}(\Omega_{X^{(1)}/S})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{V})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}F_{X/S*}(F_{X}^{*}(\Omega_{X/S})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{V}),

where the first isomorphism follows from the projection formula.
By using this composite,
we occasionally regard
C_{(\mathcal{V},\nabla)}
as a morphism
\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{(2)})\rightarrow F_{X}^{*}(\Omega_{X/S})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{V}
.

Suppose further that

(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
has vanishing
p
-curvature.
Then,
according to
[
44
, Theorem 3.1.1]
(and the discussion following
[
43
, Chap. I, Proposition 1.2.4]
),
there exists an
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(1)}}
-linear isomorphism

(424)

\displaystyle\overline{C}_{(\mathcal{V},\nabla)}:\mathcal{H}^{1}(\nabla^{\bullet})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\Omega_{X^{(1)}/S}\otimes\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla)

which makes the following square diagram commute:

\textstyle{\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla^{(1)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{C_{(\mathcal{V},\nabla)}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{quotient}}
\textstyle{\Omega_{X^{(1)}/S}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{(1)}}}F_{X/S*}(\mathcal{V})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{H}^{1}(\nabla^{\bullet})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\overline{C}_{(\mathcal{V},\nabla)}}
\textstyle{\Omega_{X^{(1)}/S}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X^{(1)}}}\mathrm{Ker}(\nabla).\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{inclusion}}

7.4.
Differential operators of level
m

In this subsection, we briefly recall
the rings of differential operators on
X/S
of higher level.
The case of finite level was originally defined and studied by Berthelot (see
[
3
]
and
[
4
]
for more details).

We here suppose that

p
is nilpotent in
R
.
Let
m
be a nonnegative integer; whenever we deal with various objects associated with this
m
,
we always suppose that the scheme
S
is equipped with an
m
-PD ideal
(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\gamma)
such that
\gamma
extends to
X
.
That is to say,
\mathfrak{a}
is a quasi-coherent ideal of
\mathcal{O}_{S}
and
(\mathfrak{b},\gamma)
is an
m
-PD structure
on
\mathfrak{a}

(cf.
[
3
, § 1.3, Definition 1.3.2, (ii)]
).
Note that
\gamma
always extends to
X
in the case
\mathfrak{b}=(p)
.

It follows from the discussion in
[
3
, § 1.4]
that
there exists the
m
-PD envelope
P_{X/S,(m)}
of the diagonal embedding
X\rightarrow X\times_{S}X
.
Denote by
\overline{I}
the defining ideal of the natural morphism
X\hookrightarrow P_{X/S,(m)}
.
Recall that
\overline{I}
admits the
m
-PD-adic filtration
\{\overline{I}^{\{l\}}\}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
constructed in the manner of
[
4
, Definition A. 3]
.
Let
\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}
denote the structure sheaf of
P_{X/S,(m)}
.
For each
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we denote by
\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}^{l}
the quotient sheaf of
\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}
by
\overline{I}^{\{l+1\}}
and by
P^{l}_{X/S,(m)}
the closed subscheme of
P_{X/S,(m)}
defined by the surjection
\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}^{l}
.

On the other hand,
for each
l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, denote by
\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(\infty)}^{l}

the quotient of the structure sheaf of
X\times_{S}X
by

I^{l+1}
, where
I
is the ideal of the diagonal embedding
X\rightarrow X\times_{S}X
.
Also, denote by
P_{X/S,(\infty)}^{l}
the closed subscheme of
X\times_{S}X
defined by the surjection
\mathcal{O}_{X\times_{S}X}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(\infty)}^{l}
.

Hence, in each case of
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\sqcup\{\infty\}
, we have the following sequence of sheaves on
X
:

(429)

\displaystyle\cdots\rightarrow\mathcal{P}^{l}_{X/S,(m)}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}^{l-1}_{X/S,(m)}\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}^{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}^{0}_{X/S,(m)}\left(=\mathcal{O}_{X}\right).

Let

\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{l}
and
\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{l}
denote the morphisms
P_{X/S,(m)}^{l}\rightarrow X
induced by
the first and second projections
X\times_{S}X\rightarrow X

respectively.
As asserted in
[
3
, § 2.1, Proposition 2.1.3, (ii)]
and
[
20
, § 16, (16.8.9.2)]
,
we have the canonical
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-algebra morphism

(430)

\displaystyle\delta_{m}^{l,l^{\prime}}:\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}^{l+l^{\prime}}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}^{l}_{X/S,(m)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}^{l^{\prime}}_{X/S,(m)}

for each pair of nonnegative integers
(l,l^{\prime})
such that if
m^{\prime}
is an element of
\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\sqcup\{\infty\}
with
m^{\prime}\leq m
, then
the following diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}^{l+l^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{m}^{l,l^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\varsigma^{l+l^{\prime}}_{m,m^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}^{l}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}^{l^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varsigma^{l}_{m,m^{\prime}}\otimes\varsigma^{l^{\prime}}_{m,m^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m^{\prime})}^{l+l^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{m^{\prime}}^{l,l^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m^{\prime})}^{l}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m^{\prime})}^{l^{\prime}},}

where
\varsigma^{l^{\prime\prime}}_{m,m^{\prime}}
(
l^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
) denotes
the natural morphism.
The morphism
\delta_{m}^{l,l^{\prime}}
specifies a morphism of
X
-schemes

(435)

\displaystyle\delta_{m}^{l,l^{\prime}\sharp}:P_{X/S,(m)}^{l}\times_{X}P_{X/S,(m)}^{l^{\prime}}\rightarrow P_{X/S,(m)}^{l+l^{\prime}}.

For each nonnegative integer
l
,
write
\mathcal{D}_{X/S,l}^{(m)}
for the sheaf

(436)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{X/S,l}^{(m)}:=\mathcal{H}om_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}(\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{l*}(\mathcal{P}^{l}_{X/S,(m)}),\mathcal{O}_{X}).

In particular, we have
\mathcal{D}_{X/S,0}^{(m)}=\mathcal{O}_{X}
and
\mathcal{D}_{X/S,1}^{(m)}/\mathcal{D}_{X/S,0}^{(m)}=\mathcal{T}_{X/S}
.
The
sheaf of differential operators of level
m
is defined by

(437)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(m)}:=\bigcup_{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\mathcal{D}_{X/S,l}^{(m)}.

The various morphisms
\delta_{m}^{l,l^{\prime}}
(for
l,l^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
) induces a structure of (possibly noncommutative)
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-algebra
\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(m)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(m)}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(m)}
on
\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(m)}
.
The sheaves
\{\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(m)}\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
forms an inductive system and there exists a canonical identification
\varinjlim_{m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(m)}=\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(\infty)}
.

By a
\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(m)}
-module, we mean a pair
(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
consisting of an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{V}
and a left
\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(m)}
-action
\nabla
on
\mathcal{V}
extending its
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure.

7.5.
m
-PD stratifications on a
G
-bundle

In the rest of this Appendix, we suppose that
G
is affine.
Denote by
R_{G}
the
R
-algebra defined as the coordinate ring of
G
.
Also, let us fix
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\sqcup\{\infty\}
.

Definition 7.5.1
.

(i)

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a
G
-bundle on
X
.
An
m
-PD stratification
on
\mathcal{E}
is
a collection

(438)

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}:=\{\varepsilon_{l}\}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}},

where each
\varepsilon_{l}
denotes an isomorphism
P_{X/S,(m)}^{l}\times_{X}\mathcal{E}\left(=\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{l*}(\mathcal{E})\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{E}\times_{X}P^{l}_{X/S,(m)}\left(=\mathrm{pr}^{l*}_{1}(\mathcal{E})\right)
of
G
-bundles on
P_{X/S,(m)}^{l}

satisfying the following conditions:

–

The equalities
\varepsilon_{0}=\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{E}}
and
\varepsilon_{l_{2}}|_{P_{X/S,(m)}^{l_{1}}}=\varepsilon_{l_{1}}
hold for any pair of integers

(l_{1},l_{2})
with
l_{1}\leq l_{2}
.

–

The cocycle condition holds:
to be precise, for any pair of nonnegative integers
(l,l^{\prime})
, the following diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{P_{X/S,(m)}^{l}\times_{X}P_{X/S,(m)}^{l^{\prime}}\times_{X}\mathcal{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{m}^{l,l^{\prime}\sharp*}(\varepsilon_{l+l^{\prime}})}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{q_{2}^{l,l^{\prime}*}(\varepsilon_{l+l^{\prime}})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}\times_{X}P_{X/S,(m)}^{l}\times_{X}P_{X/S,(m)}^{l^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{P_{X/S,(m)}^{l}\times_{X}\mathcal{E}\times_{X}P_{X/S}^{l^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces,}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{q_{1}^{l,l^{\prime}*}(\varepsilon_{l+l^{\prime}})}

where
q_{1}^{l,l^{\prime}}
and
q_{2}^{l,l^{\prime}}
denote the morphisms defined, respectively, as

(443)

\displaystyle q_{1}^{l,l^{\prime}}

\displaystyle:P_{X/S,(m)}^{l}\times_{X}P_{X/S,(m)}^{l^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{1}}P_{X/S,(m)}^{l}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{immersion}}P_{X/S,(m)}^{l+l^{\prime}},

\displaystyle q_{2}^{l,l^{\prime}}

\displaystyle:P_{X/S,(m)}^{l}\times_{X}P_{X/S,(m)}^{l^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}P_{X/S,(m)}^{l^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{immersion}}P_{X/S,(m)}^{l+l^{\prime}}.

Also, by an
m
-PD stratified
G
-bundle

on
X/S
, we mean a pair

(444)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{E},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})

consisting of a
G
-bundle
\mathcal{E}
on
X
and an
m
-PD stratification
\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}
on
\mathcal{E}
.

(ii)

Let
(\mathcal{E},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})
and

(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime\blacklozenge})
be

-PD stratified
G
-bundles on

, where

.
An

from

\alpha:\mathcal{E}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
such that, for each nonnegative integer

, the following square diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{P_{X/S,(m)}^{l}\times_{X}\mathcal{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}\times\alpha}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}\times_{X}P_{X/S,(m)}^{l}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{\alpha\times\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{P_{X/S,(m)}^{l}\times_{X}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{l}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\times_{X}P_{X/S,(m)}^{l}.}

In what follows, we shall describe an
m
-PD stratification on a
G
-bundle by using
the classical notion of an

Let

Since

If

Now, let

For each nonnegative integer

The

is interpreted as the commutativity of the following square diagram:

\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}^{l}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon^{\natural}_{l}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{R}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\sharp}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}^{l}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{R}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\sharp}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}^{l}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}})\otimes_{R}R_{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon_{l}^{\natural}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{R_{G}}}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}^{l})\otimes R_{G}\left(=(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{R}R_{G})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}^{l}\right).}

Moreover, the commutativity of (
–
‣
(i)
) reads the commuativity of the following diagram:

\textstyle{\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}^{l}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}^{l^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}^{l}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}^{l^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\delta_{m}^{l+l^{\prime}\sharp*}(\varepsilon^{\natural}_{l+l^{\prime}})}
\scriptstyle{\sim}

\textstyle{\mathcal{P}^{l}_{X/S,(m)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}^{l^{\prime}}_{X/S,(m)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}

\scriptstyle{q_{2}^{l,l^{\prime}*}(\varepsilon^{\natural}_{l+l^{\prime}})}

Thus,
the resulting collection

(457)

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge\natural}:=\{\varepsilon^{\natural}_{l}\}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}

forms an

(By a

Conversely, suppose that we are given an

)
