Remark 7.5.2
.

One may verify that giving a collection as in (
457
) is equivalent to giving
a
compatible
collection

(458)

\displaystyle\overline{\varepsilon}^{\blacklozenge\natural}:=\{\overline{\varepsilon}_{l}^{\natural}\}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}},

where each
\overline{\varepsilon}_{l}^{\natural}
denotes an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-algebra morphism
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}^{l}
, satisfying the following two conditions:

•

For each
l\geq 0
, the following square diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\varepsilon}_{l}^{\natural}}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{R}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\sharp}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}^{l}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{R}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\sharp}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{R}R_{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\varepsilon}_{l}^{\natural}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{R_{G}}}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}^{l})\otimes_{R}R_{G}\left(=(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{R}R_{G})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}^{l}\right).}

•

The equality
\overline{\varepsilon}_{0}^{\natural}=\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}}
holds, and for each pair of nonnegative integers
(l,l^{\prime})
, the following square diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}^{l+l^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{id}\otimes\delta_{m}^{l,l^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}^{l}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}^{l^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\varepsilon}_{l^{\prime}}^{\natural}}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\varepsilon}_{l+l^{\prime}}^{\natural}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}^{l^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\varepsilon}_{l}^{\natural}\otimes\mathrm{id}}

In particular, according to
[
3
, § 2.3, Proposition 2.3.2]
, this collection of data induces a structure of left
\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(m)}
-module on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
extending its
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module structure.

Now, let
\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}:=\{\varepsilon_{l}\}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
be an
m
-PD stratification on
\mathcal{E}
and
m^{\prime}
an element of
\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\sqcup\{\infty\}
with
m^{\prime}\leq m
.
Denote by

\overline{\varepsilon}^{\blacklozenge\natural}:=\{\overline{\varepsilon}_{l}^{\natural}\}_{l\geq 0}

the collection as in (
458
) corresponding to
\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}
.
The collection
\overline{\varepsilon}^{\blacklozenge\natural}|^{(m^{\prime})}:=\{(\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}}\otimes q_{l})\circ\overline{\varepsilon}_{l}^{\natural}\}_{l}
, where
q_{l}
denotes the natural morphism
\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m)}^{l}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(m^{\prime})}^{l}
,
satisfies the above two conditions for
m^{\prime}
.
Hence, it determines an
m^{\prime}
-PD stratification

(467)

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}|^{(m^{\prime})}

on
\mathcal{E}
, which will be
called the
m^{\prime}
-th truncation
of
\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}
.
Since
\varinjlim_{m}\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(m)}=\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(\infty)}
, a
\infty
-PD stratification on
\mathcal{E}
may be interpreted as
a compatible collection
\{\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
of
m
-PD stratifications on
\mathcal{E}
for various
m
.

Definition 7.5.3
.

Let
\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}:=\{\varepsilon_{l}\}_{l}
be an
m
-PD stratification on
\mathcal{E}
with
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.
We shall say that
\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}
(or
(\mathcal{E},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})
) is
quasi-nilpotent

if the
\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(m)}
-module
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
corresponding to
\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge\natural}
(cf. Remark
7.5.2
above) is quasi-nilpotent in the sense of
[
3
, § 2.3, Definitions]
.

Remark 7.5.4
.

According to
[
3
, § 2.3, Proposition 2.3.7]
, giving a quasi-nilpotent
m
-PD stratification on
\mathcal{E}
is equivalent to giving an isomorphism

P_{X/S,(m)}\times_{X}\mathcal{E}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{E}\times_{X}P_{X/S,(m)}

of
G
-bundles on
P_{X/S,(m)}
satisfying the usual cocycle condition and whose reductions modulo
\overline{I}^{\{l+1\}}

for various
l
form an
m
-PD stratification.

Remark 7.5.5
.

We can define the pull-back (resp., the base-change) of an
m
-PD stratification on
\mathcal{E}
to an étale
X
-scheme (resp., an
S
-scheme) by applying both the discussion in
[
4
, § 2.1]
and the description in terms of
\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(m)}
-modules mentioned above.
Also, the formation of pull-back (resp., base-change) commutes with truncation to lower levels and preserves the quasi-nilpotency condition.
We will omit the details because the construction is straightforward.

7.6.
m
-crystals of
G
-bundles

Let
m
be an element of
\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
Y
an
S
-scheme such that the
m
-PD structure of
S
extends to
Y
.
Recall (cf.
[
33
, § 4.1, Definition 4.1.1]
) that, for a scheme
U
over
Y
, a
m
-PD thickening
of
U
over
(S,\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\gamma)
is a collection of data
(U,T,J,\delta)
consisting of a scheme
T
over
S
, a closed immersion
U\hookrightarrow T
over
S
and an
m
-PD structure
(J,\delta)
on the defining ideal of
U\hookrightarrow T
compatible with
(\mathfrak{b},\gamma)
.
A morphism of
m
-PD thickenings over
(S,\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\gamma)
can be defined in an obvious way.
The
m
-crystalline site
\mathrm{Cris}^{(m)}(Y/S)
is the category of
m
-PD thickenings
(U,T,J,\delta)
of an open subscheme
U
of
Y
over
(S,\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\gamma)
endowed with the topology induced by the étale topology on
T
.

An
m
-crystal of
G
-bundles
on
Y/S
is
a cartesian section
\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}
on
\mathrm{Cris}^{(m)}(Y/S)
.
That is to say,
\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}
is
given by the following data:
For every
m
-PD thickening
(U,T,J,\delta)
, a
G
-bundle
\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}_{T}
on
T
, and for every morphism
u:(U_{1},T_{1},J_{1},\delta_{1})\rightarrow(U_{2},T_{2},J_{2},\delta_{2})
in
\mathrm{Cris}^{(m)}(X/S)
, an isomorphism of
G
-bundles
\rho_{u}:u^{*}(\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}_{T_{2}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}_{T_{1}}
, satisfying the cocycle condition.

Let
\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}
be an
m
-crystal of
G
-bundles on
Y/S
and
m^{\prime}
a nonnegative integer with
m^{\prime}\leq m
.
Since
an
m
-PD ideal can be considered as an
m^{\prime}
-PD ideal,
the
G
-bundles on various thickenings defining
\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}

also form an
m^{\prime}
-crystal of
G
-bundles

(468)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}|^{(m)}

on
Y/S
; we shall refer to it as
the
m^{\prime}
-th truncation
of
\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}
.

Now, let
X
be as before and suppose that there exists a closed immersion
\iota:Y\hookrightarrow X
over
S
.
For each
m
-crystal
\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}
in
G
-bundles on
X/S
, we can obtain the restriction
\iota^{*}(\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit})
of
\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}
.
Then, the following assertion holds.

Proposition 7.6.1
.

Let
(J_{0},\gamma_{0})
be a sub-
m
-PD structure of
(J,\gamma)
over
I
(cf.
[
3
, § 1.3, Definition 1.3,1]
).
Write
\overline{S}
for the closed subscheme of
S
defined by the ideal
J_{0}
, and
write
\overline{X}:=X\times_{S}\overline{S}
.
(Note that the
m
-PD structure on
\overline{S}
induced from that on
S
extends to
\overline{X}
.)
Then, the restriction by the closed immersion
\overline{X}\hookrightarrow X

determines an equivalence of categories between the groupoids of
m
-crystals of
G
-bundles on
X/S
and
\overline{X}/S
.

Proof.

The assertion follows from various definitions involved together with the corresponding fact on
m
-crystals of sheaves asserted in
[
33
, § 4, Corollary 4.1.6]
.
∎

Proposition 7.6.2
.

(Recall that
G
is assumed to be affine.)
Let us keep the notation in Proposition
7.6.1
.
Then, there exists a canonical equivalence of categories

(469)

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\text{the groupoid of quasi-nilpotent}\\
\text{$m$-PD stratified $G$-bundles on $X/S$}\end{pmatrix}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\begin{pmatrix}\text{the groupoid of $m$-crystals}\\
\text{of $G$-bundles on $\overline{X}/S$}\\
\end{pmatrix}.

Proof.

The assertion follows from a routine argument of (
m
-)crystals together with
[
33
, § 4, Proposition 4.1.7]
.
∎

Remark 7.6.3
.

We shall describe concretely the bijective correspondence given by (
469
).
First, given
an
m
-PD stratified
G
-bundle
(\mathcal{E},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})
(where
\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}:=\{\varepsilon_{l}\}_{l}
) on
X/S
, we can construct an
m
-crystal of
G
-bundles
\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}
on
\overline{X}/S
.
To this end, it suffices to specify
\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}_{T}
for sufficiently small
(U,T,J,\delta)\in\mathrm{Ob}(\mathrm{Cris}^{(m)}(\overline{X}/S))
, e.g., so that there exists an
S
-morphism

h:T\rightarrow X
over
(S,\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b},\gamma)
extending the open immersion
U\hookrightarrow\overline{X}
.
For such a morphism
h
, we shall set
\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}_{T}
to be the pull-back
h^{*}(\mathcal{E})
.
If we are given another choice of a morphism
h^{\prime}:T\rightarrow X
such as
h
,
then the pair
(h,h^{\prime})
defines a morphism
T\rightarrow P_{X/S,(m)}^{l}
for a sufficiently large
l
.
The pull-back of
\varepsilon_{l}
via this morphism yields an isomorphism
(h,h^{\prime})^{*}(\varepsilon_{l}):h^{\prime*}(\mathcal{E})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}h^{*}(\mathcal{E})
.
This implies that
\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}_{T}
is well-defined up to canonical isomorphism.
Thus, the
\mathcal{E}_{T}^{\diamondsuit}
’s for various
(U,T,J,\delta)
’s form an
m
-crystal of
G
-bundles
\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}
.
One may verify that the resulting assignment
(\mathcal{E},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})\mapsto\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}
coincides with (
469
).

Conversely, let
\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}
be an
m
-crystal of
G
-bundles on
\overline{X}/S
.
For each
l\geq 0
and
i\in\{1,2\}
,
this
m
-crystal associates to the projection
\mathrm{pr}_{i}^{l}:P_{X/S,(m)}^{l}\rightarrow X
an isomorphism of
G
-bundles
\varepsilon_{l,i}:\mathrm{pr}_{i}^{l*}(\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}_{X})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}_{P_{X/S,(m)}^{l}}
.
The composite isomorphisms
\varepsilon_{l}:=\varepsilon_{l,1}^{-1}\circ\varepsilon_{l,2}:\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{l*}(\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}_{X})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{l*}(\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}_{X})
for various
l
form an
m
-PD stratification on
\mathcal{E}_{X}^{\diamondsuit}
.
The resulting assignment
\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}\mapsto(\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}_{X},\{\varepsilon_{l}\}_{l})
realizes the inverse of (
469
).

7.7.
Cartier’s theorem for
m
-PD stratified
G
-bundles

Let
m
be a nonnegative integer.
In this subsection, suppose that the base ring
R
is defined over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
.
We shall recall from
[
33
]
the definition of
p
-
m
-curvature and Cartier’s theorem for
\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(m)}
-modules.
Denote by
\mathcal{K}_{X/S}^{(m)}
the kernel of the morphism
\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(m)}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{f^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{S})}(\mathcal{O}_{X})
defining the trivial
\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(m)}
-module structure on
\mathcal{O}_{X}
.
If
(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
is a
\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(m)}
-module, then the composite

(470)

\displaystyle{{}^{p}\psi}_{\nabla}^{(m)}:\mathcal{K}_{X/S}^{(m)}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(m)}\xrightarrow{\nabla}\mathcal{E}nd_{f^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{S})}(\mathcal{V})

is called the
p
-
m
-curvature
of
\nabla
(cf.
[
33
, § 3, Definition 3.1.1]
).
We shall write

(471)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{\nabla}

for the subsheaf of
\mathcal{V}

on which
\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(m)+}
acts as zero, where
\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(m)+}
denotes the kernel of the natural projection
\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(m)}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}
.
This sheaf

\mathcal{V}^{\nabla}

is called
the
sheaf of horizontal sections
of
(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
and
occassionaly regarded as an
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(m+1)}}
-module via the underlying homeomorphism of
F_{X/S}^{(m+1)}
.

On the other hand, let
\mathcal{U}
be an
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(m+1)}}
-module.
Then, its pull-back
F_{X/S}^{(m+1)*}(\mathcal{U})
by
F_{X/S}^{(m+1)}
admits a canonical structure of
{\mathcal{D}}_{X/S}^{(m)}
-action

(472)

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mathrm{can}(m+1)}_{\mathcal{U}}:\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(m)}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}nd_{f^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{S})}(F_{X/S}^{(m+1)*}(\mathcal{U})),

for which
the pair
(F_{X/S}^{(m+1)*}(\mathcal{U}),\nabla^{\mathrm{can}(m+1)}_{\mathcal{U}})
forms a
\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(m)}
-module with vanishing
p
-
m
-curvature.
We abuse notation by writing
\nabla^{\mathrm{can}(m+1)}_{\mathcal{U}}
for the
S
-connection
on
F_{X/S}^{(m+1)*}(\mathcal{U})
corresponding to the
\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(0)}
-module structure induced by
\nabla^{\mathrm{can}(m+1)}_{\mathcal{U}}
.

According to
[
33
, § 3, Corollary 3.2.4]
,
the assignments
\mathcal{U}\mapsto(F_{X/S}^{(m+1)*}(\mathcal{U}),\nabla^{\mathrm{can}(m+1)}_{\mathcal{U}})
and
(\mathcal{V},\nabla)\mapsto\mathcal{V}^{\nabla}
determine
an equivalence of categories

(473)

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\text{the category of}\\
\text{$\mathcal{O}_{X^{(m+1)}}$-modules}\end{pmatrix}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\begin{pmatrix}\text{the category of $\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(m)}$-modules}\\
\text{with vanishing $p$-$m$-curvature}\\
\end{pmatrix},

which is compatible with the formations of tensor products and extensions.

Next, let
(\mathcal{E},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})
be an
m
-PD stratified
G
-bundle on
X/S
.
As discussed after Definition
7.5.1
,
\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}
specifies an
m
-PD stratification on the
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-algebra
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
(cf. (
457
)), and hence, specifies
a
\mathcal{D}_{X/S}^{(m)}
-module structure
\nabla
on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
(cf. Remark
7.5.2
).
In particular, we obtain the
p
-
m
-curvature
{{}^{p}}\psi^{(m)}_{\nabla}
of
\nabla
.

Definition 7.7.1
.

(i)

We refer to
{{}^{p}}\psi_{\nabla}^{(m)}
as the
p
-
m
-curvature
of
\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}
(or, of
(\mathcal{E},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})
), and write
{{}^{p}}\psi_{\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}}^{(m)}:={{}^{p}}\psi^{(m)}_{\nabla}
.

(ii)

Let
\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}
be an
m
-crystal of
G
-bundles on
X/S
.
We shall say that
\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}
is
dormant
if the
m
-PD stratified
G
-bundle corresponding to
\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}
via (
469
) has vanishing
p
-
m
-curvature.

Remark 7.7.2
.

If an
m
-PD stratification
\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}
has vanishing
p
-
m
-curvature, then it is quasi-nilpotent in the sense of Definition
7.5.3
(cf.
[
33
, § 3, Remark 3.1.2]
).

Suppose further that
\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}
has vanishing
p
-
m
-curvature.
The
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-algebra structure on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
is restricted to an
\mathcal{O}_{X^{(m+1)}}
-algebra structure on the sheaf of horizontal sections
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\nabla}
(with respect to
\nabla
).
We shall write

(474)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{\nabla}:=\mathcal{S}pec(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\nabla}),

which is a relative affine scheme over
X
.
The commutativity of (
7.5
) for
(\mathcal{E},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})
implies that
the
G
-coaction
\mathrm{R}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\sharp}:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes R_{G}
is restricted to a morphism
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\nabla}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\nabla}\otimes R_{G}
, which specifies a
G
-action on
\mathcal{E}
.
By (
473
), there exists an isomorphism
(F^{(m+1)*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\nabla}),\nabla^{\mathrm{can}(m+1)}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}^{\nabla}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}},\nabla)

compatible with the
G
-coactions, so we have
F_{X/S}^{(m+1)*}(\mathcal{E}^{\nabla})\cong\mathcal{E}
.
Hence, it follows from the faithful flatness of
F_{X/S}^{(m+1)}
that
\mathcal{E}^{\nabla}
forms a
G
-bundle with respect to the
G
-action defined above.
Moreover, by reversing the steps in the construction of
\mathcal{E}^{\nabla}
using
(\mathcal{E},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})
, we obtain the following assertion as a generalization of Cartier’s theorem (cf.
[
33
, § 3.2, Corollary 3.2.4]
).

Proposition 7.7.3
.

(Recall that
G
is assumed to be affine.) Let
m
be a nonnegative integer.

(i)

The assignment
(\mathcal{E},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})\mapsto\mathcal{E}^{\nabla}
constructed above defines an equivalence of categories

(475)

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\text{the groupoid of $m$-PD stratified $G$-bundles }\\
\text{on $X/S$ with vanishing $p$-$m$-curvature}\end{pmatrix}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\begin{pmatrix}\text{the groupoid of }\\
\text{$G$-bundles on $X^{(m+1)}$}\\
\end{pmatrix}.

In particular, for each
G
-bundle
\mathcal{G}
on
X^{(m+1)}
, there exists a canonical structure of
m
-PD stratification on
F^{(m+1)*}_{X/S}(\mathcal{G})
with vanishing
p
-
m
-curvature.

(ii)

Let
m^{\prime}
be another nonnegative integers with
m\leq m^{\prime}
.
Then, the following square diagram is
1
-commutative:

(476)

\displaystyle\begin{CD}\begin{pmatrix}\text{ the groupoid of $m^{\prime}$-PD stratified $G$-bundles}\\
\text{ on $X/S$ with vanishing $p$-$m^{\prime}$-curvature}\end{pmatrix}@>{(\ref{E3erf002})\ \text{for}\ m^{\prime}}>{\sim}>\begin{pmatrix}\text{ the groupoid of }\\
\text{$G$-bundles on $X^{(m^{\prime}+1)}$}\end{pmatrix}\\
@V{(-)|^{(m)}}V{}V@V{}V{F^{(m^{\prime}-m)*}_{X^{(m+1)}/S}(-)}V\\
\begin{pmatrix}\text{ the groupoid of $m$-PD stratified $G$-bundles }\\
\text{on $X/S$ with vanishing $p$-$m$-curvature}\end{pmatrix}@>{\sim}>{(\ref{E3erf002})\ \text{for}\ m}>\begin{pmatrix}\text{the groupoid of }\\
\text{$G$-bundles on $X^{(m+1)}$}\end{pmatrix}.\end{CD}

Here, recall the notion of an
F
-divided
G
-bundle (cf.
[
12
, § 2.3, Definition 9]
) to construct an equivalence of categories for
\infty
-PD stratified
G
-bundles.

Definition 7.7.4
.

An
F
-divided
G
-bundle
on
X/S
is a collection of data

(477)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\infty}:=\{(\mathcal{G}_{l},\varpi_{l})\}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}},

where each
\mathcal{G}_{l}
denotes a
G
-bundle on
X^{(l)}
and each
\varpi_{l}
denotes an isomorphism of
G
-bundles
\mathcal{G}_{l}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}F^{*}_{X^{(l)}/S}(\mathcal{G}_{l+1})
.
The notion of an isomorphism between
F
-divided
G
-bundles can be defined in a natural manner.

Corollary 7.7.5
.

(Recall that
G
is assumed to be affine.)
There exists an equivalence of categories

(478)

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\text{the groupoid of }\\
\text{$\infty$-PD stratified $G$-bundles on $X/S$}\end{pmatrix}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\begin{pmatrix}\text{the groupoid of}\\
\text{$F$-divided $G$-bundles on $X/S$}\\
\end{pmatrix}.

Proof.

The assertion follows from Proposition
7.7.3
, (i), (ii), and the final comment in Remark
7.5.2
.
∎

7.8.
Flat connections and
0
-PD stratifications

We shall focus on the case of
m=0
.
It is well-known that flat connections on an
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module correspond to
0
-stratifications on it.
In what follows, we generalize this correspondence to those on
G
-bundles.

Let
\pi:\mathcal{E}\rightarrow X
be a
G
-bundle on
X
and
\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}:=\{\varepsilon_{l}\}_{l}
a
0
-PD stratification on
\mathcal{E}
.
According to the discussion following Definition
7.5.1
,
the data
\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}
corresponds to a collection
\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge\natural}:=\{\varepsilon_{l}^{\natural}\}_{l}
consisting of
\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(0)}^{l}
-algebra isomorphisms
\varepsilon_{l}^{\natural}:\mathcal{P}^{l}_{X/S,(0)}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{X/S,(0)}^{l}
(
l\geq 0
) as in (
457
).
This collection
specifies
a flat
S
-connection
\nabla:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\rightarrow\Omega_{X/S}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
on

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
(cf.
[
5
, § 4, Theorem 4.8]
).
The isomorphism
\varepsilon_{1}^{\sharp}
preserves the multiplications, so
\nabla
satisfies the equality
\nabla(a\cdot b)=\nabla(a)\cdot b+\nabla(b)\cdot a
for any local sections
a
,
b\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
.
Hence, for any local section
\partial\in\mathcal{T}_{X/S}
, the induced
f^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{S})
-linear endomorphism
\nabla_{\partial}:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}

defines a local section of
\pi_{*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}/S})
.
By the commutativity of (
7.5
) for
\varepsilon_{l}^{\natural}
, we see that
\nabla_{\partial}
lies in
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}/S}\left(=\pi_{*}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{E}/S})^{G}\right)
.
Since the restriction of
\nabla
to
\mathcal{O}_{X}\left(\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\right)
coincides with the universal derivation
d:\mathcal{O}_{X}\rightarrow\Omega_{X/S}
, the equality
d_{\mathcal{E}}(\nabla_{\partial})=\partial
holds.
The morphism

(479)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}}:\mathcal{T}_{X/S}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{\mathcal{E}/S}

given by assigning
\partial\mapsto\nabla_{\partial}
defines an
S
-connection on
\mathcal{E}
, which is flat because of
the flatness of
\nabla
.
If
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime\blacklozenge})
is another
0
-PD stratified
G
-bundle, then each isomorphism
(\mathcal{E},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime\blacklozenge})
is, by construction, transformed into an isomorphism of flat
G
-bundles
(\mathcal{E},\nabla_{\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}(\mathcal{E},\nabla_{\varepsilon^{\prime\blacklozenge}})
.

Moreover, we may reverse the steps in this construction to obtain a
0
-PD stratification from a flat
S
-connection.
The observation discussed here shows the following assertion.

Proposition 7.8.1
.

(Recall that
G
is assumed to be affine.)
The assignment
(\mathcal{E},\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge})\mapsto(\mathcal{E},\nabla_{\varepsilon^{\blacklozenge}})
constructed above
defines
an equivalence of categories

(480)

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\text{ the groupoid of }\\
\text{$0$-PD stratified $G$-bundles on $X/S$}\end{pmatrix}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\begin{pmatrix}\text{ the groupoid of }\\
\text{flat $G$-bundles on $X/S$}\\
\end{pmatrix},

which is restricted to an equivalence of categories

(481)

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\text{ the groupoid of quasi-nilpotent}\\
\text{$0$-PD stratified $G$-bundles on $X/S$}\end{pmatrix}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\begin{pmatrix}\text{the groupoid of quasi-nilpotent}\\
\text{flat $G$-bundles on $X/S$}\\
\end{pmatrix}.

If, moreover, the base ring
R
is defined over
\mathbb{F}_{p}
, then
(
481
)
is restricted to an equivalence of categories

(482)

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\text{ the groupoid of $0$-PD stratified}\\
\text{$G$-bundles on $X/S$ with vanishing $p$-$0$-curvature}\end{pmatrix}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\begin{pmatrix}\text{ the groupoid of }\\
\text{$p$-flat $G$-bundles on $X/S$}\\
\end{pmatrix}.

Proof.

The remaining portions follow from the comments in Remark
7.7.2
and
[
33
, § 3, Remark 3.1.2]
.
∎

Corollary 7.8.2
.

(Recall that
G
is assumed to be affine.)
There exists a canonical equivalence of categories

(483)

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\text{the groupoid of }\\
\text{$G$-bundles on $X^{(1)}$}\end{pmatrix}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\begin{pmatrix}\text{ the groupoid of }\\
\text{$p$-flat $G$-bundles on $X/S$}\\
\end{pmatrix}

such that, if
\mathcal{G}
is a
G
-bundle on
X^{(1)}
, then the underlying
G
-bundle of the corresponding
p
-flat
G
-bundle coincides with
F_{X/S}^{*}(\mathcal{G})
.

Proof.

The assertion follows from the equivalences of categories (
475
) and (
482
).
∎

For each
G
-bundle
\mathcal{G}
on
X^{(1)}
,
we shall denote by

(484)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathcal{G}}^{\mathrm{can}}

the
p
-flat connection on the pull-back
F_{X/S}^{*}(\mathcal{G})
corresponding to
\mathcal{G}
via (
483
).

Also, with the notation in Proposition
7.6.1
,
let
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
be a quasi-nilpotent flat
G
-bundle on
X/S
.
Then, by composing the equivalences of categories (
469
) and (
481
),
we obtain a
0
-crystal of
G
-bundles

(485)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{E},\nabla)^{\diamondsuit}

on
\overline{X}/S
corresponding to
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
.

Finally, we shall conclude the present paper with the following assertion, which says
that the category of quasi-nilpotent flat
G
-bundles on an infinitesimal thickening of a smooth scheme
does not depend on the choice of the thickening.

Proposition 7.8.3
.

Let us keep the assumption in Proposition
7.6.1
.
Also, let
X^{\prime}
be another smooth scheme over
S

such that
\gamma
extends to
X^{\prime}
and
whose reduction modulo
J_{0}
is isomorphic to
\overline{X}
.
Then, for each quasi-nilpotent flat
G
-bundle
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
on
X/S
,
there exists a unique (up to isomorphism) quasi-nilpotent flat
G
-bundle

(486)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime})

on
X^{\prime}/S

with
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)^{\diamondsuit}\cong(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime})^{\diamondsuit}
.
Moreover, the assignment
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)\mapsto(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime})
determines an equivalence of categories between the groupoids of quasi-nilpotent flat
G
-bundles on
X/S
and
X^{\prime}/S
.

Proof.

The assertion follows from Propositions
7.6.1
,
7.6.2
, and
7.8.1
.
∎

Remark 7.8.4
.

Let us describe a down-to-earth construction of the assignment
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)\mapsto(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime})
asserted in the above proposition.
Since

J_{0}
is nilpotent and both
X
,
X^{\prime}
are smooth over
S
,
there exists a collection of data

(487)

\displaystyle(\{U_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in I},\{\eta_{\alpha\beta}\}_{(\alpha,\beta)\in I_{2}})

where

•

I
denotes a finite set and

\{U_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in I}
is an open covering of
X
;

•

I_{2}
denotes the subset of
I\times I
consisting of pairs
(\alpha,\beta)
with
U_{\alpha\beta}:=U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta}\neq\emptyset
and
each
\eta_{\alpha\beta}
(
(\alpha,\beta)\in I_{2}
) is an automorphism of
U_{\alpha\beta}
inducing the identity morphism of
U_{\alpha\beta}\times_{S}\overline{S}
via reduction modulo
J_{0}
,

such that
X^{\prime}
may be obtained by gluing together
U_{\alpha\beta}
’s by means of

\eta_{\alpha\beta}
’s.
Now, let
\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}
be the
0
-crystal of
G
-bundles corresponding to
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)
via (
469
) (for
m=0
) and (
480
).
This crystal associate to each
\eta_{\alpha\beta}

a
G
-equivariant isomorphism
\eta_{\mathcal{E},\alpha\beta}
of
\mathcal{E}|_{U_{\alpha\beta}}
over
\eta_{\alpha\beta}
.
(According to the discussion in Remark
7.6.3
,
\eta_{\mathcal{E},\alpha\beta}
also arises from pulling-back the isomorphism
\varepsilon_{l}
for a sufficiently large
l
constituting the
0
-PD stratification corresponding to
\mathcal{E}^{\diamondsuit}
by the morphism
U_{\alpha\beta}\rightarrow P_{X/S,(0)}^{l}
induced from
(\iota,\iota\circ\eta_{\alpha\beta}):U_{\alpha\beta}\rightarrow X\times_{S}X
, where
\iota
denotes the natural inclusion
U_{\alpha\beta}\hookrightarrow X
.)
Then, by the definition of the assignment
(\mathcal{E},\nabla)\mapsto(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime})
,
we see that the resulting flat
G
-bundle
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime})
may be obtained by gluing together

(\mathcal{E}|_{U_{\alpha}},\nabla|_{U_{\alpha}})
’s by means of
\eta_{\mathcal{E},\alpha\beta}
’s.
